Play 10 · Send, reply and conversion

Cold Calling on Five Lists

Trigger Five lists pooled into one calling queue
Outcome Meetings booked on the call
Channels call, email

Give this to your agent 
Install the Pipeline.tech "Cold Calling on Five Lists" outbound play. Fetch
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/core/SKILL.md and
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/plays/cold-calling-five-lists/SKILL.md,
save them as ~/.claude/skills/pipeline-outbound-core/SKILL.md and
~/.claude/skills/pipeline-cold-calling-five-lists/SKILL.md, then run /pipeline-cold-calling-five-lists.
Paste into Claude Code. It installs the shared core rules plus this play, then runs it as /pipeline-cold-calling-five-lists.
Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

Calling works when three things are true at once: the list is warm or at least qualified, the script is fixed, and the dialer runs lines in parallel so the caller spends the hour talking instead of listening to ringtones. Most teams get one of the three right and write the channel off. This play pools five lists into one queue, tags every row with its source, and books the meeting while the person is still on the line.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. Stack answers from core section 1, plus the dialer, caller, calling hours and time zone.
  2. The ICP in one paragraph and the Full-TAM Map export, or the sheet that stands in for it.
  3. The offer in one sentence, one proof point and the booking calendar.
  4. Which of the five sources exist today. A missing source is skipped, never faked.

Steps

1Define the five lists and their sources

ToolsAttioHubSpot (option)BeehiivEmailBisonRB2BGoogle Sheets
  • List 1, cold ICP: Full-TAM Map accounts that passed the written ICP. Apply core: enrichment for the decision maker by size band.
  • List 2, subscribers and lead-magnet downloads: two clicks in 30 days or a signup in the last 7 days, matched to a company by domain. Opens never make the list.
  • List 3, unbooked replies: the handoff from Reply Routing. Interested, question and cost replies that never became a booked time. The number found during routing travels with the reply.
  • List 4, website visitors: identified visitors, plus repeat visits to pricing or demo inside 14 days.
  • List 5, missed deals: no-shows, deals stalled 30 days, closed-lost inside the revival window (6 months by default).

2Pool into one queue and enrich the numbers

ToolsGoogle SheetsBlitzAPILeadMagicProspeoMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • Merge the five lists into one queue. Every row keeps its source tag and the date it qualified. Dedupe by person; on two lists, keep the warmer tag (list 3 beats list 1) and note both.
  • Apply core: exclusions. Also remove anyone on a do-not-call list or who asked not to be called.
  • Numbers go through a waterfall, each vendor seeing only what the one before it missed. BlitzAPI first for owner-operators. LeadMagic then Prospeo for business mobiles and direct dials. Normalise every number to one format with the country code and record its time zone.
  • Any email used alongside the call: apply core: verification. A number with no valid email is still called.

3Tier and set the cadence

ToolsClaudeAttioHubSpot (option)
  • Apply core: tiering, with the source doing most of the work. Tier 1 is lists 3, 4 and 5 plus anyone met in person: called the same day they land, then every 2 to 3 days for two weeks.
  • Tier 2 is list 2 plus any list 1 account with a dated signal: one attempt a week for four weeks.
  • Tier 3 is the rest of list 1: parallel blocks by time zone, two attempts per quarter, then email only.
  • Claude writes a one-line context note per Tier 1 row from the reply thread or visit history, so the caller knows the hook before the line connects.

4Lock the script

ToolsClaudeGoogle Sheets
  • One script per list, approved by the owner and memorized by the caller. No ad-libbed positioning. Sentences may move, claims may not.
  • The hook lands inside the first twenty seconds and names the source: "you downloaded X", "you replied to Y about Z", "you were on our pricing page on Tuesday". Cold ICP opens with the segment's pain, never a compliment.
  • Then one question, and the caller listens.
  • Close by confirming the email on file and booking live with two specific times inside 2 business days. Never "I'll send a link". The invite goes out on the line.
  • A voicemail script under 15 seconds: name, company, the source hook, a call-back number. No pitch.

5Run the dialer in parallel

ToolsReadyModeNooks (option)Salesfinity (option)Dialer (option)Phone
  • Load the queue with the source tag and context note. Dial 3 to 5 lines in parallel, voicemail auto-skip on, local presence on where it is lawful.
  • Volume follows list quality: Tier 1 on 2 lines, 100 to 300 dials a day; Tier 3 cold blocks at 1K to 2K dials a day per caller.
  • A 90-minute block per time zone, late morning and mid afternoon local time, inside the calling hours and consent rules of the recipient's country and state. A small team runs the same blocks from a plain dialer or the phone.

6Log and route every call

ToolsAttioHubSpot (option)CalendlySlackEmailBison
  • Apply core: CRM and alerts. Every call writes the recording link, disposition, source tag, duration and next step date. A booked call opens a deal; no-shows stay deals in a lost stage.
  • Booked meetings enter the pre-call reminder cadence from Reply Routing: confirmation now, reminders at 24 hours, 4 hours, 1 hour and 10 minutes. Nothing booked past 2 days.
  • A callback is a dated task at the time the prospect gave, never a note. A wrong number goes back to step 2 for a second pass; still none, the row stays on the list with a flag.
  • "Do not call" removes the number from the dialer and the CRM the same day.

7Read the week by list

ToolsGoogle SheetsClaudeSlack
  • Weekly, per list: dials, connects, conversations over 2 minutes, meetings booked, meetings held, show rate. Connect rate by list and by hour in the prospect's zone.
  • Move dial time toward the lists that convert. If list 3 books every 15 connects and list 1 every 80, list 3 is called first each morning.
  • Two recordings per caller per week are checked against the script; drift is fixed before the next block.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • Calling hours, consent and do-not-call rules of the recipient's country and state come first.
  • The script is the script. A caller who cannot answer a question says so and books the call.
  • Never "I'll send a link". Two times, booked live, invite sent before the call ends.
  • Opens are not a signal. List 2 is clicks and signups only.
  • A number found through Reply Routing is for the warm call about that reply. It never moves to a cold block.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-cold-calling-five-lists for our payroll offer to 20 to 200 person companies, one caller, Eastern hours, we have the newsletter and visitor identification"

  1. Confirmed the CRM, newsletter tool, visitor tool, reply routing and dialer. Loaded the ICP and offer.
  2. Week one: 1,840 cold ICP, 212 subscribers, 38 unbooked replies, 61 visitors, 27 missed deals. After dedupe and exclusions 2,104 rows, 126 in Tier 1.
  3. Numbers found for 1,537 rows (73 percent), 1,120 of them mobiles. 88 wrong numbers flagged back after the first blocks.
  4. Five scripts approved, caller drilled for two days, voicemail under 15 seconds.
  5. 4,210 dials across four blocks a day. 389 connects, 141 conversations over 2 minutes, 23 meetings booked on the call, 19 held.
  6. By list: replies 9 meetings from 41 connects, missed deals 5 from 29, visitors 4 from 38, subscribers 3 from 67, cold 2 from 214. Cold blocks moved to the last hour; Tier 1 dialed first each morning.

outcome: Meetings booked on the call

channels: call, email
tools: Attio, HubSpot, Beehiiv, EmailBison, RB2B, BlitzAPI, LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan, Claude, ReadyMode, Nooks, Salesfinity, Dialer, Phone, Slack, Calendly, Google Sheets

# Cold Calling on Five Lists

Calling works when three things are true at once: the list is warm or at least qualified, the script is fixed, and the dialer runs lines in parallel so the caller spends the hour talking instead of listening to ringtones. Most teams get one of the three right and write the channel off. This play pools five lists into one queue, tags every row with its source, and books the meeting while the person is still on the line.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. Stack answers from core section 1, plus the dialer, caller, calling hours and time zone.
2. The ICP in one paragraph and the Full-TAM Map export, or the sheet that stands in for it.
3. The offer in one sentence, one proof point and the booking calendar.
4. Which of the five sources exist today. A missing source is skipped, never faked.

## Steps

### 1. Define the five lists and their sources
Tools: Attio, HubSpot (option), Beehiiv, EmailBison, RB2B, Google Sheets
Flow:
- Cold ICP accounts | Full-TAM Map, owner or function head by size band | with: Attio, Google Sheets | tag: list 1
- Subscribers | Repeat clickers and new signups matched to a company, never opens | with: Beehiiv | tag: list 2
- Unbooked replies and visitors | Positive, neutral, question and cost replies that never booked, plus identified visitors to pricing or demo pages | with: EmailBison, RB2B | tag: lists 3 and 4
= Missed deals | No-shows, stalled and closed-lost inside the revival window | with: Attio, HubSpot | tag: list 5

- List 1, cold ICP: Full-TAM Map accounts that passed the written ICP. Apply core: enrichment for the decision maker by size band.
- List 2, subscribers and lead-magnet downloads: two clicks in 30 days or a signup in the last 7 days, matched to a company by domain. Opens never make the list.
- List 3, unbooked replies: the handoff from Reply Routing. Interested, question and cost replies that never became a booked time. The number found during routing travels with the reply.
- List 4, website visitors: identified visitors, plus repeat visits to pricing or demo inside 14 days.
- List 5, missed deals: no-shows, deals stalled 30 days, closed-lost inside the revival window (6 months by default).

### 2. Pool into one queue and enrich the numbers
Tools: Google Sheets, BlitzAPI, LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
= One queue with a source tag | Dedupe by person, exclusions, then direct dials and mobiles through the waterfall | with: Google Sheets, BlitzAPI, LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan | chips: source, time zone, number type, email status

- Merge the five lists into one queue. Every row keeps its `source` tag and the date it qualified. Dedupe by person; on two lists, keep the warmer tag (list 3 beats list 1) and note both.
- Apply core: exclusions. Also remove anyone on a do-not-call list or who asked not to be called.
- Numbers go through a waterfall, each vendor seeing only what the one before it missed. BlitzAPI first for owner-operators. LeadMagic then Prospeo for business mobiles and direct dials. Normalise every number to one format with the country code and record its time zone.
- Any email used alongside the call: apply core: verification. A number with no valid email is still called.

### 3. Tier and set the cadence
Tools: Claude, Attio, HubSpot (option)
Flow:
- Tier 1 | Replies, visitors, met in person, missed deals. Same day, then every 2 to 3 days for two weeks | with: Attio | tag: same day
- Tier 2 | Subscribers and ICP fit with a signal. Weekly | with: Attio | tag: weekly
- Tier 3 | Cold ICP in parallel-dial blocks | with: Attio | tag: blocks

- Apply core: tiering, with the source doing most of the work. Tier 1 is lists 3, 4 and 5 plus anyone met in person: called the same day they land, then every 2 to 3 days for two weeks.
- Tier 2 is list 2 plus any list 1 account with a dated signal: one attempt a week for four weeks.
- Tier 3 is the rest of list 1: parallel blocks by time zone, two attempts per quarter, then email only.
- Claude writes a one-line context note per Tier 1 row from the reply thread or visit history, so the caller knows the hook before the line connects.

### 4. Lock the script
Tools: Claude, Google Sheets
Flow:
= Approved script, memorized | Hook in twenty seconds tied to the source, one question, confirm the email, book live with two times | with: Claude, Google Sheets | chips: you downloaded X, you replied to Y, you were on our pricing page | tag: no ad-libs

- One script per list, approved by the owner and memorized by the caller. No ad-libbed positioning. Sentences may move, claims may not.
- The hook lands inside the first twenty seconds and names the source: "you downloaded X", "you replied to Y about Z", "you were on our pricing page on Tuesday". Cold ICP opens with the segment's pain, never a compliment.
- Then one question, and the caller listens.
- Close by confirming the email on file and booking live with two specific times inside 2 business days. Never "I'll send a link". The invite goes out on the line.
- A voicemail script under 15 seconds: name, company, the source hook, a call-back number. No pitch.

### 5. Run the dialer in parallel
Tools: ReadyMode, Nooks (option), Salesfinity (option), Dialer (option), Phone
Flow:
= 3 to 5 lines in parallel | 500 to 2K dials a day per caller depending on list quality, blocks by time zone | with: ReadyMode, Nooks, Salesfinity | chips: local presence, voicemail auto-skip, calling hours, consent | tag: parallel dial

- Load the queue with the source tag and context note. Dial 3 to 5 lines in parallel, voicemail auto-skip on, local presence on where it is lawful.
- Volume follows list quality: Tier 1 on 2 lines, 100 to 300 dials a day; Tier 3 cold blocks at 1K to 2K dials a day per caller.
- A 90-minute block per time zone, late morning and mid afternoon local time, inside the calling hours and consent rules of the recipient's country and state. A small team runs the same blocks from a plain dialer or the phone.

### 6. Log and route every call
Tools: Attio, HubSpot (option), Calendly, Slack, EmailBison
Flow:
- Every call to the CRM | Recording, disposition, source, duration, next step date | with: Attio, HubSpot | chips: booked, callback, no answer, wrong number, not interested
- Booked meetings | Into the pre-call reminder cadence from Reply Routing | with: Calendly, EmailBison | tag: show rate
= Callbacks and wrong numbers | Callbacks dated as tasks, wrong numbers flagged back to enrichment | with: Attio, Slack

- Apply core: CRM and alerts. Every call writes the recording link, disposition, source tag, duration and next step date. A booked call opens a deal; no-shows stay deals in a lost stage.
- Booked meetings enter the pre-call reminder cadence from Reply Routing: confirmation now, reminders at 24 hours, 4 hours, 1 hour and 10 minutes. Nothing booked past 2 days.
- A callback is a dated task at the time the prospect gave, never a note. A wrong number goes back to step 2 for a second pass; still none, the row stays on the list with a flag.
- "Do not call" removes the number from the dialer and the CRM the same day.

### 7. Read the week by list
Tools: Google Sheets, Claude, Slack
Flow:
= Weekly read by list and by hour | Dials, connects, conversations, meetings, show rate | with: Google Sheets, Claude, Slack | chips: connect rate, conversation rate, meetings per 100 dials | tag: move dial time

- Weekly, per list: dials, connects, conversations over 2 minutes, meetings booked, meetings held, show rate. Connect rate by list and by hour in the prospect's zone.
- Move dial time toward the lists that convert. If list 3 books every 15 connects and list 1 every 80, list 3 is called first each morning.
- Two recordings per caller per week are checked against the script; drift is fixed before the next block.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- Calling hours, consent and do-not-call rules of the recipient's country and state come first.
- The script is the script. A caller who cannot answer a question says so and books the call.
- Never "I'll send a link". Two times, booked live, invite sent before the call ends.
- Opens are not a signal. List 2 is clicks and signups only.
- A number found through Reply Routing is for the warm call about that reply. It never moves to a cold block.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-cold-calling-five-lists for our payroll offer to 20 to 200 person companies, one caller, Eastern hours, we have the newsletter and visitor identification"

1. Confirmed the CRM, newsletter tool, visitor tool, reply routing and dialer. Loaded the ICP and offer.
2. Week one: 1,840 cold ICP, 212 subscribers, 38 unbooked replies, 61 visitors, 27 missed deals. After dedupe and exclusions 2,104 rows, 126 in Tier 1.
3. Numbers found for 1,537 rows (73 percent), 1,120 of them mobiles. 88 wrong numbers flagged back after the first blocks.
4. Five scripts approved, caller drilled for two days, voicemail under 15 seconds.
5. 4,210 dials across four blocks a day. 389 connects, 141 conversations over 2 minutes, 23 meetings booked on the call, 19 held.
6. By list: replies 9 meetings from 41 connects, missed deals 5 from 29, visitors 4 from 38, subscribers 3 from 67, cold 2 from 214. Cold blocks moved to the last hour; Tier 1 dialed first each morning.

A reply is the most expensive thing a campaign produces, and most teams answer it hours later with a bare booking link. After 20 minutes the chance of a meeting drops by half or more, so this play runs on one clock. Every reply gets a bucket, a value score and a next step, and the rep only touches the ones that need a human.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. Stack answers from core section 1, plus the Slack channel and its owner.
  2. The ICP in one paragraph, used by the value score.
  3. The offer in one sentence, one proof point and the booking link.
  4. Rep hours and time zone, and whether a newsletter exists.

Steps

1Collect every reply in one place

ToolsEmailBisonInstantly (option)HeyReach
  • Turn on the sender's reply webhook, or poll the replies endpoint every 2 minutes. LinkedIn replies come in the same way.
  • Attach the thread, campaign, step, lead record and receive time, which starts the 5 minute clock. Dedupe by thread.
  • Stop the sequence for that contact the moment any reply lands. Apply core: send rules.

2Classify intent and score value

ToolsClaude
  • One pass, two outputs. First the intent bucket, exactly one of six: interested, question or more info, objection or cost, wrong person or referral, not now or long-term, out of office or auto-reply.
  • Second a value score from 1 to 5 from ICP fit, company size and urgency in their words. Keep it separate from intent.
  • Return a one-line reason and a confidence. Below 0.7 the rep picks the bucket in Slack, and the choice is kept as a labelled example for step 7.
  • "Not interested", "unsubscribe" or "stop" is not a bucket and gets no reply. Mark do-not-contact in the sender and the CRM, log it, done.

3Alert on interested within 5 minutes

ToolsSlackLeadMagicProspeo
  • Interested and question replies post to Slack inside 5 minutes of receive time. The card carries the reply, profile, value score with reason, direct dial if found, and the draft from step 4.
  • Apply core: tiering. Score 4 or 5 is Tier 1: the owner calls the direct dial first, then sends the written answer. Score 3 adds a LinkedIn connection. Score 1 or 2 gets the written answer only.
  • Measure receive time to first human action. The median is the play's KPI.

4Draft the answer, rep sends

ToolsClaudeEmailBisonHeyReach
  • Every draft: under 100 words, in the thread, from the inbox that got the reply, no greeting line, no re-pitch, no repeat of a hook from the cold email. The rep edits and sends.
  • Interested: thank them in one line, share the proof point, propose two or three specific afternoon slots in the next 2 to 4 business days, computed from today. The booking link rides along with the times, never alone.
  • More info: a short neutral breakdown, the proof point, a soft time proposal. Agreed to a call then went quiet: place a slot and say it is on the calendar.
  • Cost: the pricing model in two sentences, it depends on scope, ask for 7 to 9 minutes. The odd number reads as specific and low commitment.
  • Objection: acknowledge in one line without defending, then a lower-commitment ask (a 15 minute walk-through).

5Route everything else

ToolsLeadMagicProspeoMillionVerifierBounceBanEmailBisonBeehiiv (optional)
  • Wrong person with a name: reply in the thread, CC that person, reference the conversation, restate the offer in one line. Without a name: ask for the right title, then find the contact. Apply core: enrichment. Apply core: verification. The new lead enters a fresh sequence with source: referral.
  • Not now: one line accepting the timing, a dated recall in the CRM (their date, default 6 months), a newsletter add if one exists.
  • Out of office: no reply. Re-queue the same step 7 to 14 days out, or the day after a stated return date.

6Log to the CRM

ToolsAttioHubSpot (option)
  • Apply core: CRM and alerts. Every reply writes bucket, score, reason, action, owner, minutes to first action and the thread link. Recalls and re-queues are dated tasks, not notes.
  • Interested with a proposed time opens a deal. No-shows and disqualified calls stay deals, in a lost stage, so the funnel counts every booked call.

7Supervise for 7 days before any auto-send

ToolsSlackAttio
  • For the first 7 days the rep approves or edits every draft in every bucket. Record approved, edited or wrong bucket, then compute agreement per bucket at day 7.
  • Auto-sending is off by default. The user may turn it on per bucket at 95 percent agreement with at least 20 examples, and only for out of office re-queues, not-now acknowledgements and named-referral CCs. Interested, more info, cost and objection are always sent by a human.

8Pre-call reminders and report

ToolsCalendlyEmailBisonHeyReachPhone
  • Never book more than 2 days out. On booking: confirmation email with a 60 second video. Reminders at 24 hours, 4 hours, 1 hour, and 10 minutes before with a reschedule option. Tier 1 also gets a LinkedIn connection and a call a few hours before.
  • Weekly: replies, bucket mix, median minutes to first action, meetings, show rate, agreement per bucket.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • The 5 minute clock runs in rep hours only; outside them the action is due 5 minutes into the next working hour. Never auto-send to beat it.
  • Never a bare booking link. Times first, link second, same message.
  • A direct dial found for a reply is for the warm call about that reply. It never moves into a cold calling block; cold dialing has its own play and its own consent rules.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-reply-routing for our outsourced SDR offer, Slack channel #replies, I work 9 to 5 Eastern"

  1. Confirmed the webhook, LinkedIn tool, CRM and channel. Loaded the ICP and proof point.
  2. Week one: 148 replies. 41 interested or more info, 12 cost, 9 objection, 17 wrong person, 23 not now, 46 out of office. 6 bucketed by hand.
  3. 53 Slack cards, median 2 minutes 40 seconds to card. 11 Tier 1, 7 with a direct dial, 4 calls connected.
  4. 53 drafts sent after rep edits, 19 meetings booked inside 2 days, 15 showed.
  5. 17 wrong-person replies became 13 CCs and 4 new verified leads. 23 recalls dated, 46 re-queued 10 days out.
  6. Day 7 agreement: out of office 100 percent, not now 96 percent, referral CC 88 percent. Auto-send on for the first two only.

# Reply Routing

A reply is the most expensive thing a campaign produces, and most teams answer it hours later with a bare booking link. After 20 minutes the chance of a meeting drops by half or more, so this play runs on one clock. Every reply gets a bucket, a value score and a next step, and the rep only touches the ones that need a human.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. Stack answers from core section 1, plus the Slack channel and its owner.
2. The ICP in one paragraph, used by the value score.
3. The offer in one sentence, one proof point and the booking link.
4. Rep hours and time zone, and whether a newsletter exists.

## Steps

### 1. Collect every reply in one place
Tools: EmailBison, Instantly (option), HeyReach
Flow:
- Email replies | Webhook or 2-minute polling; the sequence stops for that contact on any reply | with: EmailBison, Instantly
- LinkedIn replies | Same pipe, same clock | with: HeyReach
= One queue, deduped by thread | Receive time starts the 5-minute clock | chips: thread, campaign, step, lead record, received at

- Turn on the sender's reply webhook, or poll the replies endpoint every 2 minutes. LinkedIn replies come in the same way.
- Attach the thread, campaign, step, lead record and receive time, which starts the 5 minute clock. Dedupe by thread.
- Stop the sequence for that contact the moment any reply lands. Apply core: send rules.

### 2. Classify intent and score value
Tools: Claude
Flow:
= Six buckets plus a separate value score | One LLM pass; value from ICP fit, company size and urgency | with: Claude | chips: interested, question, objection or cost, wrong person, not now, out of office | tag: one pass

- One pass, two outputs. First the intent bucket, exactly one of six: interested, question or more info, objection or cost, wrong person or referral, not now or long-term, out of office or auto-reply.
- Second a value score from 1 to 5 from ICP fit, company size and urgency in their words. Keep it separate from intent.
- Return a one-line reason and a confidence. Below 0.7 the rep picks the bucket in Slack, and the choice is kept as a labelled example for step 7.
- "Not interested", "unsubscribe" or "stop" is not a bucket and gets no reply. Mark do-not-contact in the sender and the CRM, log it, done.

### 3. Alert on interested within 5 minutes
Tools: Slack, LeadMagic, Prospeo
Flow:
= Slack card within 5 minutes | The reply, the profile, the direct dial if found, and a drafted answer | with: Slack, LeadMagic, Prospeo | chips: Tier 1 warm call, Tier 2 email, Tier 3 template | tag: speed to lead

- Interested and question replies post to Slack inside 5 minutes of receive time. The card carries the reply, profile, value score with reason, direct dial if found, and the draft from step 4.
- Apply core: tiering. Score 4 or 5 is Tier 1: the owner calls the direct dial first, then sends the written answer. Score 3 adds a LinkedIn connection. Score 1 or 2 gets the written answer only.
- Measure receive time to first human action. The median is the play's KPI.

### 4. Draft the answer, rep sends
Tools: Claude, EmailBison, HeyReach
Flow:
= Rep sends, two or three specific times | Never a bare booking link; cost replies get the 7 to 9 minute frame | with: Claude, EmailBison, HeyReach | chips: interested, more info, cost, book a call

- Every draft: under 100 words, in the thread, from the inbox that got the reply, no greeting line, no re-pitch, no repeat of a hook from the cold email. The rep edits and sends.
- Interested: thank them in one line, share the proof point, propose two or three specific afternoon slots in the next 2 to 4 business days, computed from today. The booking link rides along with the times, never alone.
- More info: a short neutral breakdown, the proof point, a soft time proposal. Agreed to a call then went quiet: place a slot and say it is on the calendar.
- Cost: the pricing model in two sentences, it depends on scope, ask for 7 to 9 minutes. The odd number reads as specific and low commitment.
- Objection: acknowledge in one line without defending, then a lower-commitment ask (a 15 minute walk-through).

### 5. Route everything else
Tools: LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan, EmailBison, Beehiiv (optional)
Flow:
- Wrong person | CC the right one, or find and verify a new lead | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
- Not now | A dated recall, 90 days when no date is given; long-term to the newsletter | with: Beehiiv
- Out of office | Re-queued 7 to 14 days out | with: EmailBison

- Wrong person with a name: reply in the thread, CC that person, reference the conversation, restate the offer in one line. Without a name: ask for the right title, then find the contact. Apply core: enrichment. Apply core: verification. The new lead enters a fresh sequence with `source: referral`.
- Not now: one line accepting the timing, a dated recall in the CRM (their date, default 6 months), a newsletter add if one exists.
- Out of office: no reply. Re-queue the same step 7 to 14 days out, or the day after a stated return date.

### 6. Log to the CRM
Tools: Attio, HubSpot (option)
Flow:
= Every reply on the CRM record | with: Attio, HubSpot | chips: bucket, value score, owner, next step date

- Apply core: CRM and alerts. Every reply writes bucket, score, reason, action, owner, minutes to first action and the thread link. Recalls and re-queues are dated tasks, not notes.
- Interested with a proposed time opens a deal. No-shows and disqualified calls stay deals, in a lost stage, so the funnel counts every booked call.

### 7. Supervise for 7 days before any auto-send
Tools: Slack, Attio
Flow:
= 7-day supervised window | Auto-send stays off until a bucket agrees with the rep 95% of the time, then only for that bucket | with: Slack, Attio | tag: auto-send off

- For the first 7 days the rep approves or edits every draft in every bucket. Record approved, edited or wrong bucket, then compute agreement per bucket at day 7.
- Auto-sending is off by default. The user may turn it on per bucket at 95 percent agreement with at least 20 examples, and only for out of office re-queues, not-now acknowledgements and named-referral CCs. Interested, more info, cost and objection are always sent by a human.

### 8. Pre-call reminders and report
Tools: Calendly, EmailBison, HeyReach, Phone
Flow:
= Confirmation, reminders, warm call | with: Calendly, EmailBison, HeyReach, Phone | chips: now, 24 h, 4 h, 1 h, 10 min, no bookings past 2 days

- Never book more than 2 days out. On booking: confirmation email with a 60 second video. Reminders at 24 hours, 4 hours, 1 hour, and 10 minutes before with a reschedule option. Tier 1 also gets a LinkedIn connection and a call a few hours before.
- Weekly: replies, bucket mix, median minutes to first action, meetings, show rate, agreement per bucket.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- The 5 minute clock runs in rep hours only; outside them the action is due 5 minutes into the next working hour. Never auto-send to beat it.
- Never a bare booking link. Times first, link second, same message.
- A direct dial found for a reply is for the warm call about that reply. It never moves into a cold calling block; cold dialing has its own play and its own consent rules.

Play 12 · Send, reply and conversion

ABM at Scale

Trigger A funded or tiered account list (lookalike pull, database export, or the user's own targets)
Outcome One custom asset per contact, at hundreds of accounts
Channels email, linkedin

Give this to your agent 
Install the Pipeline.tech "ABM at Scale" outbound play. Fetch
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/core/SKILL.md and
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/plays/abm-at-scale/SKILL.md,
save them as ~/.claude/skills/pipeline-outbound-core/SKILL.md and
~/.claude/skills/pipeline-abm-at-scale/SKILL.md, then run /pipeline-abm-at-scale.
Paste into Claude Code. It installs the shared core rules plus this play, then runs it as /pipeline-abm-at-scale.
Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

ABM used to stop at 20 accounts because research, deliverables and contact maps were done by hand. This play runs the same steps over a few hundred accounts: classify, enrich, bucket by hiring signal, map 3 to 5 people, write a short strategy doc for each one. Every contact gets their own asset, because three people in a 30-person company compare what they received. One run took 413 raw accounts to about 900 unique deliverables.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The account list, or pull criteria to build one. Default pull: Series C to F, 11 to 50 employees, US.
  2. The ICP in one paragraph and the problem you solve.
  3. The offer in one sentence and one proof point. Both go into the doc and the copy.
  4. Strategy doc only (default) or doc plus a video brief the user records from.
  5. Stack answers from core section 1.

Steps

1Build the account list

ToolsOcean.ioApollo (option)CSV (option)
  • Pull funded companies in the user's band, or load the user's list. A funded pull is noisy.
  • Apply core: lists.
  • Apply core: exclusions. Dedupe against the CRM now so warm accounts are never double-touched.

2Classify B2B fit as KEEP / DROP / REVIEW

ToolsFirecrawlClaude
  • Scrape homepage and about page. Give the model the ICP paragraph and the page text, and ask for one of three verdicts with a one-line reason.
  • Three verdicts, not two. A hard yes/no filter throws out borderline accounts that turn out to be in market, which is lost TAM. REVIEW rows get a 30-second human look.
  • Read DROP reasons in bulk. One reason repeating means the ICP definition is wrong, not the list.
  • Only KEEP and approved REVIEW rows continue. A few hundred accounts classified this way costs under a dollar.

3Enrich each account

ToolsFirecrawlExaSerper
  • Pull homepage, about, services and one or two case study pages. From that text write five columns: product or service, business model, top 3 ICP segments as size plus geography plus industry, GTM motion, whether case studies exist.
  • Map the sales team with one Exa search filtered to LinkedIn profiles and count titles into AE, SDR or BDR, manager, director, VP, C-suite. A BDR with no visible cold email is intent in itself.

4Score six signals and assign one hiring bucket

ToolsSerperExaCareers pages
  • Score six weighted signals per account: funded in the last 12 months (15), new product or market expansion (10), sales-team gripes on review sites about leads or quotas (25), new growth-stage investor (10), hiring a salesperson in the last 15 days (25), salesperson hired in the last 90 days (15). Store hit, summary and URL for each.
  • The score is directional. Review sites and job boards gate access, so those signals under-fire. A low score means no confirmed signal, not no signal.
  • Put every account in exactly one bucket: currently hiring, recent hire under 90 days, or no signal. If both hiring signals fire, the account goes to currently hiring. No fourth bucket; every extra bucket is another variant of every asset.
  • Apply core: tiering. Two dated signals inside a quarter make the account Tier 1 and add the call layer.

5Map and tier contacts

ToolsExaProspeoLeadMagicMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • Find 3 to 5 people per account. Primary titles CRO and VP Sales; fallback founder plus head of sales when the company is too small for either.
  • Tier each person: decision maker (owns the budget), champion (runs the team or feels the pain daily), gatekeeper (chief of staff, executive assistant, ops lead). A gatekeeper gets an asset too, written for someone who forwards rather than buys.
  • Apply core: enrichment. Apply core: verification.

6Write one asset per contact

ToolsGoogle DocsClaudeTella (optional)
  • One short strategy doc per person, five pages at most: why now (from the bucket and signals), a segmentation matrix of their top 3 ICPs with a persona and buying trigger each, an infrastructure sketch sized to their sales headcount, a starter list of 30 target accounts in their best segment, three cold email variants, a closing CTA.
  • Write for the tier. The decision maker's version leads with pipeline math against headcount; the champion's with the list and copy; the gatekeeper's is a one-page summary built to be forwarded.
  • Never reuse a doc across contacts at the same account. Vary the opening, examples and account list, not just the name.
  • Optional: a 60 to 90 second video brief per contact (what we saw, what we built, where it is) for the user to record.
  • Label the CTA a strategy session or growth roadmap session, never an audit or consulting call.

7Send on email and LinkedIn

ToolsEmailBisonInstantly (option)HeyReach
  • Apply core: copy and send rules.
  • Three copy variants, one per bucket. Currently hiring: the doc is the pipeline the new rep inherits. Recent hire: the doc shortens the ramp. No signal: lead with the gap the enrichment found (a BDR with no cold email, no case studies, one ICP served when three are visible).
  • Email 1 names the gap and says a doc exists; email 2 is the threaded nudge with the link. LinkedIn in parallel: connect on day 1, send the doc on acceptance.
  • Companies past Series B already run outbound. Write to lower their cost per meeting, not to start outbound.
  • Stop every contact at an account the moment one replies; hand the thread to the owner.

8Log and read by bucket

ToolsAttio
  • Apply core: CRM and alerts. Write play name, bucket, contact tier, asset URL and date on each person.
  • Weekly: accounts kept, assets produced, sent, reply and interested rate by bucket and contact tier. If no-signal matches hiring on replies, the signal scan is under-firing and needs new sources, not new copy.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • Never mention review-site gripes or a named new hire in copy. Those signals pick the bucket, never the message.
  • One contact, one asset. If a doc cannot be made unique, drop the contact, not the uniqueness.
  • The doc is the CTA, not a gate. Send it in full, ask for nothing first.
  • Do not run this play on an account in another play's sequence this month.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-abm-at-scale on this Ocean.io export, we sell outsourced outbound to small B2B software companies"

  1. Confirmed offer line, proof point, doc-only assets, EmailBison plus HeyReach plus Attio.
  2. Loaded 413 accounts, 9 removed as customers or open deals. Fit check: 287 KEEP, 71 DROP, 46 REVIEW; 19 REVIEW rows approved by hand.
  3. 306 accounts enriched; 41 flagged as BDR with no cold email.
  4. Buckets: 81 currently hiring, 118 recent hire, 107 no signal. 22 accounts Tier 1 on two dated signals.
  5. 912 contacts found, 847 verified across two passes, 38 catch-all in their own campaign.
  6. 885 strategy docs written and linked on the CRM. Three bucket variants approved, campaigns scheduled for Monday.

# ABM at Scale

ABM used to stop at 20 accounts because research, deliverables and contact maps were done by hand. This play runs the same steps over a few hundred accounts: classify, enrich, bucket by hiring signal, map 3 to 5 people, write a short strategy doc for each one. Every contact gets their own asset, because three people in a 30-person company compare what they received. One run took 413 raw accounts to about 900 unique deliverables.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The account list, or pull criteria to build one. Default pull: Series C to F, 11 to 50 employees, US.
2. The ICP in one paragraph and the problem you solve.
3. The offer in one sentence and one proof point. Both go into the doc and the copy.
4. Strategy doc only (default) or doc plus a video brief the user records from.
5. Stack answers from core section 1.

## Steps

### 1. Build the account list
Tools: Ocean.io, Apollo (option), CSV (option)
Flow:
= A funded pull or your own list, deduped against the CRM | with: Ocean.io, Apollo, CSV | chips: Series C to F, 11 to 50 people, US

- Pull funded companies in the user's band, or load the user's list. A funded pull is noisy.
- Apply core: lists.
- Apply core: exclusions. Dedupe against the CRM now so warm accounts are never double-touched.

### 2. Classify B2B fit as KEEP / DROP / REVIEW
Tools: Firecrawl, Claude
Flow:
- KEEP | Continues | with: Claude | tag: verdict
- REVIEW | A 30-second human look | with: Claude | tag: verdict
- DROP | Reasons read in bulk; one reason repeating means the ICP is wrong | with: Firecrawl | tag: verdict

- Scrape homepage and about page. Give the model the ICP paragraph and the page text, and ask for one of three verdicts with a one-line reason.
- Three verdicts, not two. A hard yes/no filter throws out borderline accounts that turn out to be in market, which is lost TAM. REVIEW rows get a 30-second human look.
- Read DROP reasons in bulk. One reason repeating means the ICP definition is wrong, not the list.
- Only KEEP and approved REVIEW rows continue. A few hundred accounts classified this way costs under a dollar.

### 3. Enrich each account
Tools: Firecrawl, Exa, Serper
Flow:
= Five columns from the website and news | with: Firecrawl, Exa, Serper | chips: product, business model, top 3 ICPs, GTM motion, sales headcount, BDR with no cold email

- Pull homepage, about, services and one or two case study pages. From that text write five columns: product or service, business model, top 3 ICP segments as size plus geography plus industry, GTM motion, whether case studies exist.
- Map the sales team with one Exa search filtered to LinkedIn profiles and count titles into AE, SDR or BDR, manager, director, VP, C-suite. A BDR with no visible cold email is intent in itself.

### 4. Score six signals and assign one hiring bucket
Tools: Serper, Exa, Careers pages
Flow:
= Six weighted signals, one bucket per account | Tie goes to currently hiring; no fourth bucket | with: Serper, Exa, Careers pages | chips: currently hiring, recent hire under 90 days, no signal | tag: core: tiering

- Score six weighted signals per account: funded in the last 12 months (15), new product or market expansion (10), sales-team gripes on review sites about leads or quotas (25), new growth-stage investor (10), hiring a salesperson in the last 15 days (25), salesperson hired in the last 90 days (15). Store hit, summary and URL for each.
- The score is directional. Review sites and job boards gate access, so those signals under-fire. A low score means no confirmed signal, not no signal.
- Put every account in exactly one bucket: currently hiring, recent hire under 90 days, or no signal. If both hiring signals fire, the account goes to currently hiring. No fourth bucket; every extra bucket is another variant of every asset.
- Apply core: tiering. Two dated signals inside a quarter make the account Tier 1 and add the call layer.

### 5. Map and tier contacts
Tools: Exa, Prospeo, LeadMagic, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
= 3 to 5 people per account, tiered | with: Exa, Prospeo, LeadMagic, MillionVerifier, BounceBan | chips: decision maker, champion, gatekeeper

- Find 3 to 5 people per account. Primary titles CRO and VP Sales; fallback founder plus head of sales when the company is too small for either.
- Tier each person: decision maker (owns the budget), champion (runs the team or feels the pain daily), gatekeeper (chief of staff, executive assistant, ops lead). A gatekeeper gets an asset too, written for someone who forwards rather than buys.
- Apply core: enrichment. Apply core: verification.

### 6. Write one asset per contact
Tools: Google Docs, Claude, Tella (optional)
Flow:
= One strategy doc per contact, five pages at most | Written for the tier; never reused across contacts at one account | with: Google Docs, Claude, Tella | chips: why now, segmentation matrix, infrastructure sketch, 30 accounts, 3 emails, CTA

- One short strategy doc per person, five pages at most: why now (from the bucket and signals), a segmentation matrix of their top 3 ICPs with a persona and buying trigger each, an infrastructure sketch sized to their sales headcount, a starter list of 30 target accounts in their best segment, three cold email variants, a closing CTA.
- Write for the tier. The decision maker's version leads with pipeline math against headcount; the champion's with the list and copy; the gatekeeper's is a one-page summary built to be forwarded.
- Never reuse a doc across contacts at the same account. Vary the opening, examples and account list, not just the name.
- Optional: a 60 to 90 second video brief per contact (what we saw, what we built, where it is) for the user to record.
- Label the CTA a strategy session or growth roadmap session, never an audit or consulting call.

### 7. Send on email and LinkedIn
Tools: EmailBison, Instantly (option), HeyReach
Flow:
= Three copy variants, one per bucket | Email 1 names the gap, email 2 carries the doc; LinkedIn connects day 1 and sends the doc on accept | with: EmailBison, Instantly, HeyReach | tag: send

- Apply core: copy and send rules.
- Three copy variants, one per bucket. Currently hiring: the doc is the pipeline the new rep inherits. Recent hire: the doc shortens the ramp. No signal: lead with the gap the enrichment found (a BDR with no cold email, no case studies, one ICP served when three are visible).
- Email 1 names the gap and says a doc exists; email 2 is the threaded nudge with the link. LinkedIn in parallel: connect on day 1, send the doc on acceptance.
- Companies past Series B already run outbound. Write to lower their cost per meeting, not to start outbound.
- Stop every contact at an account the moment one replies; hand the thread to the owner.

### 8. Log and read by bucket
Tools: Attio
Flow:
= Read by bucket and contact tier | with: Attio | chips: play, bucket, tier, asset URL, date

- Apply core: CRM and alerts. Write play name, bucket, contact tier, asset URL and date on each person.
- Weekly: accounts kept, assets produced, sent, reply and interested rate by bucket and contact tier. If no-signal matches hiring on replies, the signal scan is under-firing and needs new sources, not new copy.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- Never mention review-site gripes or a named new hire in copy. Those signals pick the bucket, never the message.
- One contact, one asset. If a doc cannot be made unique, drop the contact, not the uniqueness.
- The doc is the CTA, not a gate. Send it in full, ask for nothing first.
- Do not run this play on an account in another play's sequence this month.

Play 13 · Send, reply and conversion

Film-It-For-You

Trigger A sub-vertical with two recognisable peer customers
Outcome A yes that asks you to make something for them
Channels email, linkedin

Give this to your agent 
Install the Pipeline.tech "Film-It-For-You" outbound play. Fetch
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/core/SKILL.md and
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/plays/film-it-for-you/SKILL.md,
save them as ~/.claude/skills/pipeline-outbound-core/SKILL.md and
~/.claude/skills/pipeline-film-it-for-you/SKILL.md, then run /pipeline-film-it-for-you.
Paste into Claude Code. It installs the shared core rules plus this play, then runs it as /pipeline-film-it-for-you.
Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

Most cold emails ask the prospect for something: a call, a reply, fifteen minutes. This play asks permission to make something for them, a two-minute video of their own product with your workflow inside it, and the yes comes easily because it costs them nothing. For a screen-recording software client, 7 of 7 real positives replied to that CTA, 5 of 7 on the first email and 3 of 7 to an opener that named two peer customers.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The ICP in one paragraph, including the team where the product lives (support, implementation, CS ops).
  2. The customer list, each customer's sub-vertical, and whether there is written permission to name them.
  3. The one-link workflow in one sentence, the way a customer says it on a call.
  4. Who records the videos, with which tool, and the booking link. That person owns the step 5 deadline.
  5. Stack answers from core section 1.

Steps

1Pick the lane

ToolsClaudeAttio
  • Group the customer list by sub-vertical, using the buyer's own label. "Technical B2B SaaS" and "online health clinic" are lanes; "software" is not. If the lookalike or pain-qualified-segments play has run, take the sub-verticals from there.
  • A lane qualifies when two or three customers in it read as peers to the prospect, each with written permission to be named. One name is a reference; two is a pattern their peers have already chosen.
  • Write one sentence per lane on the workflow as the peers run it. It becomes the middle of email 1.

2Build the list and enrich

ToolsOcean.ioApolloClaudeLeadMagicProspeoMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • Seed each lane with its named customers and expand to lookalikes. Apply core: lists. Qualify each account from its own website before any paid lookup.
  • Apply core: exclusions. A customer receiving a peer-proof email about its own peers is the worst reply this play can produce.
  • Find three to five people per account where the workflow lives: head of support, head of implementation, support operations, head of CS, or whatever the product touches. Use title keyword variations rather than seniority filters; the queue owner is often a senior manager, not a VP.
  • Apply core: enrichment. Apply core: verification.

3Write the copy

ToolsClaude
  • Apply core: copy and send rules. Body under 55 words, no link in email 1; the video link only travels in a reply.
  • Email 1 has three parts in a fixed order. Open with the peers: "{PEER_1} and {PEER_2} both use this for {WORKFLOW_SHORT}". One sentence on the workflow: the one link, what the customer does with it, what lands back. Then the ask, which never changes: "Can I film a 2-minute video for {COMPANY} showing how that would work?" Naming their company makes it something built for them, not a demo.
  • Email 2 is the threaded nudge: one detail email 1 left out (nothing to install, hours the peer team saves), then "Glad to film it for {COMPANY}, want one?"
  • One variant per lane; the CTA is identical everywhere so the result reads clean. Without written permission the opener becomes "two support teams in {SUB_VERTICAL}", which works less well. Show the user both emails and the subject block before upload.

4Launch one campaign per sub-vertical

ToolsEmailBisonInstantly (option)HeyReach
  • Apply core: send rules. One campaign per lane, never mixed: peer names only mean something inside their lane, and step 6 reads yes rate per lane.
  • Upload leads with {COMPANY}, {PEER_1}, {PEER_2}, {WORKFLOW} and {SUB_VERTICAL}. Step 2 goes three business days after step 1; LinkedIn connection on day one for heads of support and CS.
  • Stop the sequence for anyone who replies, books or opens as a deal.

5The yes is the trigger: film within one business day

ToolsTellaLoom (option)ClaudeCalendly
  • A yes, a "sure", or a question about how it works all start the same clock: the video goes out within one business day. The reply cost them nothing and is as easy to forget.
  • Record with their website or product in frame. Say their name and company in the first five seconds, show the one workflow from email 1 end to end, finish with one next step. Under two minutes thirty, no slides.
  • Reply in the same thread: one line, the link, two or three specific times. Never a bare booking link; they said yes to a video, not a calendar.
  • A question reply ("where is the recording stored?") gets the answer in the first line and the video anyway. A referral reply gets the video addressed to the new person, original recipient kept on the thread.

6Route and measure

ToolsSlackAttio
  • Apply core: CRM and alerts. Positives go to the reply-routing play; the Slack alert carries the thread, lane, recorder and deadline. Log each contact with lane, replying step, video sent date, and watched or not.
  • Weekly, per lane: sent, reply rate, "yes, film it" rate, videos sent on time, videos watched, meetings from videos. The video is the demo, so a strong yes rate with a weak watch rate is a video problem, not a copy problem. A weak yes rate means the peer names did not land: back to step 1.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • Never send a generic video. If it does not show their product and say their name, record again. Never promise a video and send a deck or a booking link.
  • Peer customers are named only with written permission, per name. Without it the opener is "two support teams in {SUB_VERTICAL}"; a logo on the website is not permission.
  • Keep the video under two minutes thirty. Say two minutes and mean it.
  • The video link never appears in email 1 or 2, only in a reply to a yes.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-film-it-for-you for our screen recording tool, buyers are support and implementation teams at B2B SaaS 50 to 1,000 people, we have named customers in productivity software and online health clinics"

  1. Two lanes confirmed with written permission for two names each, one workflow sentence per lane, Tella for recording, EmailBison plus HeyReach plus Attio.
  2. 6 seeds expanded to 1,120 lookalike accounts, 640 passed the ICP read, 31 excluded. 2,260 contacts, 1,490 verified valid, 88 catch-all in their own campaigns.
  3. One email 1 per lane, one threaded nudge each, a 24-option subject block per lane. Approved on the first read.
  4. Two campaigns launched Monday from separate inbox sets, 210 LinkedIn connections sent.
  5. Four weeks in: 1,490 sent, 19 interested, 17 of them a yes or a question to the video CTA, 13 on step 1. 17 videos recorded, 16 inside one business day. Yes rate 1.6% in productivity software, 0.9% in health clinics.
  6. 14 of 17 videos watched, 9 meetings booked, 6 from the productivity lane.

# Film-It-For-You

Most cold emails ask the prospect for something: a call, a reply, fifteen minutes. This play asks permission to make something for them, a two-minute video of their own product with your workflow inside it, and the yes comes easily because it costs them nothing. For a screen-recording software client, 7 of 7 real positives replied to that CTA, 5 of 7 on the first email and 3 of 7 to an opener that named two peer customers.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

2. The customer list, each customer's sub-vertical, and whether there is written permission to name them.
3. The one-link workflow in one sentence, the way a customer says it on a call.
4. Who records the videos, with which tool, and the booking link. That person owns the step 5 deadline.
5. Stack answers from core section 1.

- Three to five roles per account | The people who live in the workflow: head of support, head of implementation, support ops, head of CS | with: Apollo, Ocean.io | chips: support, implementation, CS ops

### 3. Write the copy
Tools: Claude

- Apply core: copy and send rules. Body under 55 words, no link in email 1; the video link only travels in a reply.
- Email 1 has three parts in a fixed order. Open with the peers: "{PEER_1} and {PEER_2} both use this for {WORKFLOW_SHORT}". ### 4. Launch one campaign per sub-vertical
Tools: EmailBison, Instantly (option), HeyReach
Flow:
= One campaign per lane | Each sub-vertical sends from its own inbox set with its own peer names; LinkedIn connection on day one for the senior roles | with: EmailBison, Instantly, HeyReach | chips: lane, inbox set, {PEER_1}, {PEER_2} | tag: send

- Apply core: send rules. One campaign per lane, never mixed: peer names only mean something inside their lane, and step 6 reads yes rate per lane.
- Upload leads with `{COMPANY}`, `{PEER_1}`, `{PEER_2}`, `{WORKFLOW}` and `{SUB_VERTICAL}`. Step 2 goes three business days after step 1; LinkedIn connection on day one for heads of support and CS.
- Stop the sequence for anyone who replies, books or opens as a deal.

### 5. The yes is the trigger: film within one business day
Tools: Tella, Loom (option), Claude, Calendly
Flow:
- Record the video | Their website or product on screen, their name said out loud, one workflow end to end, one next step, under 2 minutes 30 | with: Tella, Loom | chips: their product, their name, one workflow, one next step | tag: within one business day
- Reply in the thread | Video link plus two or three concrete times; a question reply gets the answer and the video | with: Claude, Calendly | tag: never a bare booking link

- A yes, a "sure", or a question about how it works all start the same clock: the video goes out within one business day. The reply cost them nothing and is as easy to forget.
- Record with their website or product in frame. Say their name and company in the first five seconds, show the one workflow from email 1 end to end, finish with one next step. Under two minutes thirty, no slides.
- Reply in the same thread: one line, the link, two or three specific times. Never a bare booking link; they said yes to a video, not a calendar.
- A question reply ("where is the recording stored?") gets the answer in the first line and the video anyway. A referral reply gets the video addressed to the new person, original recipient kept on the thread.

### 6. Route and measure
Tools: Slack, Attio
Flow:
- Route every reply | Positives to the reply-routing play, a Slack alert with the thread and the recording deadline, every contact and video on the CRM | with: Slack, Attio | tag: core: CRM and alerts
= Read per lane | Yes rate, videos sent on time, videos watched, meetings from videos; the video is the demo so watched and booked are the numbers | with: Attio | chips: yes rate, filmed on time, watched, booked

- Apply core: CRM and alerts. Positives go to the reply-routing play; the Slack alert carries the thread, lane, recorder and deadline. Log each contact with lane, replying step, video sent date, and watched or not.
- Weekly, per lane: sent, reply rate, "yes, film it" rate, videos sent on time, videos watched, meetings from videos. The video is the demo, so a strong yes rate with a weak watch rate is a video problem, not a copy problem. A weak yes rate means the peer names did not land: back to step 1.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- Never send a generic video. If it does not show their product and say their name, record again. Never promise a video and send a deck or a booking link.
- Peer customers are named only with written permission, per name. Without it the opener is "two support teams in {SUB_VERTICAL}"; a logo on the website is not permission.
- Keep the video under two minutes thirty. Say two minutes and mean it.
- The video link never appears in email 1 or 2, only in a reply to a yes.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-film-it-for-you for our screen recording tool, buyers are support and implementation teams at B2B SaaS 50 to 1,000 people, we have named customers in productivity software and online health clinics"

1. Two lanes confirmed with written permission for two names each, one workflow sentence per lane, Tella for recording, EmailBison plus HeyReach plus Attio.
2. 6 seeds expanded to 1,120 lookalike accounts, 640 passed the ICP read, 31 excluded. 2,260 contacts, 1,490 verified valid, 88 catch-all in their own campaigns.
3. One email 1 per lane, one threaded nudge each, a 24-option subject block per lane. Approved on the first read.
4. Two campaigns launched Monday from separate inbox sets, 210 LinkedIn connections sent.
5. Four weeks in: 1,490 sent, 19 interested, 17 of them a yes or a question to the video CTA, 13 on step 1. 17 videos recorded, 16 inside one business day. Yes rate 1.6% in productivity software, 0.9% in health clinics.
6. 14 of 17 videos watched, 9 meetings booked, 6 from the productivity lane.

Reply-Triggered Live Demo

Trigger A yes reply when the product can demo itself
Outcome The product calls them within 5 minutes

Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

A calendar link asks the prospect to do work before they have seen anything. When the product can act on a phone number, an email, a domain or a URL on its own, the yes reply should fire the product, not a booking page. An AI voice agent client ran this for two and a half months: the prospect replies yes, the agent calls within five minutes, and the demo is the product.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. What the product does from one input with nothing else from the prospect: a call to a number, a sample site from a URL, an audit on a domain, a report from their data.
  2. The product API: endpoint, auth, the input field, what it returns and how long it takes.
  3. Verticals as exact business types, the regions, and the calling hours in each.
  4. Stack answers from core section 1, the Slack channel, and the rep who reviews text output.

Steps

1Confirm the product qualifies

ToolsClaude
  • The test: can the product do something real for the prospect from one field you already hold? If the prospect has to install, connect or fill anything first, run a different play.
  • Write down input, output, time to deliver and what a failed run looks like. Never promise five minutes if the product takes thirty.
  • Text output (a site, an audit, a report) needs a human review before it goes out. Decide now who reviews it.

2Build the list by vertical

ToolsGoogle MapsSerperOcean.ioApolloLeadMagicProspeoBlitzAPIMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • Local businesses come from Google Maps and Serper, SaaS and larger firms from Ocean.io and Apollo. One list per vertical. Apply core: lists and exclusions.
  • The contact is the owner or the ops lead. For an AI voice agent client, personal injury law was the hottest vertical; in a dental clinics campaign the office manager answered as often as the owner.
  • Apply core: enrichment, plus a direct dial or mobile pass where the demo is a call. A contact with no number stays on the list with a flag.
  • Apply core: verification.

3Write the copy: the offer is the demo

ToolsClaude
  • Apply core: copy and send rules. The offer is the demo, not a meeting: "reply yes and our agent calls you in five minutes", "reply yes and we build your sample site today". Under 50 words, no link in email 1.
  • Two variants. A carries the product's own phone number; B has no number and only asks for the yes. An AI voice agent client ran both: no number produced more positive replies; the number produced more inbound calls a day and far higher intent, callers who stayed on the line for minutes and asked to meet the team.
  • Avoid the word "AI" in the subject and first line; it trips filters. Say what happens: "it calls you back".
  • Email 2 is a threaded nudge, one line restating the demo. Show drafts before upload.

4Launch one campaign per vertical

ToolsEmailBisonInstantly (option)Attio
  • Apply core: send rules. One campaign per vertical, split evenly between the two variants, from its own inbox set.
  • Send inside the calling window from the inputs; a yes at 11 pm must not fire a call at 11:05.
  • Write every contact to the CRM with play name, vertical, variant and date.

5The yes fires the product

Toolsn8nClaudeLeadMagicProspeoBlitzAPIProduct APISlack
  • Catch the positive reply with a webhook from the sending tool, or a polling pass every two minutes. Classify it with the Reply Routing play's classifier and apply core: CRM and alerts. Only interested fires.
  • If the demo is a call and the row has no number, read the signature, then run the waterfall. Nothing inside three minutes: a rep asks in the thread and the clock restarts when the number arrives.
  • Call the product API with the one input. Log triggered time, completed time and result URL on the CRM record, and post a Slack card.
  • Text output never goes out without a human review. The reviewer approves or edits in Slack, then the reply goes back in the same thread: three bullets on what the demo showed and a soft ask for 15 minutes.

6Follow up on the demo, not the reply

ToolsPhoneCalendlyAttioSlack
  • If the demo connected (call answered, site opened, report viewed), a rep calls and emails the same day, two or three touches.
  • If not, one retry the next business day inside calling hours, then a dated recall. Never a third automatic attempt.
  • Inbound calls to the product's number count as connected demos even without a reply.

7Measure product touches, not replies

ToolsAttioSlack
  • Weekly: sent, yes replies, demos triggered, demos completed, inbound calls, outputs viewed, meetings, by vertical and by variant. Positive replies are the wrong KPI here; optimizing for them produces a thin stream of lukewarm leads who forget to answer once you send the number.
  • Expect a worse contact-to-lead ratio than a plain offer. Cut verticals where demos trigger but never complete.
  • Log whether each meeting came from the agent, the thread or the website.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • Never auto-call or auto-build without the yes. A question or an objection goes to a human.
  • Respect calling hours and consent rules in the prospect's region. A yes outside the window fires at the start of the next one.
  • A demo that fails returns an apology from a human within the hour.
  • The product output never carries the Pipeline.tech credit.
  • One campaign per vertical, nothing else on the same domain in the same month.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-reply-triggered-live-demo for an AI receptionist that calls back missed leads; verticals are dental clinics, personal injury law firms and HVAC contractors in Texas and Florida"

  1. Product qualifies: takes a phone number, calls within 90 seconds. Promise set to five minutes. EmailBison plus n8n plus Attio plus Slack.
  2. 4,120 businesses from Google Maps and Serper, 3,610 after exclusions, 2,205 verified valid, 1,480 with a direct dial or mobile, 118 catch-all in their own campaigns.
  3. Two variants per vertical plus one threaded nudge, approved after one round.
  4. Six campaigns live, 2,205 contacts, started Monday.
  5. Week three: 61 yes replies, 58 demos triggered, 49 calls answered. The number variant also produced 34 inbound calls with no reply at all.
  6. Same-day follow-up on 49 connected demos and 34 inbound callers, 27 meetings booked, 14 of them personal injury law.
  7. No-number variant 1.9% yes rate, number variant 1.2% but 2.3x the meetings once inbound calls were counted. Scaled law firms, cut HVAC.

# Reply-Triggered Live Demo

A calendar link asks the prospect to do work before they have seen anything. When the product can act on a phone number, an email, a domain or a URL on its own, the yes reply should fire the product, not a booking page. An AI voice agent client ran this for two and a half months: the prospect replies yes, the agent calls within five minutes, and the demo is the product.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. What the product does from one input with nothing else from the prospect: a call to a number, a sample site from a URL, an audit on a domain, a report from their data.
2. The product API: endpoint, auth, the input field, what it returns and how long it takes.
3. Verticals as exact business types, the regions, and the calling hours in each.
4. Stack answers from core section 1, the Slack channel, and the rep who reviews text output.

## Steps

### 1. Confirm the product qualifies
Tools: Claude
Flow:
- One input, zero setup | The product acts on a phone number, an email, a domain or a URL with nothing from the prospect | with: Claude | chips: voice agent calls, site generator builds, audit runs, sample report
= Fit verdict in writing | What fires, what the prospect receives, how long it takes, what a failure looks like | chips: input, output, minutes to deliver, failure mode

- The test: can the product do something real for the prospect from one field you already hold? If the prospect has to install, connect or fill anything first, run a different play.
- Write down input, output, time to deliver and what a failed run looks like. Never promise five minutes if the product takes thirty.
- Text output (a site, an audit, a report) needs a human review before it goes out. Decide now who reviews it.

### 2. Build the list by vertical
Tools: Google Maps, Serper, Ocean.io, Apollo, LeadMagic, Prospeo, BlitzAPI, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- Businesses where the demo lands | Clinics, law firms, trades, local services, small SaaS; one list per vertical, owner or ops lead | with: Google Maps, Serper, Ocean.io, Apollo | chips: owner, ops lead, vertical, region
- Email waterfall plus a direct dial | Each vendor only sees what the one before it missed; a mobile or direct dial where the demo is a call | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo, BlitzAPI | tag: core: enrichment
- Two-pass verification | Valid to the main campaign, catch-all to its own | with: MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: verification

- Local businesses come from Google Maps and Serper, SaaS and larger firms from Ocean.io and Apollo. One list per vertical. Apply core: lists and exclusions.
- The contact is the owner or the ops lead. For an AI voice agent client, personal injury law was the hottest vertical; in a dental clinics campaign the office manager answered as often as the owner.
- Apply core: enrichment, plus a direct dial or mobile pass where the demo is a call. A contact with no number stays on the list with a flag.
- Apply core: verification.

### 3. Write the copy: the offer is the demo
Tools: Claude
Flow:
- Email 1, the demo as the offer | Reply yes and our agent calls you in five minutes; reply yes and we build your sample site today | with: Claude | chips: {VERTICAL}, {CITY}, {PRODUCT_NUMBER}
- Two variants | With the product's phone number in the email and without it | with: Claude | tag: number vs no number
= Email 2, threaded nudge | Same thread, under 50 words, the demo restated in one line | with: Claude

- Apply core: copy and send rules. The offer is the demo, not a meeting: "reply yes and our agent calls you in five minutes", "reply yes and we build your sample site today". Under 50 words, no link in email 1.
- Two variants. A carries the product's own phone number; B has no number and only asks for the yes. An AI voice agent client ran both: no number produced more positive replies; the number produced more inbound calls a day and far higher intent, callers who stayed on the line for minutes and asked to meet the team.
- Avoid the word "AI" in the subject and first line; it trips filters. Say what happens: "it calls you back".
- Email 2 is a threaded nudge, one line restating the demo. Show drafts before upload.

### 4. Launch one campaign per vertical
Tools: EmailBison, Instantly (option), Attio
Flow:
= One campaign per vertical, two variants each | Own inbox set, recipient's business hours, written to the CRM with play and variant | with: EmailBison, Instantly, Attio | tag: send

- Apply core: send rules. One campaign per vertical, split evenly between the two variants, from its own inbox set.
- Send inside the calling window from the inputs; a yes at 11 pm must not fire a call at 11:05.
- Write every contact to the CRM with play name, vertical, variant and date.

### 5. The yes fires the product
Tools: n8n, Claude, LeadMagic, Prospeo, BlitzAPI, Product API, Slack
Flow:
- Catch the yes | A webhook from the sending tool or a polling pass every two minutes; interested only, nothing else fires | with: n8n, Claude | tag: core: reply routing
- Enrich at reply time | The reply's own signature read first, then the phone waterfall if the row has no number | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo, BlitzAPI | chips: mobile, direct dial, signature
- Trigger within 5 minutes | Product API called with the one input; the agent calls, the generator builds, the audit runs | with: Product API, Slack | chips: triggered at, completed at, result URL
= Output back in the same thread | Three bullets on what the demo showed and a soft ask; text output waits for a human review | with: Claude, Slack

- Catch the positive reply with a webhook from the sending tool, or a polling pass every two minutes. Classify it with the Reply Routing play's classifier and apply core: CRM and alerts. Only `interested` fires.
- If the demo is a call and the row has no number, read the signature, then run the waterfall. Nothing inside three minutes: a rep asks in the thread and the clock restarts when the number arrives.
- Call the product API with the one input. Log triggered time, completed time and result URL on the CRM record, and post a Slack card.
- Text output never goes out without a human review. The reviewer approves or edits in Slack, then the reply goes back in the same thread: three bullets on what the demo showed and a soft ask for 15 minutes.

### 6. Follow up on the demo, not the reply
Tools: Phone, Calendly, Attio, Slack
Flow:
- Demo connected | Call answered, site opened or report viewed: a rep follows up the same day with two or three touches | with: Phone, Calendly | tag: same day
- Demo missed | One retry the next business day, then a dated recall on the CRM record | with: Attio, Slack | tag: one retry

- If the demo connected (call answered, site opened, report viewed), a rep calls and emails the same day, two or three touches.
- If not, one retry the next business day inside calling hours, then a dated recall. Never a third automatic attempt.
- Inbound calls to the product's number count as connected demos even without a reply.

### 7. Measure product touches, not replies
Tools: Attio, Slack
Flow:
= Weekly read by vertical and by variant | Sent, yes replies, demos triggered, demos completed, inbound calls, viewed, meetings; number and no number side by side | with: Attio, Slack

- Weekly: sent, yes replies, demos triggered, demos completed, inbound calls, outputs viewed, meetings, by vertical and by variant. Positive replies are the wrong KPI here; optimizing for them produces a thin stream of lukewarm leads who forget to answer once you send the number.
- Expect a worse contact-to-lead ratio than a plain offer. Cut verticals where demos trigger but never complete.
- Log whether each meeting came from the agent, the thread or the website.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- Never auto-call or auto-build without the yes. A question or an objection goes to a human.
- Respect calling hours and consent rules in the prospect's region. A yes outside the window fires at the start of the next one.
- A demo that fails returns an apology from a human within the hour.
- The product output never carries the Pipeline.tech credit.
- One campaign per vertical, nothing else on the same domain in the same month.

New Funding

Trigger A Series A to D round in the last 7 days (Form D filing or announced round)
Outcome Meetings inside the spend window

Install the Pipeline.tech "New Funding" outbound play. Fetch
