Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

Fresh money is a budget window that closes. A Form D is due within 15 days of the first sale, so it often lands before the press release. Hiring since the round shows what the money is for, and that names the buyer. One short note inside 48 hours, then wait.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it. If the user has an ICP file, read it.
  2. Which rounds count. Default: Series A to D. Seed is skipped (rarely budget for a paid GTM stack).
  3. Stack answers from core section 1.
  4. The offer in one sentence and one proof point. Both go into the copy.

Steps

1Catch the raise early

ToolsSEC EDGARSerperPredictLeads (optional)Crunchbase (optional)
  • Pull new Form D filings daily from EDGAR full-text search (https://efts.sec.gov/LATEST/search-index?q=...&forms=D, free, no key). Keep offerings with amount sold above the user's floor (default 2M USD) and an issuer in the ICP geography.
  • Confirm or add rounds with a Serper news search for "raises" OR "funding" OR "Series" in the trailing 7 days.
  • Older than 7 days: skip. It has been worked by everyone.
  • Write one row per company: name, domain (resolve it in a separate pass, asking inline costs recall), amount, round, lead investors, filing or article URL, date.

2Filter to your ICP

ToolsOcean.ioApolloClaude
  • Enrich headcount, industry, location.
  • Read each company's own website and judge ICP fit with a yes/no and one reason. Drop the no's before any paid people lookup.
  • Apply core: exclusions.

3Read where the money goes

ToolsSerperCareers pagesLinkedIn
  • Pull open roles posted since the round. The teams hiring are what the money funds.
  • Pick the buyer the round pays for: a sales tool goes to the sales leader, a finance tool to finance, a product tool to product. The CEO only when headcount is under 50 or no function head exists.
  • Score round size against headcount. A large round at a small team predicts spend on exactly what they are hiring for.

4Enrich and verify

ToolsLeadMagicProspeoMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • Apply core: enrichment (one buyer plus one likely champion per account).
  • Apply core: verification.

5Tier and alert

ToolsSlackAttio
  • Tier 1: ICP fit plus round above the user's Tier 1 floor (default 10M USD) plus hiring in the function you sell to. Post a Slack card with company, round, investors, roles opened, buyer, LinkedIn URL, and a one-line suggested opener. The owner sends a personal LinkedIn or email note within 48 hours.
  • Tier 2 and 3: go to the campaign in step 6.
  • Write every account to the CRM with signal type funding, date, amount and source URL.

6Write and launch

ToolsEmailBisonInstantly (option)HeyReach (optional)
  • Apply core: copy and send rules.
  • Angle: congratulations is the trigger, not the message. Open on what they will hire for next, tie your offer to that, one direct ask. Amount and lead investor are personalization, used once.
  • Draft 3 angle variants for email 1 and one threaded nudge for email 2. Show them to the user for approval before upload.
  • Upload leads with variables {ROUND}, {AMOUNT}, {HIRING_FOR}, {INVESTOR}; create the campaign from its own inbox set; schedule to start the next business day.
  • Stop the sequence for any account that replies, books, or appears in the CRM as an open deal.

7Report

ToolsAttioSlack
  • Weekly: rounds caught, ICP-fit rate, Tier 1 count, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings. Compare against the user's cold baseline.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • Never state the amount if the only source is a Form D with "indefinite" or a range. Say "the round" instead.
  • Never mention the filing itself. "Saw you raised" is enough.
  • One account, one sequence. Do not run this play and a cold play on the same domain in the same month.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-new-funding for our sales-training offer, ICP is B2B SaaS 50 to 500 people in the US"

  1. Asked for the offer line and proof, confirmed EmailBison plus Attio plus Slack.
  2. Pulled 212 Form D filings and 48 news rounds for the week, 73 after geography and amount floor.
  3. 31 passed the website ICP check, 4 removed as customers or open deals.
  4. 27 buyers plus 19 champions found, 41 verified valid, 3 catch-all kept in a separate campaign.
  5. 6 Tier 1 Slack cards posted. 35 contacts loaded into "New Funding, week 46", approved copy, scheduled for Monday.

# New Funding

Fresh money is a budget window that closes. A Form D is due within 15 days of the first sale, so it often lands before the press release. Hiring since the round shows what the money is for, and that names the buyer. One short note inside 48 hours, then wait.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it. If the user has an ICP file, read it.
2. Which rounds count. Default: Series A to D. Seed is skipped (rarely budget for a paid GTM stack).
3. Stack answers from core section 1.
4. The offer in one sentence and one proof point. Both go into the copy.

## Steps

### 1. Catch the raise early
Tools: SEC EDGAR, Serper, PredictLeads (optional), Crunchbase (optional)
Flow:
- Form D filings | Pulled daily from EDGAR full-text search; due within 15 days of first sale, so it often lands before the press | with: SEC EDGAR | chips: amount sold, issuer, state, date | tag: free source
- Announced rounds | News search for raises in the trailing 7 days; anything older has been worked by everyone | with: Serper, PredictLeads, Crunchbase | tag: confirm
= One row per company | Domain resolved in its own pass, since asking inline costs recall | chips: name, domain, amount, round, lead investors, source URL

- Pull new Form D filings daily from EDGAR full-text search (`https://efts.sec.gov/LATEST/search-index?q=...&forms=D`, free, no key). Keep offerings with amount sold above the user's floor (default 2M USD) and an issuer in the ICP geography.
- Confirm or add rounds with a Serper news search for "raises" OR "funding" OR "Series" in the trailing 7 days.
- Older than 7 days: skip. It has been worked by everyone.
- Write one row per company: name, domain (resolve it in a separate pass, asking inline costs recall), amount, round, lead investors, filing or article URL, date.

### 2. Filter to your ICP
Tools: Ocean.io, Apollo, Claude
Flow:
- Firmographics | Headcount, industry and location added to every row | with: Ocean.io, Apollo
- Website read | A yes or no ICP verdict with one reason, before any paid people lookup | with: Claude | tag: core: exclusions

- Enrich headcount, industry, location.
- Read each company's own website and judge ICP fit with a yes/no and one reason. Drop the no's before any paid people lookup.
- Apply core: exclusions.

### 3. Read where the money goes
Tools: Serper, Careers pages, LinkedIn
Flow:
- Roles opened since the round | The teams hiring are what the money funds | with: Serper, Careers pages, LinkedIn | chips: function, seniority, post date
= Pick the buyer the round pays for | A sales tool goes to the sales leader, a finance tool to finance; the CEO only under 50 people | chips: buyer, likely champion, round size vs headcount

- Pull open roles posted since the round. The teams hiring are what the money funds.
- Pick the buyer the round pays for: a sales tool goes to the sales leader, a finance tool to finance, a product tool to product. The CEO only when headcount is under 50 or no function head exists.
- Score round size against headcount. A large round at a small team predicts spend on exactly what they are hiring for.

### 4. Enrich and verify
Tools: LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- Email waterfall | Each vendor only sees the contacts the one before it missed | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo | tag: core: enrichment
- Two-pass verification | Valid to the main campaign, catch-all to its own campaign | with: MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: verification

- Apply core: enrichment (one buyer plus one likely champion per account).
- Apply core: verification.

### 5. Tier and alert
Tools: Slack, Attio
Flow:
- Tier 1 | ICP fit plus a round above the floor plus hiring in your function: a Slack card, then a personal note within 48 hours | with: Slack | chips: company, round, investors, roles opened, buyer, suggested opener | tag: high touch
- Tier 2 and 3 | Go to the campaign, written to the CRM with signal type funding | with: Attio | chips: funding, date, amount, source URL | tag: at volume

- Tier 1: ICP fit plus round above the user's Tier 1 floor (default 10M USD) plus hiring in the function you sell to. Post a Slack card with company, round, investors, roles opened, buyer, LinkedIn URL, and a one-line suggested opener. The owner sends a personal LinkedIn or email note within 48 hours.
- Tier 2 and 3: go to the campaign in step 6.
- Write every account to the CRM with signal type `funding`, date, amount and source URL.

### 6. Write and launch
Tools: EmailBison, Instantly (option), HeyReach (optional)
Flow:
- Three angles, one nudge | Congratulations is the trigger, not the message: open on what they will hire for next, one direct ask | with: Claude | chips: {ROUND}, {AMOUNT}, {HIRING_FOR}, {INVESTOR}
= Launch from its own inbox set | Starts the next business day; stops for any account that replies, books or shows as an open deal | with: EmailBison, Instantly, HeyReach | tag: send

- Apply core: copy and send rules.
- Angle: congratulations is the trigger, not the message. Open on what they will hire for next, tie your offer to that, one direct ask. Amount and lead investor are personalization, used once.
- Draft 3 angle variants for email 1 and one threaded nudge for email 2. Show them to the user for approval before upload.
- Upload leads with variables `{ROUND}`, `{AMOUNT}`, `{HIRING_FOR}`, `{INVESTOR}`; create the campaign from its own inbox set; schedule to start the next business day.
- Stop the sequence for any account that replies, books, or appears in the CRM as an open deal.

### 7. Report
Tools: Attio, Slack
Flow:
= Weekly read | Rounds caught, ICP-fit rate, Tier 1 count, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings, against the cold baseline | with: Attio, Slack

- Weekly: rounds caught, ICP-fit rate, Tier 1 count, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings. Compare against the user's cold baseline.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- Never state the amount if the only source is a Form D with "indefinite" or a range. Say "the round" instead.
- Never mention the filing itself. "Saw you raised" is enough.
- One account, one sequence. Do not run this play and a cold play on the same domain in the same month.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-new-funding for our sales-training offer, ICP is B2B SaaS 50 to 500 people in the US"

1. Asked for the offer line and proof, confirmed EmailBison plus Attio plus Slack.
2. Pulled 212 Form D filings and 48 news rounds for the week, 73 after geography and amount floor.
3. 31 passed the website ICP check, 4 removed as customers or open deals.
4. 27 buyers plus 19 champions found, 41 verified valid, 3 catch-all kept in a separate campaign.
5. 6 Tier 1 Slack cards posted. 35 contacts loaded into "New Funding, week 46", approved copy, scheduled for Monday.

ToolsClaude

  • Tier 2: ICP fit and VP and above between day 30 and 90, or a function owner under 200 people in the first 30 days. Two-step email in week two plus a LinkedIn connection.
  • Tier 3: acceptable fit inside 90 days, plus the warm-list 6 to 12 month window. Email only.
  • Nothing goes out in week one. Write every person to the CRM with play name, tier, tenure window, start date and source URL.

ToolsClaudeEmailBisonInstantly (option)HeyReach

  • Warm-list variant: these people already know you, so the tone is risk mitigation. "Most new VPs rebuild the outbound stack in 60 days" is the frame, followed by what the ones who get it right do differently.

  • Weekly: moves caught, confirmed rate, ICP-fit rate, Tier 1 count, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings, split by tenure window and by first-in-role versus replacement, against the cold baseline.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • Nothing in week one, ever. The sequence runs from day 8 to day 90; a move older than 90 days never appears in copy, with a new CEO the one exception at 12 months.
  • Never say you were watching them. Name the move the way a peer would.
  • A single feed gets start dates wrong by months and reads promotions as moves; every move needs a second source.
  • One person, one sequence. A move from three feeds is one signal, not three emails. The warm list stays subscribed. Never run this and a cold play on the same domain in the same month.

# New Leader in Seat

A new leader arrives with budget, a mandate and no loyalty to whoever the last person bought from. For roughly 90 days they are expected to change things, and the stack they inherited is the first item on the table. Reach them in that window with what peers in the same seat fix first, and the conversation is about their new job rather than your product.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it.
2. The seat that buys: exact titles and functions, with the seniority floor.
3. Stack answers from core section 1, plus where the first-party list lives (newsletter, CRM).
4. The offer in one sentence, one proof point, and which customers may be named in writing.

## Steps

### 1. Define the seat that buys
Tools: Claude
Flow:
- The seat in writing | Exact titles and functions that own the budget: VP and above, or the function owner at companies under 200 people | with: Claude | chips: titles, functions, seniority floor, company size
= Builder or replacer | First-in-role leaders build from nothing and replacements inherit a stack; mark which the offer serves and how the copy changes | chips: first-in-role, replacement, copy angle

- With the user, write the titles and functions whose arrival means budget moves: VP Sales, CRO, VP Marketing, Head of RevOps, or whichever seat buys what you sell. The floor is VP and above; under 200 people the owner of the function counts whatever the title says.
- Note first-in-role versus replacement. A builder wants a working system fast; a replacement inherited a stack and wants to know what to keep and what the last person got wrong.
- Add the CEO as its own seat. A new CEO in the last 12 months reopens every budget below them.

### 2. Pull moves from third-party sources
Tools: LinkedIn, Sales Navigator, Apollo, AI Ark (option), Serper
Flow:
- Job-change alerts on the target list | Saved lead searches on the seats from step 1 flag title and company changes across the account list every day | with: LinkedIn, Sales Navigator | chips: new title, new company, start month
- People-data job changes | A job-change enrichment on the same account list catches the moves the alerts missed | with: Apollo, AI Ark | tag: second feed
- Press and announcements | Searches for appointment language paired with the seat titles across news, press releases and company blogs | with: Serper | chips: appoints, joins as, named, promoted to

- Load the target accounts into Sales Navigator and save the seats as lead searches so job-change alerts arrive daily.
- Run a job-change enrichment on the same accounts with Apollo, or AI Ark when the list is large.
- Run Serper searches weekly for appointment phrases ("appoints", "joins as", "named") paired with the seat titles. Press catches CEO moves weeks before the LinkedIn profile updates.

### 3. Re-enrich the warm list by tenure window
Tools: Beehiiv, Attio, HubSpot (option), Apollo, Prospeo
Flow:
- Export the warm list | Newsletter subscribers and CRM contacts with a work email, customers and open deals left out | with: Beehiiv, Attio, HubSpot | chips: work email, company, last known title
- Re-enrich title and start date | Current title, company and start date on every row; a changed title or company is a move | with: Apollo, Prospeo | tag: first party
= Split by tenure | 3, 6 and 12 months in seat, plus companies with a new CEO in the last 12 months | chips: under 3 months, 3 to 6 months, 6 to 12 months, new CEO 12 months

- Export every subscriber and CRM contact with a work email; personal addresses are matched to a company first or left out.
- Re-enrich current title, company and start date on every row. A changed title or company is a move.
- Split the moves into windows: under 3 months, 3 to 6, 6 to 12, plus a group whose company has a new CEO in the last 12 months. Rows past 12 months stay in the normal nurture.

### 4. Confirm, dedupe, qualify and score
Tools: LinkedIn, Ocean.io, Apollo, Claude, Attio
Flow:
- Second source | Every move is confirmed on LinkedIn or in press before it counts; one row per person per move with start date and previous company | with: LinkedIn, Claude | chips: confirmed, start date, previous company, source URL
- ICP check on the new company | Headcount, industry and location, then a yes or no verdict from the company website | with: Ocean.io, Apollo, Claude | tag: core: exclusions
= Score | Fit, days in seat, first-in-role or replacement, and whether the previous company was a customer | with: Claude, Attio | chips: fit, days in seat, builder or replacer, former customer

- Confirm each move with a second source: LinkedIn for a press hit, press or the team page for a LinkedIn hit. Dedupe by person. Record the start date and previous company.
- The person qualifies first, then the company: headcount, industry, location, and a yes or no ICP verdict from the website with one reason. Apply core: exclusions, and check the previous company against the customer list too.
- Score on four fields: ICP fit, days in seat, first-in-role or replacement, and whether the previous company was a customer. Store all four on the CRM record with the start date and source URL.

### 5. Enrich and verify
Tools: LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- Email waterfall at the new company | The new work address, never the one from the previous company; each vendor only sees what the one before it missed | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo | tag: core: enrichment
- Two-pass verification | Valid to the main campaign, catch-all to its own campaign | with: MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: verification

- Find the address at the new company, not the old one. New addresses reach data vendors two to four weeks after the start date, so re-run the waterfall weekly on anything empty.
- Apply core: enrichment.
- Apply core: verification.

### 6. Tier and alert
Tools: Slack, LinkedIn, HeyReach, Phone, Attio
Flow:
- Tier 1 | ICP fit plus VP and above in the first 30 days, or a former customer: Slack alert, a LinkedIn connection with a congratulations note, a call in week two | with: Slack, LinkedIn, HeyReach, Phone | chips: name, new title, company, start date, previous company, builder or replacer | tag: high touch
- Tier 2 and 3 | Go to the two-step email in week two, written to the CRM with signal type new leader | with: Attio | chips: new leader, start date, tenure window, tier | tag: at volume

- Tier 1: ICP fit plus VP and above in the first 30 days, or anyone whose previous company was a customer. Post a Slack alert with a one-line opener. The owner sends a LinkedIn connection with a short congratulations note, then calls in week two.
- Tier 2: ICP fit and VP and above between day 30 and 90, or a function owner under 200 people in the first 30 days. Two-step email in week two plus a LinkedIn connection.
- Tier 3: acceptable fit inside 90 days, plus the warm-list 6 to 12 month window. Email only.
- Nothing goes out in week one. Write every person to the CRM with play name, tier, tenure window, start date and source URL.

### 7. Write and launch
Tools: Claude, EmailBison, Instantly (option), HeyReach
Flow:
- Their job first | Open on the move, then what peers in that seat fix first in the first 90 days; one direct ask | with: Claude | chips: {NEW_TITLE}, {COMPANY}, {PREVIOUS_COMPANY}, {DAYS_IN_SEAT}
- Warm-list variant | Risk-mitigation tone for readers who already know you: most new VPs rebuild the outbound stack in 60 days | with: Claude | tag: variant
= Launch from its own inbox set | Email 1 lands no earlier than day 8 in seat and the sequence stops at day 90 or on a reply, a booked call or an open deal | with: EmailBison, Instantly, HeyReach | tag: send

- Apply core: copy and send rules.
- Angle: their new job first, your product second. Name the move in the first sentence, say what peers in that seat fix first in their 90 days, then make one direct ask. Builders hear what a first stack looks like done right; replacers hear what to audit before deciding what to keep.
- Warm-list variant: these people already know you, so the tone is risk mitigation. "Most new VPs rebuild the outbound stack in 60 days" is the frame, followed by what the ones who get it right do differently.
- Name customers only with written permission; otherwise "a Series B team in the same space".
- Draft three variants for email 1 (builder, replacer, warm list) plus one threaded nudge for email 2. Show them to the user before upload.
- Upload leads with `{NEW_TITLE}`, `{COMPANY}`, `{PREVIOUS_COMPANY}`, `{DAYS_IN_SEAT}`; create the campaign from its own inbox set; email 1 lands no earlier than day 8 in seat, Tier 2 LinkedIn connection the same day.
- Stop on a reply, a booking, an open deal or day 90.

### 8. Report
Tools: Attio, Slack
Flow:
= Weekly read by window and role type | Moves caught, confirmed rate, ICP-fit rate, Tier 1 count, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings, split by tenure window and by first-in-role or replacement | with: Attio, Slack

- Weekly: moves caught, confirmed rate, ICP-fit rate, Tier 1 count, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings, split by tenure window and by first-in-role versus replacement, against the cold baseline.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- Nothing in week one, ever. The sequence runs from day 8 to day 90; a move older than 90 days never appears in copy, with a new CEO the one exception at 12 months.
- Never say you were watching them. Name the move the way a peer would.
- A single feed gets start dates wrong by months and reads promotions as moves; every move needs a second source.
- One person, one sequence. A move from three feeds is one signal, not three emails. The warm list stays subscribed. Never run this and a cold play on the same domain in the same month.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-new-leader-in-seat for our outbound agency, B2B SaaS 50 to 500 people in the US, seats VP Sales, CRO, VP Marketing and Head of RevOps, plus a 4,200-subscriber newsletter"

1. Asked for the offer line, proof and nameable customers; confirmed Sales Navigator, Apollo, Beehiiv, Attio, EmailBison, HeyReach and Slack.
2. 2,300 target accounts in Sales Navigator; 212 job-change alerts in month one, Apollo added 148, Serper found 31 announcements.
3. Warm list: 2,960 of 4,200 subscribers with a work email, 184 moves: 38 under 3 months, 61 at 3 to 6, 85 at 6 to 12; 47 companies with a new CEO in 12 months.
4. 391 third-party moves deduped to 274, 231 confirmed, 142 at ICP-fit companies, 11 removed as customers or in sequence. 29 first-in-role, 102 replacements, 6 former customers.
5. 131 addresses found at the new company, 19 waiting for next week's re-run; 118 verified valid, 7 catch-all in a separate campaign.
6. 23 Tier 1 Slack alerts and connection notes, 9 calls booked for week two. 95 Tier 2 and 3 plus 99 warm-list rows loaded into "New Leader, November", copy approved, each row scheduled from day 8.

Play 17 · Third-party signals

Open Roles

Trigger A net-new job post for a role you replace or support
Outcome A conversation with the manager the role reports to
Channels email, linkedin, call

Give this to your agent 
Install the Pipeline.tech "Open Roles" outbound play. Fetch
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/core/SKILL.md and
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/plays/open-roles/SKILL.md,
save them as ~/.claude/skills/pipeline-outbound-core/SKILL.md and
~/.claude/skills/pipeline-open-roles/SKILL.md, then run /pipeline-open-roles.
Paste into Claude Code. It installs the shared core rules plus this play, then runs it as /pipeline-open-roles.
Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

A company hiring for work your product does or supports has said the need out loud, with a date on it. The job post names the problem, the team that owns it and roughly how much they plan to spend fixing it. Write to the manager the seat reports to while it is still empty.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it. If the user has an ICP file, read it.
  2. The roles that signal need, written as exact title phrases, not functions. See step 1.
  3. Stack answers from core section 1, plus whether the user wants a paid job feed or free sources only.
  4. The offer in one sentence, one proof point, and whether the offer replaces the role or helps while they hire. Both go into the copy.

Steps

1Define the roles that signal need

ToolsClaude
  • With the user, list the exact titles that mean "they need what we sell". A specific phrase beats a generic one: "Founding SDR" beats "SDR", "Outbound Lead" beats "Sales". Add synonyms and seniority words for each.
  • Mark each title as replace (your product does the job) or support (your product makes the hire productive). The copy angle differs.
  • Three open reqs in the same function beat one. Set a minimum count per title where volume matters.

2Pull new posts daily

ToolsGreenhouseLeverAshbySerperCareers pagesTheirStack (option)PredictLeads (option)
  • Resolve each target company's ATS. Greenhouse, Lever and Ashby serve a public JSON board per company, free and dated. Pull every board once a day.
  • For companies with no public ATS, run a Serper search on the title phrases across job boards and careers pages. Add TheirStack or PredictLeads when the list is large enough that a feed is cheaper than scraping.
  • Diff today's pull against yesterday's snapshot. Only net-new posts move forward. A post you have seen before is not a signal twice.
  • Write one row per company per hiring event: company, domain, title, post date, req count, source URL. Keep the raw pull with a source column.

3Read every post and check fit

ToolsClaudeOcean.ioApollo
  • Have a cheap model read the full post and answer three questions: does it describe work your product does or supports, is the poster the company or a recruiter, and is it older than 60 days. Drop recruiters and agencies. Flag anything over 60 days; it stays in the data but never counts as fresh.
  • Enrich headcount, industry and location. Read the company website and give a yes or no ICP verdict with one reason. Drop the no's before any paid people lookup.
  • Apply core: exclusions.

4Put every account in one bucket

ToolsClaudeAttio
  • Every account on the list lands in exactly one bucket: currently hiring (a live post for a watched title), recent hire (someone started in the role in the last 90 days, visible on LinkedIn or the team page), or no signal.
  • If an account qualifies for two, it is currently hiring. Never add a fourth bucket, and never leave an account unbucketed.
  • Store the bucket on the CRM record with the post date and source URL so the weekly read in step 8 can split by it.

5Find the hiring manager, enrich and verify

ToolsLinkedInApolloOcean.ioLeadMagicProspeoMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • The post usually says who the role reports to. Find that person, not the recruiter or the head of people. Under 50 people it is often the founder. Add one likely champion, a peer of the open seat or the manager's own boss.
  • Apply core: enrichment.
  • Apply core: verification.

6Tier and alert

ToolsSlackAttioPhone
  • Tier 1: ICP fit plus either a senior title (head, director, VP, founding) or three or more open reqs in the function. Post a Slack card with company, title, req count, post date, the manager, their LinkedIn URL and a one-line opener. The owner calls within 48 hours with an offer that helps while they hire.
  • Tier 2 and 3: go to the campaign in step 7. Tier 2 also gets a LinkedIn connection request.
  • Write every account to the CRM with signal type hiring, title, post date, bucket and source URL.

7Write and launch

ToolsClaudeEmailBisonInstantly (option)HeyReach
  • Apply core: copy and send rules.
  • Angle: the post is the trigger, so quote three to six of its own words and name what the empty seat costs while it stays open (pipeline not built, calls not made, tickets not answered). One direct ask.
  • Variant for a first SDR or BDR post: every rep takes 8 to 12 weeks to ramp. Offer to run outbound while theirs ramps, then hand the system over to the new hire. Sell the system, not seats.
  • Draft three angle variants for email 1 and one threaded nudge for email 2. Show them to the user for approval before upload.
  • Upload leads with {ROLE}, {POST_PHRASE}, {REQ_COUNT}, {TEAM}; create the campaign from its own inbox set; schedule to start the next business day. Send the LinkedIn connection on day 1 for Tier 2.
  • Stop the sequence for any account that replies, books, shows as an open deal, or whose post comes down or whose hire is announced. Re-check post status daily from the same feed.

8Report

ToolsAttioSlack
  • Weekly: posts caught, net-new after the diff, ICP-fit rate, Tier 1 count, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings. Split every number by bucket so currently hiring and recent hire read on their own, and compare to the cold baseline.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • A post older than 60 days is never quoted and never counts as a signal. A recent hire older than 90 days moves to no signal.
  • Never write to HR, talent or a recruiter. If the reporting line cannot be found, the account waits in the bucket until it can.
  • Never tell a company to stop hiring. The offer helps while the seat is empty, or replaces the seat only when the offer genuinely does that job.
  • One account, one sequence. Three open reqs earn a stronger tier, not three emails. Do not run this play and a cold play on the same domain in the same month.

# Open Roles

A company hiring for work your product does or supports has said the need out loud, with a date on it. The job post names the problem, the team that owns it and roughly how much they plan to spend fixing it. Write to the manager the seat reports to while it is still empty.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it. If the user has an ICP file, read it.
2. The roles that signal need, written as exact title phrases, not functions. See step 1.
3. Stack answers from core section 1, plus whether the user wants a paid job feed or free sources only.
4. The offer in one sentence, one proof point, and whether the offer replaces the role or helps while they hire. Both go into the copy.

## Steps

### 1. Define the roles that signal need
Tools: Claude
Flow:
- Title phrases | Specific titles beat functions: Founding SDR beats SDR, Head of Outbound beats Sales | with: Claude | chips: exact titles, synonyms, seniority words
= Role list in writing | Each title mapped to replace or support, with the minimum req count that counts | chips: title, replace or support, min reqs

- With the user, list the exact titles that mean "they need what we sell". A specific phrase beats a generic one: "Founding SDR" beats "SDR", "Outbound Lead" beats "Sales". Add synonyms and seniority words for each.
- Mark each title as replace (your product does the job) or support (your product makes the hire productive). The copy angle differs.
- Three open reqs in the same function beat one. Set a minimum count per title where volume matters.

### 2. Pull new posts daily
Tools: Greenhouse, Lever, Ashby, Serper, Careers pages, TheirStack (option), PredictLeads (option)
Flow:
- Public ATS feeds | Greenhouse, Lever and Ashby expose JSON job boards per company, free and dated, for the whole target list | with: Greenhouse, Lever, Ashby | chips: title, location, post date, board URL | tag: free source
- Boards and careers pages | Search for the title phrases on job boards and careers pages, for companies without a public ATS | with: Serper, Careers pages | tag: fill the gaps
- Paid feeds | A single aggregated feed with post dates and counts when the target list is large | with: TheirStack, PredictLeads | tag: option
= Diff against yesterday | Only posts absent from the last snapshot count; one row per company per hiring event | chips: company, domain, title, post date, req count, source URL

- Resolve each target company's ATS. Greenhouse, Lever and Ashby serve a public JSON board per company, free and dated. Pull every board once a day.
- For companies with no public ATS, run a Serper search on the title phrases across job boards and careers pages. Add TheirStack or PredictLeads when the list is large enough that a feed is cheaper than scraping.
- Diff today's pull against yesterday's snapshot. Only net-new posts move forward. A post you have seen before is not a signal twice.
- Write one row per company per hiring event: company, domain, title, post date, req count, source URL. Keep the raw pull with a `source` column.

### 3. Read every post and check fit
Tools: Claude, Ocean.io, Apollo
Flow:
- Post read | A cheap model confirms the need from the post text, drops recruiters and agencies, flags anything older than 60 days | with: Claude | chips: need confirmed, replace or support, age flag
- ICP check | Headcount, industry and location added, then a yes or no verdict from the company website | with: Ocean.io, Apollo, Claude | tag: core: exclusions

- Have a cheap model read the full post and answer three questions: does it describe work your product does or supports, is the poster the company or a recruiter, and is it older than 60 days. Drop recruiters and agencies. Flag anything over 60 days; it stays in the data but never counts as fresh.
- Enrich headcount, industry and location. Read the company website and give a yes or no ICP verdict with one reason. Drop the no's before any paid people lookup.
- Apply core: exclusions.

### 4. Put every account in one bucket
Tools: Claude, Attio
Flow:
= Three buckets | Currently hiring, recent hire under 90 days, or no signal; a tie goes to currently hiring and there is no fourth bucket | with: Claude, Attio | chips: currently hiring, recent hire, no signal

- Every account on the list lands in exactly one bucket: currently hiring (a live post for a watched title), recent hire (someone started in the role in the last 90 days, visible on LinkedIn or the team page), or no signal.
- If an account qualifies for two, it is currently hiring. Never add a fourth bucket, and never leave an account unbucketed.
- Store the bucket on the CRM record with the post date and source URL so the weekly read in step 8 can split by it.

### 5. Find the hiring manager, enrich and verify
Tools: LinkedIn, Apollo, Ocean.io, LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- The leader the role reports to | Read the post for the reporting line, then confirm the person on LinkedIn; never HR or talent | with: LinkedIn, Apollo, Ocean.io | chips: manager, one champion, founder under 50 people
- Email waterfall | Each vendor only sees the contacts the one before it missed | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo | tag: core: enrichment
- Two-pass verification | Valid to the main campaign, catch-all to its own campaign | with: MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: verification

- The post usually says who the role reports to. Find that person, not the recruiter or the head of people. Under 50 people it is often the founder. Add one likely champion, a peer of the open seat or the manager's own boss.
- Apply core: enrichment.
- Apply core: verification.

### 6. Tier and alert
Tools: Slack, Attio, Phone
Flow:
- Tier 1 | ICP fit plus a senior title or three or more reqs: a Slack card, then a call with an offer that helps while they hire | with: Slack, Phone | chips: company, title, req count, post date, manager, suggested opener | tag: high touch
- Tier 2 and 3 | Go to the campaign, written to the CRM with signal type hiring | with: Attio | chips: hiring, title, post date, source URL | tag: at volume

- Tier 1: ICP fit plus either a senior title (head, director, VP, founding) or three or more open reqs in the function. Post a Slack card with company, title, req count, post date, the manager, their LinkedIn URL and a one-line opener. The owner calls within 48 hours with an offer that helps while they hire.
- Tier 2 and 3: go to the campaign in step 7. Tier 2 also gets a LinkedIn connection request.
- Write every account to the CRM with signal type `hiring`, title, post date, bucket and source URL.

### 7. Write and launch
Tools: Claude, EmailBison, Instantly (option), HeyReach
Flow:
- Quote the post | Open on the post's own words and what the empty seat costs each month; one direct ask | with: Claude | chips: {ROLE}, {POST_PHRASE}, {REQ_COUNT}, {TEAM}
- First SDR variant | Every rep takes 8 to 12 weeks to ramp: run outbound while yours ramps, then hand the system off | with: Claude | tag: variant
= Launch from its own inbox set | Email plus LinkedIn connection; stops when the post comes down or a hire is announced | with: EmailBison, Instantly, HeyReach | tag: send

- Apply core: copy and send rules.
- Angle: the post is the trigger, so quote three to six of its own words and name what the empty seat costs while it stays open (pipeline not built, calls not made, tickets not answered). One direct ask.
- Variant for a first SDR or BDR post: every rep takes 8 to 12 weeks to ramp. Offer to run outbound while theirs ramps, then hand the system over to the new hire. Sell the system, not seats.
- Draft three angle variants for email 1 and one threaded nudge for email 2. Show them to the user for approval before upload.
- Upload leads with `{ROLE}`, `{POST_PHRASE}`, `{REQ_COUNT}`, `{TEAM}`; create the campaign from its own inbox set; schedule to start the next business day. Send the LinkedIn connection on day 1 for Tier 2.
- Stop the sequence for any account that replies, books, shows as an open deal, or whose post comes down or whose hire is announced. Re-check post status daily from the same feed.

### 8. Report
Tools: Attio, Slack
Flow:
= Weekly read by bucket | Posts caught, net-new rate, ICP-fit rate, Tier 1 count, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings, split by hiring bucket | with: Attio, Slack

- Weekly: posts caught, net-new after the diff, ICP-fit rate, Tier 1 count, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings. Split every number by bucket so currently hiring and recent hire read on their own, and compare to the cold baseline.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- A post older than 60 days is never quoted and never counts as a signal. A recent hire older than 90 days moves to no signal.
- Never write to HR, talent or a recruiter. If the reporting line cannot be found, the account waits in the bucket until it can.
- Never tell a company to stop hiring. The offer helps while the seat is empty, or replaces the seat only when the offer genuinely does that job.
- One account, one sequence. Three open reqs earn a stronger tier, not three emails. Do not run this play and a cold play on the same domain in the same month.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-open-roles for our outbound agency, ICP is B2B SaaS 20 to 300 people in the US, watching Founding SDR, SDR, BDR and Head of Outbound"

1. Asked for the offer line and proof, set the first-SDR variant, confirmed EmailBison plus HeyReach plus Attio plus Slack.
2. Resolved 1,840 target companies to 612 Greenhouse, Lever and Ashby boards; Serper covered the rest. Day one snapshot 3,210 open posts, 94 net-new on day two across watched titles.
3. 61 posts confirmed as real need, 18 dropped as recruiters or agencies, 9 flagged over 60 days. 44 passed the ICP check, 5 removed as customers or in sequence.
4. Buckets: 39 currently hiring, 14 recent hire from the wider list, rest no signal.
5. 39 managers plus 27 champions found, 58 verified valid, 4 catch-all in a separate campaign.
6. 7 Tier 1 Slack cards posted, 3 calls booked for the week. 51 contacts loaded into "Open Roles, week 46", approved copy, scheduled for Monday.

Play 18 · Third-party signals

Stack Switch

Trigger A competitor removed or added, a pairing tool added, or a renewal due on a target account, with dates
Outcome A displacement conversation with a reason to leave
Channels email, linkedin, call

Give this to your agent 
Install the Pipeline.tech "Stack Switch" outbound play. Fetch
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/core/SKILL.md and
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/plays/stack-switch/SKILL.md,
save them as ~/.claude/skills/pipeline-outbound-core/SKILL.md and
~/.claude/skills/pipeline-stack-switch/SKILL.md, then run /pipeline-stack-switch.
Paste into Claude Code. It installs the shared core rules plus this play, then runs it as /pipeline-stack-switch.
Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

The change is the signal, not the state. A company running a competitor is a cold account, a company that dropped that competitor last month has an open window, and a company that just added the tool people pair with yours is heading toward a renewal decision on a date you can estimate. Two dated stack signals inside a quarter earn a call, and the email leads with a reason to leave, never a feature list.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it. Read the user's ICP file if one exists.
  2. The target list as a CSV or CRM view, one domain per row. This play watches a list; it does not build one.
  3. Stack answers from core section 1.
  4. The switch offer in one sentence: a concrete reason to leave an incumbent (done-for-you migration, a price or term guarantee, a capability the incumbent lacks that they already pay for elsewhere). Features only: stop and build the offer first.

Steps

1Write down the tools that matter

ToolsClaudeAttioGoogle Sheets
  • Write three lists in a sheet: competitors you displace, pairing tools (what a buyer adopts right before or after your product), and renewal-heavy incumbents on annual contracts.
  • Pull two years of won deals from the CRM and note the previous tool on each. Switches that actually closed go to the top; the rest are dropped.
  • For each tool, record its category, the usual contract length (12 months when nobody knows), and a fingerprint hint: a script domain, a cookie name, a CSS prefix or a widget path.

2Detect the stack, free first

ToolsFirecrawlClaudeBuiltWith (option)TheirStack (option)
  • Fetch the homepage HTML plus one product page per domain. Save the raw HTML so reruns cost nothing.
  • Match the watch list fingerprints against script sources, meta tags, cookie names and widget paths; store the matched string as evidence. Then run a cheap model over the same HTML asking which vendors the site runs, so unlisted tools still land in the snapshot.
  • Mark each fetch ok, blocked or timeout. Blocked domains and categories that leave no trace in HTML (back-office tools) go to BuiltWith or TheirStack if the user has either; otherwise they stay flagged no detection and are watched through job posts only.

3Snapshot and diff

ToolsGoogle SheetsAttioClaude
  • Store every detection with first-seen and last-seen dates. Never overwrite; a detection that stops appearing keeps its last-seen date, which is the signal.
  • Rerun monthly and diff against the previous snapshot. Absent for two consecutive runs counts as removed (one miss is usually a page change). Present for the first time counts as added.
  • Write each change to the CRM as a dated signal with tool, direction and the evidence string as source. Undated changes are not stored.

4Split the events and stack them

ToolsTheirStackSerperPredictLeads (option)Claude
  • Sort every change into four events: competitor removed, pairing tool added, migration or admin job post, renewal month approaching.
  • Job posts: roles that mention the tool alongside migrate, implement, roll out, admin or owner. The post date is the signal date.
  • Renewal month: first-seen date plus the contract length from step 1, unless a job post or press mention names a start date. The window opens about 90 days before the anniversary.
  • Apply core: tiering. A single signal waits in the CRM. Two dated signals on one account inside a quarter (removed plus a job post, added plus an approaching renewal) earn a call.
  • Apply core: exclusions.

5Find the team that runs the tool

ToolsApolloOcean.ioLinkedIn
  • The buyer is the leader of the function that owns the category: a billing tool belongs to finance, a support tool to the support leader. The CEO only under 50 people or when no function head exists.
  • Add one daily user as champion. The job post from step 4 often names the hiring manager; LinkedIn headlines mentioning the tool name the admin.
  • Apply core: enrichment for the size bands.

6Enrich and verify

ToolsLeadMagicProspeoMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • Apply core: enrichment (one buyer plus one champion per account).
  • Apply core: verification.

7Launch the switch track and the renewal track

ToolsSlackPhoneEmailBisonInstantly (option)HeyReach (option)Claude
  • Tier 1: post a Slack card with the stacked events, the renewal month, the buyer, the LinkedIn URL and the switch offer in one line. The owner calls within 48 hours, opening on the change, not the product.
  • Tiers 2 and 3: two-step email, apply core: copy and send rules. Removed goes to the switch track; added and renewal-due go to the renewal track, as two campaigns, never one sequence.
  • Every email leads with the concrete reason to leave from the inputs. A feature list is not a reason: for a fintech client, displacement copy that sold features alone produced 0 positives from about 4,400 sends.
  • Never name the competitor. Describe the failure mode: "most teams your size hit the ceiling on seat pricing around year two".
  • Draft 3 angle variants per track for email 1 and one threaded nudge for email 2; show them to the user before upload. Stop the sequence for any account that replies, books or shows as an open deal.

8Report by event type

ToolsAttioSlack
  • Weekly: detections, changes, stacked accounts, Tier 1 calls, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings, split by event type. Removed should outperform added; if not, the failure mode in the copy is wrong.
  • Retire any watch-list tool that has not produced a reply in two quarters.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • One detection alone never triggers outreach. Two dated signals inside a quarter, or the account waits.
  • Never name the competitor in an email or call opener; describe the failure mode.
  • Never send displacement copy without the switch offer. No reason to leave in one sentence, no play.
  • Never say you scanned the site or tracked the stack. "Saw the team moved off its old billing tool" is the limit.
  • A renewal month is an estimate unless a source named it. Write "coming up on renewal", not a date you cannot prove.
  • One account, one sequence. Do not run this play and a cold play on the same domain in the same month.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-stack-switch for our support platform, target list is 1,800 mid-market SaaS accounts in the sheet"

  1. Asked for the switch offer (free migration of macros and history plus a 24-month price lock), confirmed EmailBison plus Attio plus Slack. Listed 4 competitors, 3 pairing tools and 2 incumbents; two competitors produced 80 percent of won switches.
  2. Scraped 1,800 homepages: 1,612 ok, 141 blocked, 47 timeouts, blocked rows sent to TheirStack. 1,090 accounts showed a watch-list tool.
  3. Diffed against last month: 38 competitor removals, 64 pairing tool additions, 29 migration or admin job posts, 112 accounts inside 90 days of an estimated renewal.
  4. 71 accounts carried two or more dated signals inside the quarter, 9 removed as customers or open deals.
  5. 62 function owners plus 44 champions found, 97 verified valid, 6 catch-all kept in a separate campaign.
  6. 14 Tier 1 Slack cards posted with renewal months. 48 contacts loaded into the switch track and 41 into the renewal track, both scheduled for Monday.

# Stack Switch

The change is the signal, not the state. A company running a competitor is a cold account, a company that dropped that competitor last month has an open window, and a company that just added the tool people pair with yours is heading toward a renewal decision on a date you can estimate. Two dated stack signals inside a quarter earn a call, and the email leads with a reason to leave, never a feature list.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it. Read the user's ICP file if one exists.
2. The target list as a CSV or CRM view, one domain per row. This play watches a list; it does not build one.
3. Stack answers from core section 1.
4. The switch offer in one sentence: a concrete reason to leave an incumbent (done-for-you migration, a price or term guarantee, a capability the incumbent lacks that they already pay for elsewhere). Features only: stop and build the offer first.

## Steps

### 1. Write down the tools that matter
Tools: Claude, Attio, Google Sheets
Flow:
- Three tool lists | Competitors you replace, pairing tools people adopt next to your product, renewal-heavy incumbents in your category | with: Claude, Google Sheets | chips: competitor, pairing, incumbent
- Won-deal read | Which switches actually closed in the last two years, from the CRM and call notes | with: Attio | tag: evidence first
= Watch list | One row per tool with category, typical contract length and a fingerprint hint | chips: tool, category, contract months, fingerprint

- Write three lists in a sheet: competitors you displace, pairing tools (what a buyer adopts right before or after your product), and renewal-heavy incumbents on annual contracts.
- Pull two years of won deals from the CRM and note the previous tool on each. Switches that actually closed go to the top; the rest are dropped.
- For each tool, record its category, the usual contract length (12 months when nobody knows), and a fingerprint hint: a script domain, a cookie name, a CSS prefix or a widget path.

### 2. Detect the stack, free first
Tools: Firecrawl, Claude, BuiltWith (option), TheirStack (option)
Flow:
- Homepage scrape | Fetch the HTML and every script src on the homepage and one product page, 20 in parallel | with: Firecrawl | chips: script domains, meta generator, cookies, widget paths | tag: free source
- Fingerprint match | Pattern match against the watch list, then a cheap model pass lists every vendor it can see | with: Claude | chips: tool, evidence string, fetch status
- Paid fallback | Blocked or timed-out domains and anything invisible in HTML go to a paid lookup | with: BuiltWith, TheirStack | tag: paid

- Fetch the homepage HTML plus one product page per domain. Save the raw HTML so reruns cost nothing.
- Match the watch list fingerprints against script sources, meta tags, cookie names and widget paths; store the matched string as evidence. Then run a cheap model over the same HTML asking which vendors the site runs, so unlisted tools still land in the snapshot.
- Mark each fetch ok, blocked or timeout. Blocked domains and categories that leave no trace in HTML (back-office tools) go to BuiltWith or TheirStack if the user has either; otherwise they stay flagged `no detection` and are watched through job posts only.

### 3. Snapshot and diff
Tools: Google Sheets, Attio, Claude
Flow:
- Dated snapshot | Every detection stored with first-seen and last-seen, one row per domain and tool | with: Google Sheets, Attio | chips: domain, tool, first seen, last seen, source
= Monthly diff | Compare against the previous snapshot; a tool missing for two runs is removed, a new tool is added | with: Claude | chips: removed, added, unchanged | tag: the change is the signal

- Store every detection with first-seen and last-seen dates. Never overwrite; a detection that stops appearing keeps its last-seen date, which is the signal.
- Rerun monthly and diff against the previous snapshot. Absent for two consecutive runs counts as removed (one miss is usually a page change). Present for the first time counts as added.
- Write each change to the CRM as a dated signal with tool, direction and the evidence string as source. Undated changes are not stored.

### 4. Split the events and stack them
Tools: TheirStack, Serper, PredictLeads (option), Claude
Flow:
- Competitor removed | Open window, highest priority, the switch has already started | tag: priority 1
- Pairing tool added | A buyer is assembling the stack your product sits in | tag: priority 2
- Migration or admin job post | A role to migrate, implement or administer the category, dated by post date | with: TheirStack, Serper, PredictLeads | tag: priority 2
= Renewal month | From the first-seen date plus contract norms, or named in a job post; two dated signals in one quarter earn a call | with: Claude | chips: renewal month, signal count | tag: core: tiering

- Sort every change into four events: competitor removed, pairing tool added, migration or admin job post, renewal month approaching.
- Job posts: roles that mention the tool alongside migrate, implement, roll out, admin or owner. The post date is the signal date.
- Renewal month: first-seen date plus the contract length from step 1, unless a job post or press mention names a start date. The window opens about 90 days before the anniversary.
- Apply core: tiering. A single signal waits in the CRM. Two dated signals on one account inside a quarter (removed plus a job post, added plus an approaching renewal) earn a call.
- Apply core: exclusions.

### 5. Find the team that runs the tool
Tools: Apollo, Ocean.io, LinkedIn
Flow:
- Function owner | The leader of the function that owns the category, not the CEO | with: Apollo, Ocean.io | chips: title, function, seniority
- Daily user | One person who runs the tool day to day, from headlines and the job post itself | with: LinkedIn | tag: champion

- The buyer is the leader of the function that owns the category: a billing tool belongs to finance, a support tool to the support leader. The CEO only under 50 people or when no function head exists.
- Add one daily user as champion. The job post from step 4 often names the hiring manager; LinkedIn headlines mentioning the tool name the admin.
- Apply core: enrichment for the size bands.

### 6. Enrich and verify
Tools: LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- Email waterfall | Each vendor only sees the contacts the one before it missed | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo | tag: core: enrichment
- Two-pass verification | Valid to the main campaign, catch-all to its own campaign | with: MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: verification

- Apply core: enrichment (one buyer plus one champion per account).
- Apply core: verification.

### 7. Launch the switch track and the renewal track
Tools: Slack, Phone, EmailBison, Instantly (option), HeyReach (option), Claude
Flow:
- Tier 1 call | Slack card with the event, the renewal month, the buyer and the reason to leave; the owner calls before the renewal window closes | with: Slack, Phone | chips: event, renewal month, buyer, offer | tag: high touch
- Switch track | Removed accounts: noticed the switch, where that tool usually breaks at their stage, what moving looks like | with: Claude, EmailBison, Instantly | chips: {CATEGORY}, {FAILURE_MODE}, {OFFER}
- Renewal track | Added and renewal accounts: lands 60 to 90 days ahead of the renewal month with the same shape | with: Claude, EmailBison, HeyReach | chips: {CATEGORY}, {RENEWAL_MONTH}, {OFFER} | tag: send

- Tier 1: post a Slack card with the stacked events, the renewal month, the buyer, the LinkedIn URL and the switch offer in one line. The owner calls within 48 hours, opening on the change, not the product.
- Tiers 2 and 3: two-step email, apply core: copy and send rules. Removed goes to the switch track; added and renewal-due go to the renewal track, as two campaigns, never one sequence.
- Every email leads with the concrete reason to leave from the inputs. A feature list is not a reason: for a fintech client, displacement copy that sold features alone produced 0 positives from about 4,400 sends.
- Never name the competitor. Describe the failure mode: "most teams your size hit the ceiling on seat pricing around year two".
- Draft 3 angle variants per track for email 1 and one threaded nudge for email 2; show them to the user before upload. Stop the sequence for any account that replies, books or shows as an open deal.

### 8. Report by event type
Tools: Attio, Slack
Flow:
= Weekly read | Changes detected, stacked accounts, Tier 1 calls, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings, split by removed, added, job post, renewal | with: Attio, Slack

- Weekly: detections, changes, stacked accounts, Tier 1 calls, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings, split by event type. Removed should outperform added; if not, the failure mode in the copy is wrong.
- Retire any watch-list tool that has not produced a reply in two quarters.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- One detection alone never triggers outreach. Two dated signals inside a quarter, or the account waits.
- Never name the competitor in an email or call opener; describe the failure mode.
- Never send displacement copy without the switch offer. No reason to leave in one sentence, no play.
- Never say you scanned the site or tracked the stack. "Saw the team moved off its old billing tool" is the limit.
- A renewal month is an estimate unless a source named it. Write "coming up on renewal", not a date you cannot prove.
- One account, one sequence. Do not run this play and a cold play on the same domain in the same month.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-stack-switch for our support platform, target list is 1,800 mid-market SaaS accounts in the sheet"

1. Asked for the switch offer (free migration of macros and history plus a 24-month price lock), confirmed EmailBison plus Attio plus Slack. Listed 4 competitors, 3 pairing tools and 2 incumbents; two competitors produced 80 percent of won switches.
2. Scraped 1,800 homepages: 1,612 ok, 141 blocked, 47 timeouts, blocked rows sent to TheirStack. 1,090 accounts showed a watch-list tool.
3. Diffed against last month: 38 competitor removals, 64 pairing tool additions, 29 migration or admin job posts, 112 accounts inside 90 days of an estimated renewal.
4. 71 accounts carried two or more dated signals inside the quarter, 9 removed as customers or open deals.
5. 62 function owners plus 44 champions found, 97 verified valid, 6 catch-all kept in a separate campaign.
6. 14 Tier 1 Slack cards posted with renewal months. 48 contacts loaded into the switch track and 41 into the renewal track, both scheduled for Monday.

  • The top guess goes into {COMPETITOR}; store the runners-up and the confidence. Below the floor the user sets (40 percent to start) the account waits.

# Competitor Complaints

A competitor's unhappy customers already feel the problem you fix, and their public reviews tell you the exact words they use for it. Find the companies running that competitor, match each to the complaint its segment keeps raising, and pitch the fix with a concrete reason to leave. Where no tool is detectable, a model infers the likely competitor from the website and the email names it as a question, which earns a reply even when the guess misses.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it.
2. Three to six named competitors, and which of them you have won deals from.
3. Stack answers from core section 1, plus whether the user has G2 Buyer Intent.
4. The switch offer in one sentence with one proof point: a concrete reason to leave. Features only: build the offer first.

## Steps

### 1. Mine the reviews into complaint themes
Tools: G2, Capterra, Firecrawl, Apify, Claude, Google Sheets
Flow:
- Pull the low-star reviews | 1 to 3 star reviews plus the dislikes section of every review, for each named competitor | with: G2, Capterra, Firecrawl, Apify | chips: competitor, stars, dislikes, reviewer title, company size, date
- Cluster into themes | A model groups the complaints into themes with a frequency count and a one-line description in plain words, no quotes | with: Claude | chips: theme, frequency, segment, one-liner
= Keep what you fix | A theme stays only when the offer is a real fix with a proof point; the rest are dropped | with: Claude, Google Sheets | tag: proof or drop

- Name three to six competitors; the ones you have won deals from go first.
- Pull every 1 to 3 star review and every dislikes section from G2 and Capterra, with Firecrawl or an Apify actor. Keep reviewer title, size band, industry and date on each row.
- A model clusters the complaints into themes, each with a frequency, the segments raising it most, and a one-line description in buyer words.
- Keep only themes the offer fixes with a number or a named outcome as proof. Drop the rest however often they appear; three to five survive.

### 2. Build the competitor's user list
Tools: BuiltWith, TheirStack, G2, Capterra, Firecrawl, Serper, Google Sheets
Flow:
- Technographics | Domains where the competitor's tool is detectable, with the evidence string | with: BuiltWith, TheirStack | chips: domain, tool, evidence, last seen | tag: confirmed
- Reviewers and logos | Companies named on reviews, plus customer logo and case-study pages scraped from the competitor's own site | with: G2, Capterra, Firecrawl | chips: company, source URL, review date | tag: confirmed
- Footprints | Job posts naming the tool and powered-by lines found through search | with: Serper | chips: job post, powered by, post date | tag: likely
= One row per domain | Merged and deduped, each tagged confirmed, likely or unknown with its source | with: Google Sheets | chips: confirmed, likely, unknown, source

- Apply core: lists. Pull from at least two sources: technographics where the tool leaves a fingerprint, reviewer companies named on reviews, logo and case-study pages on the competitor's site, job posts and "powered by" footers.
- Tag each domain confirmed when a technographic match, a logo page or a named review backs it, likely when only a job post or footprint does, unknown otherwise, with source and date on every row.
- This is the state, not the change. Stack Switch watches a tool removed or added; this play works whoever runs the competitor today.

### 3. Add G2 buyer intent, if the user has it
Tools: G2 (option), Google Sheets
Flow:
= Intent rows | Companies that viewed the competitor's profile, your profile or a comparison page in the last 30 days, added as confirmed intent | with: G2, Google Sheets | chips: profile view, comparison view, pricing view, last 30 days | tag: skip without G2

- Only with a G2 Buyer Intent subscription. Export companies that viewed the competitor's profile, your profile or a comparison page in the last 30 days as confirmed intent rows with page type and date; comparison and pricing views outrank category views.
- No subscription: log the step as skipped and move on; the play runs on steps 2 and 4.

### 4. The poke lane: infer the competitor
Tools: Firecrawl, Claude, Google Sheets
Flow:
- Read the website | Homepage, product, integrations and careers pages for every target-segment account with no detected tool | with: Firecrawl | chips: integrations page, pricing page, careers page
= Three likely competitors | A model lists the three most likely tools in use with a confidence each; the top one becomes {COMPETITOR} on the row | with: Claude, Google Sheets | chips: {COMPETITOR}, confidence, runner-up, reasoning | tag: inference

- For every target-segment account where step 2 found nothing, fetch the homepage, product, integrations and careers pages.
- A model names the three most likely competitors in use, each with a confidence and one line of reasoning: an integration listed, a tool in a job post, the norm for that size and industry.
- The top guess goes into `{COMPETITOR}`; store the runners-up and the confidence. Below the floor the user sets (40 percent to start) the account waits.
- The guess need not be right. The email asks a question, so a wrong guess earns a correction, and a correction is a reply.

### 5. Qualify and exclude
Tools: Ocean.io, Apollo, Claude, Attio, HubSpot (option)
Flow:
- ICP check | Headcount, industry and location, then a yes or no verdict from the company website | with: Ocean.io, Apollo, Claude | tag: core: exclusions
- Competitor-side removals | Competitor staff, partners, resellers and agencies dropped | with: Claude, Apollo | chips: staff, partner, agency, reseller
= Write to the CRM | Competitor, confidence, complaint theme, lane and source URL stored on every record | with: Attio, HubSpot | chips: competitor, confidence, theme, lane, source URL

- Qualify every account against the written ICP: headcount, industry, location, and a yes or no verdict from its website.
- Apply core: exclusions. Then remove the competitor's staff, partners, resellers and implementing agencies; a partner page or headline gives them away.
- Assign each account the theme its segment raises most in step 1. Store competitor, confidence, theme, lane and source URL on the CRM record. The buyer is the function leader who owns the category, per core: enrichment.

### 6. Enrich and verify
Tools: LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- Email waterfall | Each vendor only sees the contacts the one before it missed | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo | tag: core: enrichment
- Two-pass verification | Valid to the main campaign, catch-all to its own campaign | with: MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: verification

- Apply core: enrichment.
- Apply core: verification.

### 7. Tier and write
Tools: Claude, Slack, Attio
Flow:
- Tier 1 | Confirmed user at an ICP account whose segment matches a top complaint theme, or any intent row: Slack alert, switch email, LinkedIn connection | with: Slack, Claude | chips: confirmed, theme match, intent | tag: high touch
- Tier 2 | Likely user at an ICP account: switch email plus LinkedIn connection | with: Claude | tag: at volume
- Tier 3 | Poke lane: the question variant, email only | with: Claude | chips: {COMPETITOR}, {COMPLAINT} | tag: inference
= Two variants | The switch variant leads with the complaint and a reason to leave; the poke variant asks a question and offers to send something | with: Claude, Attio | chips: {COMPLAINT}, {OFFER}, {PROOF}, {COMPETITOR}

- Apply core: copy and send rules.
- Tier 1: a confirmed user whose segment matches a top complaint theme, plus every intent row. Slack alert, switch email, LinkedIn connection. Tier 2: a likely user, switch email plus connection. Tier 3: the poke lane, email only.
- Switch variant: open on the complaint in the prospect's own kind of words. Never the competitor's name in the subject line, never a quoted reviewer. Then a concrete reason to leave: migration done for them, a parallel run, a contract buyout, a fixed timeline. One proof point, one direct ask. Never a feature list; feature-only displacement copy produced 0 positives from about 4,400 sends.
- Poke variant: a question, not a claim. "Most teams on {COMPETITOR} end up <complaint>, is that the case for you?" Then offer to send something, a one-page comparison or a migration checklist, never a meeting.
- Draft one switch variant per kept theme, one poke variant and a threaded nudge for email 2; show the user before upload.

### 8. Launch and report by competitor and theme
Tools: EmailBison, Instantly (option), HeyReach, Attio, Slack
Flow:
- Launch one campaign per lane | Confirmed, likely and poke each from its own inbox set, so results read on their own; stop on a reply, a booking or an open deal | with: EmailBison, Instantly, HeyReach | tag: send
= Weekly read | Sent, reply rate, interested and meetings split by competitor, by theme and by lane confirmed, likely and poke | with: Attio, Slack | chips: competitor, theme, confirmed, likely, poke

- Upload leads with `{COMPETITOR}`, `{COMPLAINT}`, `{OFFER}` and `{PROOF}`. One campaign per lane from its own inbox set; LinkedIn connections go with email 1.
- Weekly: sent, reply rate, interested, meetings, split by competitor, theme and lane. Confirmed should beat poke; if poke keeps pace, the inference is good and the lane can scale. A theme with no replies after 300 sends is retired. Corrected poke guesses become confirmed rows.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- Never disparage a competitor or quote a reviewer. Themes are plain buyer words; review text never leaves the raw file.
- A theme must be something you fix, with proof. No proof point, no theme.
- A switch offer is required. No concrete reason to leave in one sentence, no play.
- The competitor's name never appears in a subject line, and in the body only in the poke variant, as a question.
- Never say you read their reviews or scanned their site. A poke guess is never written as fact.
- Do not run this play and Stack Switch on the same domain in the same month.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-competitor-complaints for our support platform, mid-market SaaS 100 to 1,000 people in the US and UK, four incumbents"

1. Asked for the switch offer (free migration of macros and ticket history plus a 60-day parallel run) and its proof; confirmed BuiltWith, EmailBison, HeyReach, Attio and Slack. No G2 intent, step 3 skipped.
2. 1,840 low-star reviews across four competitors; 11 themes, 4 survived the proof check: slow vendor support, seat pricing jumps at renewal, reporting that needs an analyst, a six-week migration.
3. 612 domains confirmed by BuiltWith, 94 reviewer companies, 141 from logo pages, 230 likely from job posts; 918 after dedupe.
4. Poke lane: 1,400 accounts with no detected tool, a top competitor above the floor on 1,090.
5. ICP check passed 1,560 of 2,008; 38 removed as competitor staff, partners or agencies, 61 as customers, open deals or in sequence.
6. 1,380 function owners, 1,261 verified valid, 71 catch-all in separate campaigns.
7. 402 Tier 1, 180 Tier 2, 679 Tier 3 poke rows; four switch variants and one poke variant approved.
8. Week three: confirmed 6.1 percent reply, likely 4.2, poke 5.4 with 38 corrected guesses now confirmed; seat pricing leads, reporting retired.
