Play 20 · Third-party signals

Public Record Flags

Trigger A public dataset that flags a problem your offer fixes
Outcome A dated, verifiable reason to write this week
Channels email, call

Give this to your agent 
Install the Pipeline.tech "Public Record Flags" outbound play. Fetch
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/core/SKILL.md and
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/plays/public-record-flags/SKILL.md,
save them as ~/.claude/skills/pipeline-outbound-core/SKILL.md and
~/.claude/skills/pipeline-public-record-flags/SKILL.md, then run /pipeline-public-record-flags.
Paste into Claude Code. It installs the shared core rules plus this play, then runs it as /pipeline-public-record-flags.
Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

Government and regulator datasets hold dated, verifiable facts about the companies you sell to: quality scores, inspection results, permits, licenses, filing fields, registry attributes like asset size or branch count. No data vendor packages them, so almost nobody emails on them, and the buyer can check every word against the record. Find the field that says a prospect has the problem you fix, and write to the person who owns it.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1 and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The ICP in one paragraph: industry, size band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it.
  2. The fact, as one sentence a buyer would recognise: "hospitals worse than the national average on an infection measure". If unknown, step 1 works it out.
  3. Stack answers from core section 1, plus any dataset already in mind.
  4. The offer in one sentence and one proof point.

Steps

1Name the fact and map the source

ToolsClaudeSerperPublic datasets
  • Two kinds of fact: one that qualifies (bed count, asset size, branch count) and one that creates urgency (worse than a benchmark, a failed inspection, a new permit). Most runs need one of each.
  • Find the public source: health agencies publish quality measures per facility, financial regulators publish call reports, telecom regulators publish coverage filings, cities publish permits. Locate the dataset page, API docs and bulk download.
  • Record the join key (facility id, legal name, domain, address) and the refresh cadence.
  • Read ten raw records by hand first; the nursing home file is not the hospital file.

2Pull broad on a schedule

ToolsPublic datasetsFirecrawlApify (option)CSV
  • Pull the whole dataset, not a filtered slice; filter in step 4. Public APIs and bulk downloads first, Firecrawl or an Apify scraper for portals with no download, throttled.
  • Zip code beats city: spellings vary across public files, zips join cleanly and roll up to any region.
  • Keep the raw file untouched with a source column and the pull date; every claim in step 6 traces to a row in it. Schedule the pull to the cadence from step 1.

3Resolve each record to a company

ToolsClaudeSerperOcean.ioApolloGoogle Sheets
  • Public records name legal entities, not brands. Strip Inc and LLC, expand abbreviations, lowercase. Where two files share an official id, join on it and skip names.
  • Match in order: id, then normalized name plus address (zip, not city), then domain. A search on legal name plus city finds the website for roughly nine in ten.
  • High confidence routes on, medium goes to a review sheet the user clears weekly, low is dropped and logged. Resolve to the entity that makes the buying decision; keep the parent as a column.

4Build the flag and the tier

ToolsClaudeGoogle SheetsAttio
  • Count or derive. Count: one flag per measure where the record is worse than its benchmark, summed per account. Derive: where the record has no benchmark, read the situation from the fields. Branch count means fragmented support, fiber build-out means a support volume spike, "Federal" in a credit union's name means audit trail pressure.
  • Bucket: none, 1 to 2, 3 to 4, 5 plus, or equivalent thresholds for a derived situation. Tone per bucket: efficiency, proactive, pattern, urgent. Urgent means a direct ask, never a scare.
  • Store flag count, flagged measures, record date and source URL on the account. Apply core: tiering.

5Qualify, pick the owner, enrich and verify

ToolsClaudeOcean.ioApolloLeadMagicProspeoMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • Apply core: exclusions. The record often carries the size field (beds, assets, subscribers); drop everything under the ICP threshold before any paid lookup.
  • The owner is the function the record points at: an infection measure points at operations and quality (COO, VP Operations, CNO, compliance), a branch count at customer operations. Never the registrant on the filing. Add one peer.
  • Apply core: enrichment. Apply core: verification.

6Write the finding

ToolsClaude
  • Apply core: copy and send rules. Under 50 words.
  • Lead with the fact as merge fields: "{HOSPITAL} shows worse than the national average on {FLAG_COUNT} infection measures in the latest public quality file." No adjectives.
  • Follow with a diagnostic question in the tier's tone, the kind a technician asks before quoting a job, not a stat dump.
  • One variant asks whether the read is right: "Reading that right, or is the public file behind?"
  • Audit before upload: a model compares every sentence to the raw row. Five flags means five rows; the benchmark label is the dataset's own, never a paraphrase. Cut anything the record does not say; never imply causation.
  • Email 2 names the source and offers the record. One set per tier, approved by the user before upload.

7Alert Tier 1, launch Tiers 2 and 3

ToolsSlackPhoneEmailBisonInstantly (option)Attio
  • Tier 1: 5 plus flags, or a single record that licenses action alone (a failed inspection, a new permit). Slack card with the account, flagged measures, verbatim excerpt, source URL, owner and a one-line opener; the owner calls within 48 hours.
  • Tier 2 and 3: upload with {RECORD_FACT}, {FLAG_COUNT}, {SIZE_FIELD}, {STATE}, one campaign per tier from this play's own inbox set.
  • Write every account to the CRM with signal type public record, flag count, record date and source URL. Stop on a reply, a booking, an open deal, or a cleared flag.

8Report and refresh

ToolsAttioSlackPublic datasets
  • Weekly: records pulled, match rate, flagged share, Tier 1 count, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings, split by tier and record type.
  • Refresh when the source refreshes; a cleared flag stops the sequence, a new flag re-enters at step 4.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • Never write anything the record does not say. Quote the dataset's own label; never upgrade it to "failing" or "dangerous".
  • Never imply the buyer was watched. The record is public; name the source when asked.
  • A record older than its refresh cycle comes out of the copy.
  • Medium and low confidence matches never send; a wrong-entity email about a quality flag does real harm.
  • One account, one sequence, tone set by tier. Five flags earn a call, not five emails.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-public-record-flags for our healthcare consent software, ICP is US hospitals with 50 or more beds, COO or VP Operations"

  1. Named the fact: worse than the national average on any of five infection measures or the complications file; join key the facility id; quarterly refresh.
  2. Pulled all three files whole: 6,103 hospitals with bed counts, 172,476 infection rows, 95,820 complication rows.
  3. Joined on facility id; websites by search on legal name plus city, 88% hit rate.
  4. 776 hospitals flagged (23%), 202 high-risk at 5 plus, about 200 at 3 to 4, about 374 at 1 to 2.
  5. Dropped under 50 beds, customers and open deals. COO or VP Operations plus one compliance peer, verified.
  6. Four copy sets by tier, under 50 words, audited against the record.
  7. 202 Tier 1 Slack cards queued for calls over four weeks; Tiers 2 and 3 loaded to "Public Record Flags, hospitals" with {HOSPITAL}, {FLAG_COUNT}, {BEDS}, {STATE}.

A second run for a customer-support software client derived the flag from registries: credit union call reports above one billion in assets and telecom coverage filings for regional ISPs gave 888 accounts, 448 credit unions and 440 ISPs, 130 of them fiber providers in growth mode. Branch count read as fragmented support, fiber build-out as a volume spike, each with its own diagnostic opener.

# Public Record Flags

Government and regulator datasets hold dated, verifiable facts about the companies you sell to: quality scores, inspection results, permits, licenses, filing fields, registry attributes like asset size or branch count. No data vendor packages them, so almost nobody emails on them, and the buyer can check every word against the record. Find the field that says a prospect has the problem you fix, and write to the person who owns it.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1 and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The ICP in one paragraph: industry, size band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it.
2. The fact, as one sentence a buyer would recognise: "hospitals worse than the national average on an infection measure". If unknown, step 1 works it out.
3. Stack answers from core section 1, plus any dataset already in mind.
4. The offer in one sentence and one proof point.

## Steps

### 1. Name the fact and map the source
Tools: Claude, Serper, Public datasets
Flow:
- The qualifying fact | One field or threshold that means they have the problem: a quality flag, an inspection result, a permit, a license, a registry attribute | with: Claude | chips: field, threshold, qualifies or urgency
- The source map | The public dataset that holds the field, its join key and how often it refreshes | with: Serper, Public datasets | chips: dataset, join key, refresh cadence, bulk or API
= Source sheet | One row per record type with field, source URL, join key, cadence and whether it licenses action alone | with: Google Sheets

- Two kinds of fact: one that qualifies (bed count, asset size, branch count) and one that creates urgency (worse than a benchmark, a failed inspection, a new permit). Most runs need one of each.
- Find the public source: health agencies publish quality measures per facility, financial regulators publish call reports, telecom regulators publish coverage filings, cities publish permits. Locate the dataset page, API docs and bulk download.
- Record the join key (facility id, legal name, domain, address) and the refresh cadence.
- Read ten raw records by hand first; the nursing home file is not the hospital file.

### 2. Pull broad on a schedule
Tools: Public datasets, Firecrawl, Apify (option), CSV
Flow:
- APIs and bulk files | Public APIs and bulk downloads first, the whole country at once, filtered later | with: Public datasets, CSV | chips: full pull, raw file kept, source column | tag: free source
- Portals | Firecrawl or a scraper for portals with no download; throttled, no CAPTCHA fights | with: Firecrawl, Apify | tag: fill the gaps
= Raw snapshot | One file per source per pull with the pull date in the name; zip code beats city for geography | chips: pull date, record id, raw fields

- Pull the whole dataset, not a filtered slice; filter in step 4. Public APIs and bulk downloads first, Firecrawl or an Apify scraper for portals with no download, throttled.
- Zip code beats city: spellings vary across public files, zips join cleanly and roll up to any region.
- Keep the raw file untouched with a `source` column and the pull date; every claim in step 6 traces to a row in it. Schedule the pull to the cadence from step 1.

### 3. Resolve each record to a company
Tools: Claude, Serper, Ocean.io, Apollo, Google Sheets
Flow:
- Normalize | Strip suffixes, expand abbreviations, lowercase; join on the official id where two public files share one | with: Claude | chips: legal name, facility id, address
- Match ladder | Id first, then name plus address, then domain; website found by search where the record has none | with: Serper, Ocean.io, Apollo | chips: high, medium, low
= Confidence routing | High routes automatically, medium goes to a review sheet, low is dropped and logged | with: Google Sheets | chips: auto, review, drop

- Public records name legal entities, not brands. Strip Inc and LLC, expand abbreviations, lowercase. Where two files share an official id, join on it and skip names.
- Match in order: id, then normalized name plus address (zip, not city), then domain. A search on legal name plus city finds the website for roughly nine in ten.
- High confidence routes on, medium goes to a review sheet the user clears weekly, low is dropped and logged. Resolve to the entity that makes the buying decision; keep the parent as a column.

### 4. Build the flag and the tier
Tools: Claude, Google Sheets, Attio
Flow:
- Flag count | One flag per measure where the record is worse than the benchmark, summed per account | with: Google Sheets | chips: none, 1 to 2, 3 to 4, 5 plus
- Derived situation | Where the record has no benchmark, read the situation from the fields | with: Claude | chips: fragmented support, volume spike, audit pressure
= Tier and tone | The bucket sets the tier and the tone: efficiency, proactive, pattern, urgent | with: Attio | chips: tier, tone, flagged measures

- Count or derive. Count: one flag per measure where the record is worse than its benchmark, summed per account. Derive: where the record has no benchmark, read the situation from the fields. Branch count means fragmented support, fiber build-out means a support volume spike, "Federal" in a credit union's name means audit trail pressure.
- Bucket: none, 1 to 2, 3 to 4, 5 plus, or equivalent thresholds for a derived situation. Tone per bucket: efficiency, proactive, pattern, urgent. Urgent means a direct ask, never a scare.
- Store flag count, flagged measures, record date and source URL on the account. Apply core: tiering.

### 5. Qualify, pick the owner, enrich and verify
Tools: Claude, Ocean.io, Apollo, LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- Exclusions and ICP | Customers, open deals and active sequences out; size band from the record, ICP verdict from the website | with: Claude, Ocean.io, Apollo | tag: core: exclusions
- Owner of the problem | The function the record points at: quality and operations for a care flag, customer operations for a support situation | with: Apollo, Ocean.io | chips: function head, one peer
- Enrich and verify | Email waterfall, then two verifiers | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: enrichment and verification

- Apply core: exclusions. The record often carries the size field (beds, assets, subscribers); drop everything under the ICP threshold before any paid lookup.
- The owner is the function the record points at: an infection measure points at operations and quality (COO, VP Operations, CNO, compliance), a branch count at customer operations. Never the registrant on the filing. Add one peer.
- Apply core: enrichment. Apply core: verification.

### 6. Write the finding
Tools: Claude
Flow:
- The public fact first | Open on the record field as a merge field, plainly stated, something the buyer can check | with: Claude | chips: {RECORD_FACT}, {FLAG_COUNT}, {SIZE_FIELD}, {STATE}
- Diagnostic question | One question a technician would ask, not a stat dump; one variant asks whether the read is right | with: Claude | tag: variant
= Claim audit | Every sentence checked against the raw record row before upload; anything that overstates is cut | with: Claude | tag: core: copy and send rules

- Apply core: copy and send rules. Under 50 words.
- Lead with the fact as merge fields: "{HOSPITAL} shows worse than the national average on {FLAG_COUNT} infection measures in the latest public quality file." No adjectives.
- Follow with a diagnostic question in the tier's tone, the kind a technician asks before quoting a job, not a stat dump.
- One variant asks whether the read is right: "Reading that right, or is the public file behind?"
- Audit before upload: a model compares every sentence to the raw row. Five flags means five rows; the benchmark label is the dataset's own, never a paraphrase. Cut anything the record does not say; never imply causation.
- Email 2 names the source and offers the record. One set per tier, approved by the user before upload.

### 7. Alert Tier 1, launch Tiers 2 and 3
Tools: Slack, Phone, EmailBison, Instantly (option), Attio
Flow:
- Tier 1 | 5 plus flags, or one record that licenses action alone: a Slack card with the record excerpt, then a call | with: Slack, Phone | chips: account, flagged measures, record excerpt, source URL, owner | tag: high touch
- Tier 2 and 3 | Two-step email from this play's own inbox set, one campaign per tier so each tone reads on its own | with: EmailBison, Instantly, Attio | chips: public record, flag count, record date, source URL | tag: at volume

- Tier 1: 5 plus flags, or a single record that licenses action alone (a failed inspection, a new permit). Slack card with the account, flagged measures, verbatim excerpt, source URL, owner and a one-line opener; the owner calls within 48 hours.
- Tier 2 and 3: upload with `{RECORD_FACT}`, `{FLAG_COUNT}`, `{SIZE_FIELD}`, `{STATE}`, one campaign per tier from this play's own inbox set.
- Write every account to the CRM with signal type `public record`, flag count, record date and source URL. Stop on a reply, a booking, an open deal, or a cleared flag.

### 8. Report and refresh
Tools: Attio, Slack, Public datasets
Flow:
= Weekly read by tier and record type | Records pulled, match rate, flagged share, Tier 1 count, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings; refresh when the source refreshes | with: Attio, Slack, Public datasets

- Weekly: records pulled, match rate, flagged share, Tier 1 count, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings, split by tier and record type.
- Refresh when the source refreshes; a cleared flag stops the sequence, a new flag re-enters at step 4.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- Never write anything the record does not say. Quote the dataset's own label; never upgrade it to "failing" or "dangerous".
- Never imply the buyer was watched. The record is public; name the source when asked.
- A record older than its refresh cycle comes out of the copy.
- Medium and low confidence matches never send; a wrong-entity email about a quality flag does real harm.
- One account, one sequence, tone set by tier. Five flags earn a call, not five emails.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-public-record-flags for our healthcare consent software, ICP is US hospitals with 50 or more beds, COO or VP Operations"

1. Named the fact: worse than the national average on any of five infection measures or the complications file; join key the facility id; quarterly refresh.
2. Pulled all three files whole: 6,103 hospitals with bed counts, 172,476 infection rows, 95,820 complication rows.
3. Joined on facility id; websites by search on legal name plus city, 88% hit rate.
4. 776 hospitals flagged (23%), 202 high-risk at 5 plus, about 200 at 3 to 4, about 374 at 1 to 2.
5. Dropped under 50 beds, customers and open deals. COO or VP Operations plus one compliance peer, verified.
6. Four copy sets by tier, under 50 words, audited against the record.
7. 202 Tier 1 Slack cards queued for calls over four weeks; Tiers 2 and 3 loaded to "Public Record Flags, hospitals" with `{HOSPITAL}`, `{FLAG_COUNT}`, `{BEDS}`, `{STATE}`.

A second run for a customer-support software client derived the flag from registries: credit union call reports above one billion in assets and telecom coverage filings for regional ISPs gave 888 accounts, 448 credit unions and 440 ISPs, 130 of them fiber providers in growth mode. Branch count read as fragmented support, fiber build-out as a volume spike, each with its own diagnostic opener.

Pre-Show Warmup

Trigger An exhibitor list two to four weeks out, an attendee list the day the event ends
Outcome Booth traffic before the show, meetings after
Channels email, linkedin, call

Give this to your agent 
Install the Pipeline.tech "Pre-Show Warmup" outbound play. Fetch
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/core/SKILL.md and
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/plays/pre-show-warmup/SKILL.md,
save them as ~/.claude/skills/pipeline-outbound-core/SKILL.md and

Paste into Claude Code. It installs the shared core rules plus this play, then runs it as /pipeline-pre-show-warmup.
Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

Conference campaigns are among the best outbound campaigns you can run, because the ticket has already qualified the list and the calendar has fixed the timing. Before the show, the exhibitor list is public and every company on it has paid to be in one room on one date, so a meeting at the booth is an easy yes. After the show, the attendee list is warm for about a week, and the first useful email in that window wins.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The event: name, dates, city, site URL, and whether the user is exhibiting, speaking, attending, or selling the warmup to exhibitors.
  2. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it. If the user has an ICP file, read it.
  3. Stack answers from core section 1, plus which attendee sources the user can reach: event app, badge scans, LinkedIn event page, Luma or Zoom export.
  4. The offer in one sentence and one proof point. If the warmup itself is the offer, the proof is a booth traffic number from a previous show.

Steps

1Pick the timing and the audience

ToolsClaude
  • Confirm the window: exhibitors and sponsors two to four weeks out, attendees the day it ends. Most users run both.
  • Decide what is being sold: the user's own offer with the show as the reason to meet, or the warmup itself sold to exhibitors by running their attendee list through this same pipeline.
  • Each audience and timing gets its own campaign, inbox set and report line.

2Pull the exhibitor list before the show

ToolsFirecrawlSerperLinkedIn
  • Crawl the exhibitor, sponsor and agenda pages with Firecrawl. Exhibitor pages carry name, booth and website; sponsor tier is a budget signal.
  • Add speakers and session hosts: senior, certain to be there, and a session title is the easiest opener in outbound.
  • Fill gaps with a Serper search or the event's LinkedIn page. Dedupe by domain, write a source column. Apply core: lists.

3Pull the attendee list the day it ends

ToolsEvent appLinkedInLumaZoomCSV
  • Pull the list the evening the event ends. Export the directory from the event app, add the badge scans, and have whoever worked the booth mark everyone they spoke to.
  • Add LinkedIn event page attendees and any Luma or Zoom export from side events; Zoom shows who registered and who joined.
  • Mark every row met in person, attended, or registered no-show; met in person wins any tie. This split drives tiering, copy and the report.

4Score fit before spending a credit

ToolsClaudeOcean.ioApolloAttio
  • Apply core: exclusions. Add one more: vendors selling to the same crowd are warmup prospects, never main-offer prospects.
  • Enrich headcount, industry and location. A cheap model reads each website, gives a yes or no ICP verdict with one reason, and marks whether the person buys, uses or signs off.
  • Drop the no's before any paid lookup. An attendee list looks warm, which is why it burns credits: a 4K badge list is often 600 real prospects.

5Find the person, enrich and verify

ToolsLinkedInApolloOcean.ioLeadMagicProspeoMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • For exhibitors, find the booth owner: a director or head of field marketing, events or trade shows, or the GTM leader under 200 people. If the warmup is the offer, that person is the buyer; otherwise pick the decision maker by company size and keep them as champion.
  • For attendees, the person on the list is the contact. Confirm title on LinkedIn; badge data is often a job behind.
  • Apply core: enrichment.
  • Apply core: verification.

6Tier and alert

ToolsSlackAttioLinkedInPhone
  • Tier 1 is anyone met in person, plus every speaker and exhibitor that fits. Post a Slack card with name, company, where you met them or their session, LinkedIn URL and a one-line opener. The owner connects on LinkedIn and calls within two days.
  • Tier 2 is attended, Tier 3 is registered no-show, both with ICP fit; both go to the two-step email in step 7 within two days.
  • Write every account to the CRM with signal type event, event name, attendance state, tier and date. Attendance state stays on the record as a scoring input for later plays.

7Write and launch

ToolsClaudeEmailBisonInstantly (option)HeyReach
  • Apply core: copy and send rules.
  • Pre-show: you are both going to be there, so ask for 15 minutes at their booth or a coffee nearby, with a day and a time. For exhibitors buying the warmup, say plainly that you run their attendee list before the show.
  • Post-show: open on the event and give one useful thing from it: a session takeaway, the recording, a short list of who was there in their segment. No-shows get the recording and a softer ask.
  • Budget frame for manufacturers past a few million in revenue: flights, booth, hotel and a rep's week versus the same lead flow from a two-week campaign. Outbound sits next to the show, not instead of it.
  • Draft three angle variants for email 1 and one threaded nudge for email 2 per audience; show the user before upload. Upload with {EVENT}, {BOOTH}, {DAY}, {SESSION}, {TAKEAWAY}.
  • Pre-show sends stop the day the show opens; post-show sends stop a week after it ends. Stop any contact that replies, books or shows as an open deal.

8Report

ToolsAttioSlack
  • Weekly: pulled, ICP-fit rate, Tier 1 count, booth meetings, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings after. Split by audience (exhibitor, attendee, no-show) and timing (pre, post), and compare to the cold baseline.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • An attendee list is stale a week after the event ends. If it cannot be worked within two days, run it as a cold play with the event as a dated signal.
  • Never open with "great to meet you", "hope you enjoyed the show" or a line about watching their booth. Every opener carries one useful thing from the event.
  • Score fit before any paid lookup. A badge scan is attendance, not fit.
  • Only work lists you collected yourself or the organizer gave you. A purchased attendee list is not this play.
  • One person, one sequence. Someone who is both an exhibitor and an attendee is an exhibitor. Do not run this play and a cold play on the same domain in the same month.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-pre-show-warmup for our outbound agency, we are exhibiting at a 3-day building materials show in Dallas in three weeks, ICP is manufacturers 50 to 500 people in the US"

  1. Set both windows, confirmed EmailBison, HeyReach, Attio and Slack, captured offer and proof.
  2. Firecrawl pulled 412 exhibitors and 38 speakers; 388 companies after dedupe by domain.
  3. 171 passed the ICP read, 9 removed as customers, open deals or same-crowd vendors.
  4. 162 booth owners found, 118 with a GTM leader added as champion; 251 verified valid, 14 catch-all in their own campaign.
  5. 67 Tier 1 Slack cards (38 speakers, 29 sponsor-tier exhibitors), 11 booth meetings booked. 184 contacts loaded into "Pre-show, Dallas", stopped the morning the show opened.
  6. Show closed: 2,940 attendees from the app, 212 badge scans, 61 met in person. 644 passed the ICP read. 61 Tier 1 cards, 583 loaded into "Post-show, Dallas" within two days. Week one: 14 meetings after the show against 11 at the booth.
The skill fileplays/pre-show-warmup/SKILL.mdExactly what gets installed. Copy it, save it as ~/.claude/skills/pipeline-pre-show-warmup/SKILL.md next to the core skill, and run /pipeline-pre-show-warmup.

# Pre-Show Warmup

Conference campaigns are among the best outbound campaigns you can run, because the ticket has already qualified the list and the calendar has fixed the timing. Before the show, the exhibitor list is public and every company on it has paid to be in one room on one date, so a meeting at the booth is an easy yes. After the show, the attendee list is warm for about a week, and the first useful email in that window wins.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The event: name, dates, city, site URL, and whether the user is exhibiting, speaking, attending, or selling the warmup to exhibitors.
2. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it. If the user has an ICP file, read it.
3. Stack answers from core section 1, plus which attendee sources the user can reach: event app, badge scans, LinkedIn event page, Luma or Zoom export.
4. The offer in one sentence and one proof point. If the warmup itself is the offer, the proof is a booth traffic number from a previous show.

## Steps

### 1. Pick the timing and the audience
Tools: Claude
Flow:
- Before the show | Two to four weeks out: exhibitors and sponsors, meetings booked at the booth | with: Claude | chips: exhibitor, sponsor, pre
- After the show | The day it ends, worked within two days, stale after a week: attendees, speakers, no-shows | with: Claude | chips: met in person, attended, no-show, post
= One campaign per audience and timing | Exhibitor pre-show and attendee post-show never share an inbox set or a report line | chips: audience, timing, inbox set

- Confirm the window: exhibitors and sponsors two to four weeks out, attendees the day it ends. Most users run both.
- Decide what is being sold: the user's own offer with the show as the reason to meet, or the warmup itself sold to exhibitors by running their attendee list through this same pipeline.
- Each audience and timing gets its own campaign, inbox set and report line.

### 2. Pull the exhibitor list before the show
Tools: Firecrawl, Serper, LinkedIn
Flow:
- Exhibitor and sponsor pages | The event site lists every exhibitor with name, booth number and website, free and public | with: Firecrawl | chips: company, website, booth, sponsor tier | tag: free source
- Speakers and hosts | The agenda names speakers and session hosts, who are senior and easy to open on | with: Firecrawl, Serper | chips: name, title, company, session
= One row per company | Deduped by domain, with a source column and the event name and dates | chips: company, domain, booth, sponsor tier, source URL

- Crawl the exhibitor, sponsor and agenda pages with Firecrawl. Exhibitor pages carry name, booth and website; sponsor tier is a budget signal.
- Add speakers and session hosts: senior, certain to be there, and a session title is the easiest opener in outbound.
- Fill gaps with a Serper search or the event's LinkedIn page. Dedupe by domain, write a `source` column. Apply core: lists.

### 3. Pull the attendee list the day it ends
Tools: Event app, LinkedIn, Luma, Zoom, CSV
Flow:
- Event app and badge scans | Attendee directory export plus everyone the booth scanned, pulled the evening the show closes | with: Event app, CSV | chips: name, company, title, scanned, met in person
- LinkedIn event page | Attendees who marked themselves going, plus the speakers from step 2 | with: LinkedIn | chips: attending, speaker
- Registrant exports | Luma and Zoom give registrants and who actually joined, for side events and sessions | with: Luma, Zoom | chips: registered, attended, no-show
= Three attendance states | Every row marked met in person, attended or registered no-show; met in person wins any tie | chips: met in person, attended, no-show

- Pull the list the evening the event ends. Export the directory from the event app, add the badge scans, and have whoever worked the booth mark everyone they spoke to.
- Add LinkedIn event page attendees and any Luma or Zoom export from side events; Zoom shows who registered and who joined.
- Mark every row met in person, attended, or registered no-show; met in person wins any tie. This split drives tiering, copy and the report.

### 4. Score fit before spending a credit
Tools: Claude, Ocean.io, Apollo, Attio
Flow:
- Exclusions first | Customers, open deals, active sequences, competitors, and vendors selling to the same crowd | with: Attio | tag: core: exclusions
- Fit and role | A cheap model reads each company site for an ICP verdict and marks whether the person buys, uses or signs off | with: Claude, Ocean.io, Apollo | chips: ICP yes or no, buy use or sign off, headcount

- Apply core: exclusions. Add one more: vendors selling to the same crowd are warmup prospects, never main-offer prospects.
- Enrich headcount, industry and location. A cheap model reads each website, gives a yes or no ICP verdict with one reason, and marks whether the person buys, uses or signs off.
- Drop the no's before any paid lookup. An attendee list looks warm, which is why it burns credits: a 4K badge list is often 600 real prospects.

### 5. Find the person, enrich and verify
Tools: LinkedIn, Apollo, Ocean.io, LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- Booth owner or GTM leader | For exhibitors, the field marketing or events lead who owns the booth, or the GTM leader; for attendees, the person who was there | with: LinkedIn, Apollo, Ocean.io | chips: field marketing, events, GTM leader, attendee
- Email waterfall | Each vendor only sees the contacts the one before it missed | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo | tag: core: enrichment
- Two-pass verification | Valid to the main campaign, catch-all to its own campaign | with: MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: verification

- For exhibitors, find the booth owner: a director or head of field marketing, events or trade shows, or the GTM leader under 200 people. If the warmup is the offer, that person is the buyer; otherwise pick the decision maker by company size and keep them as champion.
- For attendees, the person on the list is the contact. Confirm title on LinkedIn; badge data is often a job behind.
- Apply core: enrichment.
- Apply core: verification.

### 6. Tier and alert
Tools: Slack, Attio, LinkedIn, Phone
Flow:
- Tier 1 | Met in person, speakers, and exhibitors that fit: a Slack card, a LinkedIn connection and a call within two days | with: Slack, LinkedIn, Phone | chips: name, company, where you met, session, suggested opener | tag: high touch
- Tier 2 and 3 | Attended and no-show with ICP fit, to the campaign, written to the CRM with attendance state | with: Attio | chips: event, attendance state, tier, date | tag: at volume

- Tier 1 is anyone met in person, plus every speaker and exhibitor that fits. Post a Slack card with name, company, where you met them or their session, LinkedIn URL and a one-line opener. The owner connects on LinkedIn and calls within two days.
- Tier 2 is attended, Tier 3 is registered no-show, both with ICP fit; both go to the two-step email in step 7 within two days.
- Write every account to the CRM with signal type `event`, event name, attendance state, tier and date. Attendance state stays on the record as a scoring input for later plays.

### 7. Write and launch
Tools: Claude, EmailBison, Instantly (option), HeyReach
Flow:
- Pre-show | We will be at the show too: 15 minutes at the booth or coffee nearby, with a day and a time | with: Claude | chips: {EVENT}, {BOOTH}, {DAY}, {SESSION}
- Post-show | Open on the event and give one useful thing from it: a takeaway, the recording, a short list | with: Claude | chips: {EVENT}, {SESSION}, {TAKEAWAY} | tag: no filler openers
- Budget frame | For manufacturers past a few million in revenue: one rep's trip versus the same lead flow from two weeks of email | with: Claude | tag: variant
= Launch from its own inbox set | Email plus LinkedIn connection for Tier 2; pre-show stops the day the show opens, post-show stops a week after it ends | with: EmailBison, Instantly, HeyReach | tag: send

- Apply core: copy and send rules.
- Pre-show: you are both going to be there, so ask for 15 minutes at their booth or a coffee nearby, with a day and a time. For exhibitors buying the warmup, say plainly that you run their attendee list before the show.
- Post-show: open on the event and give one useful thing from it: a session takeaway, the recording, a short list of who was there in their segment. No-shows get the recording and a softer ask.
- Budget frame for manufacturers past a few million in revenue: flights, booth, hotel and a rep's week versus the same lead flow from a two-week campaign. Outbound sits next to the show, not instead of it.
- Draft three angle variants for email 1 and one threaded nudge for email 2 per audience; show the user before upload. Upload with `{EVENT}`, `{BOOTH}`, `{DAY}`, `{SESSION}`, `{TAKEAWAY}`.
- Pre-show sends stop the day the show opens; post-show sends stop a week after it ends. Stop any contact that replies, books or shows as an open deal.

### 8. Report
Tools: Attio, Slack
Flow:
= Weekly read by audience and timing | Pulled, ICP-fit rate, Tier 1 count, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings, split by exhibitor, attendee, no-show and by pre, post | with: Attio, Slack

- Weekly: pulled, ICP-fit rate, Tier 1 count, booth meetings, sent, reply rate, interested, meetings after. Split by audience (exhibitor, attendee, no-show) and timing (pre, post), and compare to the cold baseline.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- An attendee list is stale a week after the event ends. If it cannot be worked within two days, run it as a cold play with the event as a dated signal.
- Never open with "great to meet you", "hope you enjoyed the show" or a line about watching their booth. Every opener carries one useful thing from the event.
- Score fit before any paid lookup. A badge scan is attendance, not fit.
- Only work lists you collected yourself or the organizer gave you. A purchased attendee list is not this play.
- One person, one sequence. Someone who is both an exhibitor and an attendee is an exhibitor. Do not run this play and a cold play on the same domain in the same month.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-pre-show-warmup for our outbound agency, we are exhibiting at a 3-day building materials show in Dallas in three weeks, ICP is manufacturers 50 to 500 people in the US"

1. Set both windows, confirmed EmailBison, HeyReach, Attio and Slack, captured offer and proof.
2. Firecrawl pulled 412 exhibitors and 38 speakers; 388 companies after dedupe by domain.
3. 171 passed the ICP read, 9 removed as customers, open deals or same-crowd vendors.
4. 162 booth owners found, 118 with a GTM leader added as champion; 251 verified valid, 14 catch-all in their own campaign.
5. 67 Tier 1 Slack cards (38 speakers, 29 sponsor-tier exhibitors), 11 booth meetings booked. 184 contacts loaded into "Pre-show, Dallas", stopped the morning the show opened.
6. Show closed: 2,940 attendees from the app, 212 badge scans, 61 met in person. 644 passed the ICP read. 61 Tier 1 cards, 583 loaded into "Post-show, Dallas" within two days. Week one: 14 meetings after the show against 11 at the booth.

Play 22 · Third-party signals

Closed-Lost Revival

Trigger A loss-reason window plus a detected change, or a neutral reply that never converted
Outcome A deal reopened on what changed
Channels email, linkedin, call

Give this to your agent 
Install the Pipeline.tech "Closed-Lost Revival" outbound play. Fetch
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/core/SKILL.md and
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/plays/closed-lost-revival/SKILL.md,
save them as ~/.claude/skills/pipeline-outbound-core/SKILL.md and
~/.claude/skills/pipeline-closed-lost-revival/SKILL.md, then run /pipeline-closed-lost-revival.
Paste into Claude Code. It installs the shared core rules plus this play, then runs it as /pipeline-closed-lost-revival.
Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

These accounts already know you, they sat through a call or at least replied, and the CRM holds the reason they said no. That makes them the cheapest pipeline you will ever build, and the easiest to burn with a "just checking in" that proves nothing moved. The opener is never a check-in; it names what changed since the last conversation and ties it to the reason the deal stopped.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1 and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The ICP in one paragraph, and the offer in one sentence with one proof point.
  2. Stack answers from core section 1, plus where closed-lost deals, call recordings and reply buckets live.
  3. The loss-reason field and its values, and how far back to pull (default 24 months).
  4. The switch offer for competitor losses: the concrete reason to leave an incumbent, in one line.

Steps

1Pull both feeds

ToolsAttioHubSpot (option)EmailBisonInstantly (option)Google Sheets (option)
  • Export every deal closed-lost, stalled past 60 days, no-show without a rebook, or churned, with loss reason, close date, champion, blocker and owner.
  • Pull the reply buckets from Reply Routing: not-now with the date given, and every neutral or interested reply that never became a booking. That second group is the cheapest revival list there is; they already answered once.
  • Remove customers and open deals, then apply core: exclusions. Merge on domain with a feed column (crm, reply, both).

2Name the real blocker

ToolsFirefliesGong (option)Claude
  • Rep-entered loss reasons are often polite fiction: "timing" covers a lost champion, "budget" covers a cheaper competitor.
  • Where call notes or transcripts exist, a model reads the calls themselves and writes one line per account: the actual objection, who raised it, and the quote that proves it. One blocker per account: timing, budget cycle, competitor, missing feature, champion left, no decision maker.
  • For reply-feed rows with no call, the reply text is the evidence. "Not now, back in Q2" is timing with a date; a neutral "send me more info" that went nowhere is no decision maker.
  • Write the blocker, the evidence line and the source to the CRM. Nothing in this step is sent to anyone.

3Set the window per blocker

ToolsClaudeAttioHubSpot (option)
  • Timing: the start of the next quarter. Budget cycle: 60 days before the next fiscal year, before the plan locks. Competitor: 60 to 90 days before their renewal, usually the anniversary of the loss.
  • Missing feature: the day it ships. Champion left: when a replacement is hired, which step 4 detects. Not-now reply: the date they gave, else 90 days from the reply.
  • Write the revival date and the blocker to the CRM. No date is a mistake, not a free pass to email.

4Check what changed when the window opens

ToolsSerperApolloOcean.ioLinkedInBuiltWithClaude
  • On the revival date, run a job-change check on the champion and the blocker, a news search, a hiring check on the function, and a stack check on the domain. For missing-feature rows, confirm the feature shipped first.
  • The change has to answer the blocker. Funding answers budget; a new VP answers no decision maker or champion left; a competitor dropped from the stack answers competitor; hiring in the function answers timing.
  • A model writes the why-now line from the evidence with the source URL. Nothing changed means back to the queue for another cycle. Never reach out on nothing.

5Check the people

ToolsLinkedInApolloLeadMagicProspeoMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • Confirm the old champion is still at the company. If they left, hand them to Champion Moves at the new company, and keep this account in the play for the new committee.
  • Map the committee now: the buying seat plus one level up, by the decision-maker rules in core. Apply core: enrichment. Apply core: verification.
  • The old champion first, the new committee second. Store both on the CRM record with their role in the old deal.

6Re-engage on what changed

ToolsClaudePhoneLinkedInHeyReachEmailBisonInstantly (option)
  • Apply core: copy and send rules.
  • The frame: "when we spoke in {LAST_CALL_MONTH}, {BLOCKER} was the problem; since then {CHANGE}; worth another look?" The change is the subject, the blocker is proof you listened, and the ask is small.
  • Old champion, day 0: a personal note from the original deal owner plus a call the same day. It reads like a person who remembered, not a sequence.
  • New committee, day 2: the two-step email. Step one names the earlier conversation at their company, the blocker and the change; step two is the threaded nudge.
  • Competitor losses need a switch offer: a concrete reason to leave, such as a done-for-you migration or a term that undercuts their renewal. Feature lists against an incumbent get nothing.
  • Value CTA, never a meeting ask, unless the reply bucket shows they asked for a call. Show the champion note, both emails and the switch variant to the user before upload.

7Reopen, log and tune

ToolsAttioHubSpot (option)Slack
  • On a reply or a booking, create a new deal linked to the old one with the blocker, the change and the cycle number. Slack the original owner with the why-now line.
  • Log every cycle on the account, including silent ones.
  • Weekly: queued, windows opened, changes detected, contacted, reply rate, interested, reopened, split by blocker type and by feed. Tune the windows by which blockers actually revive.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • No reach-out without a change that answers the blocker. A window opening is permission to look, not to email.
  • Never a check-in, never "circling back", never a feature list to someone who chose a competitor.
  • Someone who was never on the call is never told "when we spoke".
  • CRM loss reasons are a hypothesis until a call or a reply confirms them.
  • Not-interested inside 180 days stays excluded through core. A dated no is not a not-now.
  • One account, one cycle at a time. Two feeds on one domain is one revival; a reopened deal pauses every other play on that domain.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-closed-lost-revival for our data platform, mid-market B2B, 24 months of closed-lost in Attio, calls in Fireflies, replies in EmailBison"

  1. Confirmed the stack; switch offer set as a 30-day migration done for them.
  2. CRM feed 412 deals; reply feed 96 not-now and 231 neutral or interested that never booked. 61 removed through exclusions; 678 accounts after the merge.
  3. 287 accounts had a recording; the model moved 94 to a different blocker than the rep entered. Split: 198 timing, 141 budget, 117 competitor, 64 missing feature, 83 champion left, 75 no decision maker.
  4. 214 windows opened in month one. 71 showed a change that answered the blocker: 19 new leaders, 23 funding or hiring, 14 competitor removed from the stack, 15 shipped features. 143 back to the queue.
  5. 44 old champions still in seat, 27 handed to Champion Moves; 118 committee contacts, 101 verified valid, 6 catch-all in their own campaign.
  6. 44 champion notes and calls on day 0, 7 calls answered; 101 committee contacts loaded into "Closed-Lost Revival, November", 14 on the switch variant; copy approved before upload.
  7. Week 4: 31 replies, 12 interested, 9 deals reopened and linked to the old ones. Timing revived at twice the rate of budget; competitor window moved from 90 to 60 days.

# Closed-Lost Revival

These accounts already know you, they sat through a call or at least replied, and the CRM holds the reason they said no. That makes them the cheapest pipeline you will ever build, and the easiest to burn with a "just checking in" that proves nothing moved. The opener is never a check-in; it names what changed since the last conversation and ties it to the reason the deal stopped.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1 and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The ICP in one paragraph, and the offer in one sentence with one proof point.
2. Stack answers from core section 1, plus where closed-lost deals, call recordings and reply buckets live.
3. The loss-reason field and its values, and how far back to pull (default 24 months).
4. The switch offer for competitor losses: the concrete reason to leave an incumbent, in one line.

## Steps

### 1. Pull both feeds
Tools: Attio, HubSpot (option), EmailBison, Instantly (option), Google Sheets (option)
Flow:
- CRM feed | Deals closed-lost, stalled, no-show or churned in the lookback window, with loss reason, close date, champion and blocker | with: Attio, HubSpot | chips: loss reason, close date, champion, blocker
- Reply feed | Not-now replies with a date, plus neutral or interested replies that never booked, pulled from Reply Routing | with: EmailBison, Instantly | chips: not now, neutral, interested no booking, reply date
= Exclude and merge | Customers and open deals out, one row per account with both feeds marked | with: Google Sheets | tag: core: exclusions

- Export every deal closed-lost, stalled past 60 days, no-show without a rebook, or churned, with loss reason, close date, champion, blocker and owner.
- Pull the reply buckets from Reply Routing: not-now with the date given, and every neutral or interested reply that never became a booking. That second group is the cheapest revival list there is; they already answered once.
- Remove customers and open deals, then apply core: exclusions. Merge on domain with a `feed` column (crm, reply, both).

### 2. Name the real blocker
Tools: Fireflies, Gong (option), Claude
Flow:
- Read the loss reason | The rep-entered field is the starting point, not the answer | with: Claude | chips: timing, budget cycle, competitor, missing feature, champion left, no decision maker
= Read the calls | Where recordings or transcripts exist, a model reads the last two calls and writes one line per account with the actual objection and the evidence | with: Fireflies, Gong, Claude | tag: one line per account

- Rep-entered loss reasons are often polite fiction: "timing" covers a lost champion, "budget" covers a cheaper competitor.
- Where call notes or transcripts exist, a model reads the calls themselves and writes one line per account: the actual objection, who raised it, and the quote that proves it. One blocker per account: timing, budget cycle, competitor, missing feature, champion left, no decision maker.
- For reply-feed rows with no call, the reply text is the evidence. "Not now, back in Q2" is timing with a date; a neutral "send me more info" that went nowhere is no decision maker.
- Write the blocker, the evidence line and the source to the CRM. Nothing in this step is sent to anyone.

### 3. Set the window per blocker
Tools: Claude, Attio, HubSpot (option)
Flow:
- Window rules | Timing next quarter; budget next fiscal cycle; competitor 60 to 90 days before their renewal; missing feature the day it ships; champion left when a replacement is hired; not-now the date given, else 90 days | with: Claude | chips: next quarter, fiscal cycle, 60 to 90 days pre-renewal, ship date, replacement hired, 90 days
= Queue to a date | Every account gets a revival date on the CRM record and sits silent until then | with: Attio, HubSpot | tag: dated recall

- Timing: the start of the next quarter. Budget cycle: 60 days before the next fiscal year, before the plan locks. Competitor: 60 to 90 days before their renewal, usually the anniversary of the loss.
- Missing feature: the day it ships. Champion left: when a replacement is hired, which step 4 detects. Not-now reply: the date they gave, else 90 days from the reply.
- Write the revival date and the blocker to the CRM. No date is a mistake, not a free pass to email.

### 4. Check what changed when the window opens
Tools: Serper, Apollo, Ocean.io, LinkedIn, BuiltWith, Claude
Flow:
- People and money | New decision maker or champion in the seat, funding announced, hiring in the function you sell into | with: LinkedIn, Apollo, Serper | chips: new DM, new champion, round, open roles
- Stack and product | Competitor removed or added, the missing feature shipped on your side, the competitor's renewal month arriving | with: BuiltWith, Ocean.io, Serper | chips: stack change, feature shipped, renewal month
= Why-now line or back to the queue | A model writes one line from the evidence; no evidence means another cycle in the queue and no email | with: Claude | tag: never on nothing

- On the revival date, run a job-change check on the champion and the blocker, a news search, a hiring check on the function, and a stack check on the domain. For missing-feature rows, confirm the feature shipped first.
- The change has to answer the blocker. Funding answers budget; a new VP answers no decision maker or champion left; a competitor dropped from the stack answers competitor; hiring in the function answers timing.
- A model writes the why-now line from the evidence with the source URL. Nothing changed means back to the queue for another cycle. Never reach out on nothing.

### 5. Check the people
Tools: LinkedIn, Apollo, LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- Old champion | Still there, moved internally, or gone; a champion who left goes to Champion Moves at their new company | with: LinkedIn, Apollo | chips: still there, new title, left, new company
- New committee | Who holds the seat now and who sits beside them, by company size | with: Apollo | tag: core: enrichment
= Enrich and verify | Waterfall on every new address and two-pass verification before any send | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: verification

- Confirm the old champion is still at the company. If they left, hand them to Champion Moves at the new company, and keep this account in the play for the new committee.
- Map the committee now: the buying seat plus one level up, by the decision-maker rules in core. Apply core: enrichment. Apply core: verification.
- The old champion first, the new committee second. Store both on the CRM record with their role in the old deal.

### 6. Re-engage on what changed
Tools: Claude, Phone, LinkedIn, HeyReach, EmailBison, Instantly (option)
Flow:
- Old champion, day 0 | A personal note from the original owner and a call the same day: when we spoke in that month this was the problem, since then this changed, worth another look | with: Phone, LinkedIn, HeyReach | chips: {LAST_CALL_MONTH}, {BLOCKER}, {CHANGE}, {OWNER}
- New committee, day 2 | Two-step email that names the earlier conversation at the company and the change, without pretending to know them | with: Claude, EmailBison, Instantly | tag: send
= Competitor losses | A switch offer with a concrete reason to leave, never a feature list | with: Claude | tag: switch offer

- Apply core: copy and send rules.
- The frame: "when we spoke in {LAST_CALL_MONTH}, {BLOCKER} was the problem; since then {CHANGE}; worth another look?" The change is the subject, the blocker is proof you listened, and the ask is small.
- Old champion, day 0: a personal note from the original deal owner plus a call the same day. It reads like a person who remembered, not a sequence.
- New committee, day 2: the two-step email. Step one names the earlier conversation at their company, the blocker and the change; step two is the threaded nudge.
- Competitor losses need a switch offer: a concrete reason to leave, such as a done-for-you migration or a term that undercuts their renewal. Feature lists against an incumbent get nothing.
- Value CTA, never a meeting ask, unless the reply bucket shows they asked for a call. Show the champion note, both emails and the switch variant to the user before upload.

### 7. Reopen, log and tune
Tools: Attio, HubSpot (option), Slack
Flow:
- Reopen and alert | A reply or a booking reopens a new deal linked to the old one and the owner hears in Slack with the why-now line | with: Attio, HubSpot, Slack | chips: linked deal, revival cycle, blocker, feed
= Weekly read by blocker and feed | Queued, windows opened, changes found, contacted, replies, reopened, split by blocker type and by CRM versus reply feed | with: Attio, Slack | tag: tune the windows

- On a reply or a booking, create a new deal linked to the old one with the blocker, the change and the cycle number. Slack the original owner with the why-now line.
- Log every cycle on the account, including silent ones.
- Weekly: queued, windows opened, changes detected, contacted, reply rate, interested, reopened, split by blocker type and by feed. Tune the windows by which blockers actually revive.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- No reach-out without a change that answers the blocker. A window opening is permission to look, not to email.
- Never a check-in, never "circling back", never a feature list to someone who chose a competitor.
- Someone who was never on the call is never told "when we spoke".
- CRM loss reasons are a hypothesis until a call or a reply confirms them.
- Not-interested inside 180 days stays excluded through core. A dated no is not a not-now.
- One account, one cycle at a time. Two feeds on one domain is one revival; a reopened deal pauses every other play on that domain.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-closed-lost-revival for our data platform, mid-market B2B, 24 months of closed-lost in Attio, calls in Fireflies, replies in EmailBison"

1. Confirmed the stack; switch offer set as a 30-day migration done for them.
2. CRM feed 412 deals; reply feed 96 not-now and 231 neutral or interested that never booked. 61 removed through exclusions; 678 accounts after the merge.
3. 287 accounts had a recording; the model moved 94 to a different blocker than the rep entered. Split: 198 timing, 141 budget, 117 competitor, 64 missing feature, 83 champion left, 75 no decision maker.
4. 214 windows opened in month one. 71 showed a change that answered the blocker: 19 new leaders, 23 funding or hiring, 14 competitor removed from the stack, 15 shipped features. 143 back to the queue.
5. 44 old champions still in seat, 27 handed to Champion Moves; 118 committee contacts, 101 verified valid, 6 catch-all in their own campaign.
6. 44 champion notes and calls on day 0, 7 calls answered; 101 committee contacts loaded into "Closed-Lost Revival, November", 14 on the switch variant; copy approved before upload.
7. Week 4: 31 replies, 12 interested, 9 deals reopened and linked to the old ones. Timing revived at twice the rate of budget; competitor window moved from 90 to 60 days.

Play 23 · Third-party signals

Champion Moves

Trigger A past buyer, user or advocate lands at an ICP company
Outcome A meeting at the new logo
Channels email, linkedin, call

Give this to your agent 
Install the Pipeline.tech "Champion Moves" outbound play. Fetch
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/core/SKILL.md and
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/plays/champion-moves/SKILL.md,
save them as ~/.claude/skills/pipeline-outbound-core/SKILL.md and
~/.claude/skills/pipeline-champion-moves/SKILL.md, then run /pipeline-champion-moves.
Paste into Claude Code. It installs the shared core rules plus this play, then runs it as /pipeline-champion-moves.
Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

The warmest cold email there is goes to someone who already bought from you, used you or vouched for you, on the day they land somewhere new with budget and a problem they know you solve. The trust a stranger would have to earn is already on record in your CRM. This play keeps a roster of those people, watches for the move, and puts the person who worked with them in front of them before the new stack is decided.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1 and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve.
  2. Where the relationship history lives: CRM, product user list, event lists, newsletter replies, and who owned each account.
  3. Stack answers from core section 1.
  4. Which results and customer names may be named in writing.

Steps

1Build the champion roster

ToolsAttioHubSpot (option)Salesforce (option)Google SheetsClaude
  • Export every contact at a closed-won account and tag their role in the deal: signer, champion, daily user. The product's user list knows who logged in every day, and those people move too.
  • Add the warm non-customers: churned on good terms, replied interested or booked but never closed, event contacts, newsletter readers who wrote back.
  • Tag every row with relationship strength (bought, used, advocated, met), the owner at the time, and the result they saw where it is on record. No record, no number in the copy.

2Watch for moves

ToolsApolloAI Ark (option)LinkedInSales Navigator
  • Re-enrich current company, title and start date on every roster row on the same day each month. Apollo for most rosters, AI Ark past a few thousand rows.
  • Load the roster into Sales Navigator as saved lead lists so alerts arrive between runs.
  • A changed company is a move and continues here. A changed title at the same company is a promotion: a congratulations note from the owner, then back to the roster, never a sequence.

3Confirm and qualify the landing

ToolsLinkedInOcean.ioApolloClaudeAttio
  • Confirm every move with a second source. One feed gets start dates wrong by months and reads an advisory role as a move.
  • ICP check on the new company: headcount, industry, location, a yes or no with one reason. Apply core: exclusions. The relationship does not make a non-ICP company ICP.
  • If the new company is already a customer or an open deal, this is not a sequence. Write it as an expansion intro and route it to that account's owner, who just gained an internal ally.

4The hole left behind

ToolsSlackAttioHubSpot (option)
  • When the person leaving sits at a current customer, Slack the account owner the same day. The account just lost its internal advocate; the next renewal runs through someone who did not pick you.
  • Log a churn flag with the departed champion's role and date. The owner maps who holds the seat now and books a call within two weeks. A retention step, and it lives here because the signal arrives here first.

5Enrich and verify the new address

ToolsLeadMagicProspeoMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • Find the address at the new company; the old one is dead or forwards to a stranger.
  • New addresses reach the vendors two to four weeks after the start date, so re-run the waterfall weekly on empty rows. LinkedIn carries the personal note meanwhile.
  • Apply core: enrichment. Apply core: verification.

6Tier and route

ToolsSlackAttioPhoneLinkedInGmail
  • Tier 1: they bought, and the new company fits. Slack the original owner with the move, the shared history and a one-line opener. The owner sends a personal note from their own inbox and calls. No campaign for Tier 1, ever.
  • Tier 2: they used it or advocated for it. The person who worked with them writes a personal email; if silent by day 14, a two-step sequence follows from the same inbox.
  • Tier 3: met only. Two-step sequence from the owner's own inbox.
  • Write every row to the CRM with play name, tier, relationship strength, old account, new company, start date and source URL. If a cold play is working the new company, the champion wins and the cold row pauses.

7Write and launch

ToolsClaudeGmailEmailBisonInstantly (option)HeyReach
  • Apply core: copy and send rules, with one change: these emails come from a named person's real inbox, not a play inbox set.
  • Email 1: congratulate on the specific move, recall the shared history in one line (what they used it for and the result, named only if on record and permitted), offer to show how it would look at the new company. Email 2 is a threaded nudge.
  • The committee ask lives in the reply thread, never in the sequence.
  • Draft one variant per tier and show them to the user before anything sends. Load Tier 2 and 3 with {NEW_COMPANY}, {OLD_COMPANY}, {RESULT} and {OWNER_NAME}; stop at day 90 or on a reply.

8Report monthly

ToolsAttioSlack
  • Monthly, after the re-enrichment run: moves caught, ICP landings, expansion intros, notes sent, sequences started, reply rate, meetings, plus churn flags raised at old accounts and how many got a new contact mapped.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • A personal note may go any time after the move is public; the business ask waits until day 14.
  • No overlap with New Leader in Seat. That play watches the buying seat at target accounts for any newcomer; Champion Moves watches the people who already know you, wherever they land. A person in both is removed from New Leader in Seat; the champion relationship wins.
  • Always from the person who worked with them, never a cold inbox set. If that person has left, the owner who inherited the relationship sends, and the email says so.
  • Never reveal CRM detail they would not expect you to remember. A result they reported on a call is fair; seat count, contract value or a support ticket is not.
  • A champion who lands outside the ICP gets a congratulations note and stays on the roster. The roster is never a cold list.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-champion-moves for our outbound agency, 140 closed-won accounts over four years, Attio plus a product user export"

  1. Asked for nameable results and account owners; confirmed Attio, Apollo, Sales Navigator, EmailBison, Gmail and Slack.
  2. Roster: 612 rows. 318 at closed-won accounts (97 signers, 141 champions, 80 daily users), 64 churned well, 146 interested but never closed, 84 event and newsletter contacts. 221 bought, 119 used, 58 advocated, 214 met.
  3. First monthly run: 41 changes, 29 moves, 12 promotions to their own lane. Sales Navigator added 6 moves between runs.
  4. 35 moves, 31 confirmed; 17 at ICP companies, 3 at current customers routed as expansion intros, 11 outside the ICP, congratulations note only.
  5. 9 departed champions sat at current customers: 9 same-day Slack alerts and churn flags, 6 new contacts mapped within two weeks.
  6. 14 new addresses found, 3 waiting for next week's re-run; 13 verified valid, 1 catch-all in its own campaign.
  7. 6 Tier 1: personal notes and calls from the original owners, 4 calls held. 7 Tier 2: 3 replied inside 14 days, 4 moved to the two-step sequence. 4 Tier 3 loaded from the owner's inbox.
  8. Month one: 5 meetings at new logos, 3 expansion intros, 9 churn flags raised.

name: pipeline-champion-moves
description: Pipeline.tech play. Reach a past buyer, user or advocate on the day they land somewhere new with budget and a problem they already know you solve. Builds a champion roster from the CRM and the product, re-enriches it monthly for company and title changes alongside LinkedIn job-change alerts, confirms every move with a second source, checks the new company against the ICP, flags the hole left at the old account to its owner the same day, then routes by relationship strength: a personal note and a call from the original owner for past buyers, a personal email then a two-step sequence for users and advocates. USE WHEN user says "/pipeline-champion-moves" OR "champion moves" OR "champion tracking" OR "past customer changed jobs" OR "our champion left" OR "track where our users went".

outcome: A meeting at the new logo

# Champion Moves

The warmest cold email there is goes to someone who already bought from you, used you or vouched for you, on the day they land somewhere new with budget and a problem they know you solve. The trust a stranger would have to earn is already on record in your CRM. This play keeps a roster of those people, watches for the move, and puts the person who worked with them in front of them before the new stack is decided.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1 and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve.
2. Where the relationship history lives: CRM, product user list, event lists, newsletter replies, and who owned each account.
3. Stack answers from core section 1.
4. Which results and customer names may be named in writing.

## Steps

### 1. Build the champion roster
Tools: Attio, HubSpot (option), Salesforce (option), Google Sheets, Claude
Flow:
- Contacts from closed-won | Every contact at a closed-won account tagged by their role in the deal; the product user list fills the daily-user gap the CRM misses | with: Attio, HubSpot, Salesforce | chips: signer, champion, daily user
- Warm non-customers | Churned on good terms, replied interested or booked but never closed, met at an event, wrote back to the newsletter | with: Attio, Google Sheets | chips: churned well, interested, booked, event, newsletter reply
= Tag relationship strength | Bought, used, advocated or met, plus the account owner at the time and the result they saw where it is on record | with: Claude | chips: bought, used, advocated, met, result on record

- Export every contact at a closed-won account and tag their role in the deal: signer, champion, daily user. The product's user list knows who logged in every day, and those people move too.
- Add the warm non-customers: churned on good terms, replied interested or booked but never closed, event contacts, newsletter readers who wrote back.
- Tag every row with relationship strength (bought, used, advocated, met), the owner at the time, and the result they saw where it is on record. No record, no number in the copy.

### 2. Watch for moves
Tools: Apollo, AI Ark (option), LinkedIn, Sales Navigator
Flow:
- Monthly re-enrichment | Current company, title and start date on every roster row, run on the same day each month so the delta is clean | with: Apollo, AI Ark | chips: company, title, start date
- Job-change alerts | The roster saved as lead lists so changes surface between monthly runs | with: LinkedIn, Sales Navigator | tag: second feed
= Move or promotion | A changed company is a move and runs this play; a changed title at the same company is a promotion and goes to its own lane | chips: move, promotion

- Re-enrich current company, title and start date on every roster row on the same day each month. Apollo for most rosters, AI Ark past a few thousand rows.
- Load the roster into Sales Navigator as saved lead lists so alerts arrive between runs.
- A changed company is a move and continues here. A changed title at the same company is a promotion: a congratulations note from the owner, then back to the roster, never a sequence.

### 3. Confirm and qualify the landing
Tools: LinkedIn, Ocean.io, Apollo, Claude, Attio
Flow:
- Second source | LinkedIn profile or the new company's team page before any move counts, with start date and previous company | with: LinkedIn, Claude | chips: confirmed, start date, source URL
- ICP check on the new company | Headcount, industry, location and a yes or no with one reason from the website | with: Ocean.io, Apollo, Claude | tag: core: exclusions
= Customer or open deal | A landing at a current customer or open deal becomes an expansion intro routed to that account's owner, not a sequence | with: Attio | tag: expansion intro

- Confirm every move with a second source. One feed gets start dates wrong by months and reads an advisory role as a move.
- ICP check on the new company: headcount, industry, location, a yes or no with one reason. Apply core: exclusions. The relationship does not make a non-ICP company ICP.
- If the new company is already a customer or an open deal, this is not a sequence. Write it as an expansion intro and route it to that account's owner, who just gained an internal ally.

### 4. The hole left behind
Tools: Slack, Attio, HubSpot (option)
Flow:
= Same-day alert to the old account's owner | The account just lost its internal advocate and needs a new one; log a churn flag with the departed champion's role and date | with: Slack, Attio, HubSpot | chips: account, departed champion, role, owner | tag: retention

- When the person leaving sits at a current customer, Slack the account owner the same day. The account just lost its internal advocate; the next renewal runs through someone who did not pick you.
- Log a churn flag with the departed champion's role and date. The owner maps who holds the seat now and books a call within two weeks. A retention step, and it lives here because the signal arrives here first.

### 5. Enrich and verify the new address
Tools: LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- Email waterfall at the new company | The new work address, never the one on the old record; each vendor only sees what the one before it missed | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo | tag: core: enrichment
- Two-pass verification | Valid to the main campaign, catch-all to its own | with: MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: verification

- Find the address at the new company; the old one is dead or forwards to a stranger.
- New addresses reach the vendors two to four weeks after the start date, so re-run the waterfall weekly on empty rows. LinkedIn carries the personal note meanwhile.
- Apply core: enrichment. Apply core: verification.

### 6. Tier and route
Tools: Slack, Attio, Phone, LinkedIn, Gmail
Flow:
- Tier 1: bought | Past buyer at an ICP landing: the original owner sends a personal note and calls, never a campaign | with: Slack, Phone, LinkedIn, Gmail | tag: high touch
- Tier 2: used or advocated | Personal email from the person who worked with them, then a two-step sequence from that same inbox if silent at day 14 | with: Gmail, Attio | chips: personal first, sequence second
- Tier 3: met | Two-step sequence from the owner's own inbox | with: Attio | chips: event, newsletter reply

- Tier 1: they bought, and the new company fits. Slack the original owner with the move, the shared history and a one-line opener. The owner sends a personal note from their own inbox and calls. No campaign for Tier 1, ever.
- Tier 2: they used it or advocated for it. The person who worked with them writes a personal email; if silent by day 14, a two-step sequence follows from the same inbox.
- Tier 3: met only. Two-step sequence from the owner's own inbox.
- Write every row to the CRM with play name, tier, relationship strength, old account, new company, start date and source URL. If a cold play is working the new company, the champion wins and the cold row pauses.

### 7. Write and launch
Tools: Claude, Gmail, EmailBison, Instantly (option), HeyReach
Flow:
- Congratulate and recall | The move named specifically, one line of shared history with the result only if on record and permitted, an offer to show how it would look at the new company | with: Claude | chips: {NEW_COMPANY}, {OLD_COMPANY}, {RESULT}, {OWNER_NAME}
- Ask for the committee later | The new committee is asked for only after they reply; email 1 asks for nothing but a look | with: Claude | tag: one ask
= Launch from the owner's inbox | Personal note any time after the move is public, business ask from day 14, stop at day 90 or on a reply | with: Gmail, EmailBison, Instantly, HeyReach | tag: send

- Apply core: copy and send rules, with one change: these emails come from a named person's real inbox, not a play inbox set.
- Email 1: congratulate on the specific move, recall the shared history in one line (what they used it for and the result, named only if on record and permitted), offer to show how it would look at the new company. Email 2 is a threaded nudge.
- The committee ask lives in the reply thread, never in the sequence.
- Draft one variant per tier and show them to the user before anything sends. Load Tier 2 and 3 with `{NEW_COMPANY}`, `{OLD_COMPANY}`, `{RESULT}` and `{OWNER_NAME}`; stop at day 90 or on a reply.

### 8. Report monthly
Tools: Attio, Slack
Flow:
= Monthly read by tier | Moves caught, promotions split out, ICP landings, expansion intros, meetings, churn flags raised at the old accounts | with: Attio, Slack

- Monthly, after the re-enrichment run: moves caught, ICP landings, expansion intros, notes sent, sequences started, reply rate, meetings, plus churn flags raised at old accounts and how many got a new contact mapped.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- A personal note may go any time after the move is public; the business ask waits until day 14.
- No overlap with New Leader in Seat. That play watches the buying seat at target accounts for any newcomer; Champion Moves watches the people who already know you, wherever they land. A person in both is removed from New Leader in Seat; the champion relationship wins.
- Always from the person who worked with them, never a cold inbox set. If that person has left, the owner who inherited the relationship sends, and the email says so.
- Never reveal CRM detail they would not expect you to remember. A result they reported on a call is fair; seat count, contract value or a support ticket is not.
- A champion who lands outside the ICP gets a congratulations note and stays on the roster. The roster is never a cold list.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-champion-moves for our outbound agency, 140 closed-won accounts over four years, Attio plus a product user export"

1. Asked for nameable results and account owners; confirmed Attio, Apollo, Sales Navigator, EmailBison, Gmail and Slack.
2. Roster: 612 rows. 318 at closed-won accounts (97 signers, 141 champions, 80 daily users), 64 churned well, 146 interested but never closed, 84 event and newsletter contacts. 221 bought, 119 used, 58 advocated, 214 met.
3. First monthly run: 41 changes, 29 moves, 12 promotions to their own lane. Sales Navigator added 6 moves between runs.
4. 35 moves, 31 confirmed; 17 at ICP companies, 3 at current customers routed as expansion intros, 11 outside the ICP, congratulations note only.
5. 9 departed champions sat at current customers: 9 same-day Slack alerts and churn flags, 6 new contacts mapped within two weeks.
6. 14 new addresses found, 3 waiting for next week's re-run; 13 verified valid, 1 catch-all in its own campaign.
7. 6 Tier 1: personal notes and calls from the original owners, 4 calls held. 7 Tier 2: 3 replied inside 14 days, 4 moved to the two-step sequence. 4 Tier 3 loaded from the owner's inbox.
8. Month one: 5 meetings at new logos, 3 expansion intros, 9 churn flags raised.

Social signals

People who told you what they care about by engaging in public.

Play 24 · Social signals

Competitor Post Engagers

Trigger A reaction or comment on a competitor voice's post in the last 24 hours
Outcome A switch conversation on the post topic
Channels linkedin, email

Give this to your agent 
Install the Pipeline.tech "Competitor Post Engagers" outbound play. Fetch
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/core/SKILL.md and
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/plays/competitor-post-engagers/SKILL.md,
save them as ~/.claude/skills/pipeline-outbound-core/SKILL.md and
