Paste into Claude Code. It installs the shared core rules plus this play, then runs it as /pipeline-competitor-post-engagers.
Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

A competitor's posts pull in the people thinking about this exact problem today. By commenting or reacting they told you which part of it they care about, so the opener starts there and never mentions the act of watching. Pipeline.tech's book, The Outbound Playbook, lists LinkedIn engagement as the intent signal with the highest reply rates of any signal tool, because engaging with a post is a stated interest in the topic.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve.
  2. The 3 to 6 competitors buyers name on sales calls.
  3. The seat that buys: exact titles and functions, with the seniority floor.
  4. Stack answers from core section 1, plus the real LinkedIn profile the DMs go out from.
  5. The switch offer in one sentence, one proof point, and which customers may be named in writing.

Steps

1Pick the competitor voices

ToolsLinkedInGoogle SheetsClaude
  • Start with the 3 to 6 competitors buyers name on sales calls; more dilutes the capture and the report.
  • For each, list the company page plus the people who post for it: founders, execs with an audience, creators on partner content. The last 30 days of posts show who actually posts.
  • Store 10 to 30 profile URLs in a sheet with competitor name, voice type and date added. Review monthly: drop quiet voices, add new ones.

2Capture posts and engagers daily

ToolsTrigifyApify (option)Teamfluence (option)ClaudeGoogle Sheets
  • Run the capture daily. Trigify first: it watches the profiles and returns posts and engagers with the engagement type. Apify or Teamfluence when the user already runs one.
  • Store post URL, text, date, voice, engager LinkedIn URL, reaction or comment, comment text. Comments weigh more: a comment is a sentence about the topic, a reaction is a nod.
  • Dedupe by post id and person. Three reactions on one competitor's posts is one row with three engagements, not three leads.
  • A model tags each post with one topic class: launch, how-to, pricing, opinion, hiring, other. Hiring and other never drive copy.

3Qualify in three gates

ToolsClaudeOcean.ioApolloAttioHubSpot (option)
  • Company first. Remove anyone employed by a competitor, a partner or agency of one, or a recruiter. Then headcount, industry and location with Ocean.io or Apollo, and a yes or no ICP verdict from the website.
  • Persona second. Keep the titles from input 3. People below the seat at an ICP account stay flagged as the path to the buyer in step 4. Students and vendors are out.
  • CRM third. Apply core: exclusions. Log what each gate removed and why.

4Enrich and verify

ToolsLeadMagicProspeoApolloMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • The LinkedIn URL is the key. Apply core: enrichment with the profile URL as the lookup, never name plus company; engager headlines are often stale.
  • When the engager is below the buying seat at an ICP account, find the buyer at the same account per core section 4 and attach them to the row. The engagement belongs to the account.
  • Apply core: verification.

5Tier and store

ToolsClaudeAttioHubSpot (option)
  • Tier 1: a commenter with a buying title at an ICP account. Tier 2: a reactor in the buying seat at an ICP account, or a commenter below the seat with the buyer found. Tier 3: acceptable fit.
  • Apply core: tiering. Two engagements on the same competitor inside 30 days, or an engagement plus another dated signal, moves the row up one tier.
  • Write post URL, topic class, competitor, engagement type and date to the CRM record with the tier.

6Write the switch pitch on the post topic

ToolsClaude
  • Apply core: copy and send rules.
  • Open on the topic, never on the competitor's post or the engagement. Name it the way a peer in that seat would.
  • Launch post: how your approach compares on the thing they launched, one specific difference. How-to post: the same job done simpler. Pricing post: the cost of staying another year. Opinion post: agree with the point and extend it into what you do differently.
  • Tier 1 requires a switch offer in the first message: migration done for them, a parallel run, or a buyout of the remaining term. Features alone do not move anyone off a vendor they already pay.
  • Draft one email variant per topic class plus a threaded nudge for email 2, and one DM under 300 characters per topic. Show the user before upload. Variables: {TOPIC}, {COMPANY}, {TITLE}.

7Multi-thread and launch

ToolsHeyReachLinkedInEmailBisonInstantly (option)SlackAttio
  • Day 0: a connection request with a short note and, on accept, a DM on the topic, from a real person's profile through HeyReach. Inside 48 hours of the engagement or skip the DM.
  • Day 3: a two-step email to the engager plus one or two colleagues in the buying seat, from this play's own inbox set, email 1 inside 7 days of the engagement.
  • Slack on every reply with the post URL, topic class and competitor. Stop on a reply, a booking or an open deal.

8Report

ToolsAttioSlack
  • Weekly: engagers captured, qualified rate, connection accept rate, replies, meetings, split by competitor voice and topic class, against the cold baseline.
  • After a month, cut voices whose engagers rarely pass the gates and lean the copy toward the topics that book.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • Never say you saw them engage. No "saw your comment", no "noticed you liked".
  • Skip posts about layoffs, personal news or grief, and every engager on them.
  • Competitor staff are never contacted, nor their partners or agencies.
  • One person at most once per 60 days across all social plays.
  • Scope: this play is engagers on posts by competitor voices (their company page, founders, execs, creators who post for them). Engagers on your own, your team's or chosen creators' posts belong to Content Engagement; posts about your brand, competitors or the category pain written by third parties belong to Brand and Keyword Mentions.
  • DM window 48 hours, email window 7 days. An older engagement is stored for stacking, never named in copy.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-competitor-post-engagers for our sales engagement platform, B2B SaaS 50 to 500 people in North America, buyers VP Sales and Head of RevOps, competitors the four we lose to most"

  1. Asked for the four competitors and the switch offer (migration in 10 business days plus a parallel run); confirmed Trigify, Attio, EmailBison, HeyReach and Slack.
  2. Watch list: 4 company pages plus 17 people, 21 voices.
  3. Week one: 46 posts, 2,140 engagements (1,880 reactions, 260 comments), 1,712 people; 9 launch, 14 how-to, 3 pricing, 12 opinion, 5 hiring, 3 other.
  4. Gates: 214 removed as competitor staff, partners or recruiters; 611 at ICP-fit companies; 188 in the buying seat plus 97 below it; 23 out at the CRM gate; 262 through.
  5. 231 emails found by LinkedIn URL, 84 buyers attached to below-seat engagers; 289 valid, 14 catch-all in their own campaign.
  6. 31 Tier 1, 143 Tier 2, 88 Tier 3.
  7. 174 connection requests inside 48 hours, 71 accepted, 64 DMs. 303 addresses loaded into "Competitor Engagers, November", four topic variants approved.
  8. First weekly read: 19 replies, 7 interested, 4 meetings, 3 from how-to and pricing posts by one competitor's founder.

5. The switch offer in one sentence, one proof point, and which customers may be named in writing.

### 1. Pick the competitor voices
Tools: LinkedIn, Google Sheets, Claude
Flow:
- Competitor set | 3 to 6 competitors whose buyers match the ICP, the ones named on sales calls first | with: Claude | chips: named on calls, same buyer, same price band
- Voices per competitor | The company page plus the founders, execs and creators who post for it, found from the people tab and the last 30 days of posts | with: LinkedIn | chips: company page, founders, execs, creators

### 2. Capture posts and engagers daily
Tools: Trigify, Apify (option), Teamfluence (option), Claude, Google Sheets
Flow:
- New posts on the watch list | Every post from the last 24 hours with URL, text, date and the voice it came from | with: Trigify, Apify, Teamfluence | chips: post URL, text, date, voice
- Engagers per post | Every reaction and comment with the engager's LinkedIn URL, engagement type and comment text; comments weighted above reactions | with: Trigify, Apify, Teamfluence | chips: engager URL, reaction or comment, comment text
= Dedupe and tag | One row per post and person; a model tags each post with one topic class | with: Claude, Google Sheets | chips: launch, how-to, pricing, opinion, hiring, other

### 3. Qualify in three gates
Tools: Claude, Ocean.io, Apollo, Attio, HubSpot (option)
Flow:
- Company gate | Competitor staff, partners, agencies and recruiters removed, then headcount, industry and location, and a yes or no ICP verdict from the website | with: Ocean.io, Apollo, Claude | chips: not competitor staff, headcount, industry, ICP verdict
- Persona gate | The titles that buy, not everyone who reacts; below the seat at an ICP account is kept as a path to the buyer | with: Claude | chips: buying title, below seat, out
- CRM gate | Not a customer, not an open deal, not in any sequence in 90 days | with: Attio, HubSpot | tag: core: exclusions

- Company first. Remove anyone employed by a competitor, a partner or agency of one, or a recruiter. Then headcount, industry and location with Ocean.io or Apollo, and a yes or no ICP verdict from the website.
- Persona second. Keep the titles from input 3. People below the seat at an ICP account stay flagged as the path to the buyer in step 4. Students and vendors are out.
- CRM third. Apply core: exclusions. Log what each gate removed and why.

### 4. Enrich and verify
Tools: LeadMagic, Prospeo, Apollo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- Person by LinkedIn URL | Current title, company and work email, waterfall keyed on the profile URL | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo | tag: core: enrichment
- Buyer at the same account | When the engager sits below the buying seat, find the person in the seat at the same company | with: Apollo | chips: buying seat, same account
= Two-pass verification | Valid to the main campaign, catch-all to its own campaign | with: MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: verification

- The LinkedIn URL is the key. Apply core: enrichment with the profile URL as the lookup, never name plus company; engager headlines are often stale.
- When the engager is below the buying seat at an ICP account, find the buyer at the same account per core section 4 and attach them to the row. The engagement belongs to the account.
- Apply core: verification.

### 5. Tier and store
Tools: Claude, Attio, HubSpot (option)
Flow:
- Tier 1 | A commenter who holds a buying title at an ICP account | chips: comment, buying title, ICP | tag: high touch
- Tier 2 | A reactor in the buying seat at an ICP account, or a commenter below the seat with the buyer attached | chips: reaction, ICP, buyer attached
- Tier 3 | Acceptable fit, any engagement type | chips: acceptable fit | tag: email only
= Stack and store | Stack with other signals per core tiering; post URL, topic class, competitor and engagement type written to the CRM record | with: Claude, Attio, HubSpot | chips: post URL, topic class, competitor, engagement type

- Tier 1: a commenter with a buying title at an ICP account. Tier 2: a reactor in the buying seat at an ICP account, or a commenter below the seat with the buyer found. Tier 3: acceptable fit.
- Apply core: tiering. Two engagements on the same competitor inside 30 days, or an engagement plus another dated signal, moves the row up one tier.
- Write post URL, topic class, competitor, engagement type and date to the CRM record with the tier.

### 6. Write the switch pitch on the post topic
Tools: Claude
Flow:
- Topic to angle | Launch: how your approach compares on the thing they launched. How-to: the same job done simpler. Pricing: the cost of staying. Opinion: agree and extend | with: Claude | chips: launch, how-to, pricing, opinion
= Switch offer | A concrete reason to leave, required for Tier 1: migration done for them, a parallel run beside the incumbent, a buyout of the remaining term | with: Claude | chips: migration, parallel run, buyout, {TOPIC}, {COMPANY}

- Apply core: copy and send rules.
- Open on the topic, never on the competitor's post or the engagement. Name it the way a peer in that seat would.
- Launch post: how your approach compares on the thing they launched, one specific difference. How-to post: the same job done simpler. Pricing post: the cost of staying another year. Opinion post: agree with the point and extend it into what you do differently.
- Tier 1 requires a switch offer in the first message: migration done for them, a parallel run, or a buyout of the remaining term. Features alone do not move anyone off a vendor they already pay.
- Draft one email variant per topic class plus a threaded nudge for email 2, and one DM under 300 characters per topic. Show the user before upload. Variables: `{TOPIC}`, `{COMPANY}`, `{TITLE}`.

### 7. Multi-thread and launch
Tools: HeyReach, LinkedIn, EmailBison, Instantly (option), Slack, Attio
Flow:
- Day 0 | Connection request and DM to the engager from a real profile, inside 48 hours of the engagement or not at all | with: HeyReach, LinkedIn | chips: connection, DM, under 48 hours
- Day 3 | Two-step email to the engager and one or two colleagues in the buying seat, inside 7 days of the engagement | with: EmailBison, Instantly | chips: engager, 1 to 2 colleagues, under 7 days | tag: send
= Reply routing | Slack alert on reply with the post URL, topic class and competitor; stop on a reply, a booking or an open deal | with: Slack, Attio | chips: post URL, topic, competitor

- Day 0: a connection request with a short note and, on accept, a DM on the topic, from a real person's profile through HeyReach. Inside 48 hours of the engagement or skip the DM.
- Day 3: a two-step email to the engager plus one or two colleagues in the buying seat, from this play's own inbox set, email 1 inside 7 days of the engagement.
- Slack on every reply with the post URL, topic class and competitor. Stop on a reply, a booking or an open deal.

### 8. Report
Tools: Attio, Slack
Flow:
= Weekly by competitor voice and topic class | Engagers captured, qualified rate, connection accept rate, replies, meetings | with: Attio, Slack | chips: by voice, by topic

- Weekly: engagers captured, qualified rate, connection accept rate, replies, meetings, split by competitor voice and topic class, against the cold baseline.
- After a month, cut voices whose engagers rarely pass the gates and lean the copy toward the topics that book.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- Never say you saw them engage. No "saw your comment", no "noticed you liked".
- Skip posts about layoffs, personal news or grief, and every engager on them.
- Competitor staff are never contacted, nor their partners or agencies.
- One person at most once per 60 days across all social plays.
- Scope: this play is engagers on posts by competitor voices (their company page, founders, execs, creators who post for them). Engagers on your own, your team's or chosen creators' posts belong to Content Engagement; posts about your brand, competitors or the category pain written by third parties belong to Brand and Keyword Mentions.
- DM window 48 hours, email window 7 days. An older engagement is stored for stacking, never named in copy.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-competitor-post-engagers for our sales engagement platform, B2B SaaS 50 to 500 people in North America, buyers VP Sales and Head of RevOps, competitors the four we lose to most"

1. Asked for the four competitors and the switch offer (migration in 10 business days plus a parallel run); confirmed Trigify, Attio, EmailBison, HeyReach and Slack.
2. Watch list: 4 company pages plus 17 people, 21 voices.
3. Week one: 46 posts, 2,140 engagements (1,880 reactions, 260 comments), 1,712 people; 9 launch, 14 how-to, 3 pricing, 12 opinion, 5 hiring, 3 other.
4. Gates: 214 removed as competitor staff, partners or recruiters; 611 at ICP-fit companies; 188 in the buying seat plus 97 below it; 23 out at the CRM gate; 262 through.
5. 231 emails found by LinkedIn URL, 84 buyers attached to below-seat engagers; 289 valid, 14 catch-all in their own campaign.
6. 31 Tier 1, 143 Tier 2, 88 Tier 3.
7. 174 connection requests inside 48 hours, 71 accepted, 64 DMs. 303 addresses loaded into "Competitor Engagers, November", four topic variants approved.
8. First weekly read: 19 replies, 7 interested, 4 meetings, 3 from how-to and pricing posts by one competitor's founder.

Everyone who reacts to or comments on a post in your topic just told you they are in the topic, and the ones who did it on your own or your team's posts already know your name. So the first touch is a warm DM from the profile they engaged with, continuing the conversation the post started, never a cold email and never a thank-you for the like. The post opened the conversation; this play carries it on.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1 and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it.
  2. The seat that buys: exact titles and functions, with the seniority floor.
  3. The source list: your profile, the team members who have agreed, 5 to 10 creators your buyers follow, and the target accounts if the ABM option is on.
  4. Stack answers from core section 1, plus the capture tool and whether the website runs RB2B or Warmly.
  5. The three to five topics you post on, in the words buyers use on calls.

Steps

1Pick the profiles and posts to watch

ToolsLinkedInGoogle SheetsAttio
  • List your own profile and every team member's profile. Each team member says yes in writing first, because their profile sends the DMs in step 6.
  • Choose 5 to 10 creators your buyers follow. Count ICP titles in the comments of three recent posts before adding anyone.
  • ABM option: add the execs at your target accounts. The owner engages first; the play catches who else does.
  • Store the list with an owner per profile; the owner signs every DM from that source.

2Capture engagement daily

ToolsTrigger.devTrigify (option)Apify (option)Teamfluence (option)Google Sheets
  • A daily job finds new posts on every watched profile and pulls each reaction, comment and repost with the comment text. Trigger.dev is the orchestration we run; Trigify, Apify or Teamfluence are capture options when the user already has one.
  • Weight it: comment above repost above reaction. Store weight, post URL, topic and date, one row per person per post.
  • Keep the comment text. A question or a disagreement is the best opening line you will get.

3CRM check and qualify

ToolsAttioHubSpot (option)ClaudeOcean.ioApollo
  • Apply core: exclusions. Also remove competitors, creators, agencies, recruiters and your own team; they engage on every post and none of them buy.
  • A model scores persona and company fit from the headline, website and headcount, with a one-line reason each. Below the floor, the row is kept for reporting only.
  • When the engager sits below the buying seat, map the account to its decision maker, champion and user and add the buyer. The engager stays on the record as the way in.

4Stack signals

ToolsAttioApolloRB2B (option)Warmly (option)Claude
  • Count engagements per person across all watched profiles in 30 days. Three or more is Tier 1 on its own.
  • Add the other dated signals: open roles in the function you sell to, a round in the last 90 days, a site visit where RB2B or Warmly is installed, any prior reply.
  • Apply core: tiering. Two dated signals in a quarter earn a call; engagement counts as one.

5Enrich and verify

ToolsLeadMagicProspeoMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • The LinkedIn URL is the key on every row. The DM lane needs nothing else.
  • For the email lane, apply core: enrichment and core: verification. A qualified engager with no email still gets the DM lane.

6Run the warm DM sequence

ToolsHeyReachLinkedInClaude
  • Load each person into a HeyReach campaign sent from the profile they engaged with: a connection request, then three touches over ten days (a related point, a resource, a question), all on the topic of the post.
  • Every message continues the topic as a peer would. Never mention the like, comment or repost. Creators' engagers never hear the creator's name.
  • ABM option: the owner comments on the exec's post first, by hand; the connection request goes out two days later.
  • Stop on a reply, a booked call or an open deal.

7Launch the email lane and Tier 1 touches

ToolsSlackLinkedInPhoneClaudeEmailBisonInstantly (option)HeyReach
  • Tier 1: a Slack alert with the person, topic, comment text and engagement count. The profile owner sends a personal note, then calls within three days.
  • Tier 2 and 3: an always-on two-email campaign per topic from its own inbox set, with the LinkedIn connection the same day as email 1.
  • Apply core: copy and send rules. Email 1 names the topic in the first sentence with one direct ask; email 2 is a threaded nudge. Show drafts before upload.
  • Write every contacted person to the CRM with play, source profile, topic, tier and date.

8Report

ToolsAttioSlackGoogle Sheets
  • Weekly, by source profile and topic: engagers, qualified rate, connection accept rate, DM reply rate, email reply rate, meetings.
  • Send the topics that produce buyers back to whoever plans the content. Buyers in the funnel earn a topic more posts; peers and vendors earn it fewer.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • The first touch comes from the profile they engaged with, never from a cold inbox set. The email lane follows, from inboxes that belong to this play.
  • Never reference the like, the comment or the repost. They should feel they met you in the thread, not that they were logged.
  • A team member's profile is watched and sends DMs only with their written consent, which they can withdraw at any time.
  • Never name the creator. Their engagers hear about the topic, not whose post it was.
  • One person is contacted at most once per 60 days across all social plays; check the CRM before loading anyone.
  • Scope: this play covers engagers on your own, your team's and your chosen creators' posts, plus the ABM option on execs at target accounts. Engagers on competitor voices' posts belong to Competitor Post Engagers; third-party posts about your brand, competitors or category pain belong to Brand and Keyword Mentions.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-content-engagement for our outbound agency, B2B SaaS 50 to 500 people in the US, seats VP Sales, CRO, VP Marketing and Head of RevOps, three team profiles and eight creators"

  5. 790 rows keyed on LinkedIn URL for the DM lane; 561 emails found, 498 verified valid, 29 catch-all in their own campaign.
  6. 790 loaded into HeyReach across three owner profiles; 61% connection accept, 14% DM reply rate by week three.
  7. 118 Tier 1 Slack alerts, 71 personal notes, 38 calls completed. 672 Tier 2 and 3 into four topic campaigns; 7.1% email reply rate.
  8. 26 meetings in month one; the outbound infrastructure topic produced 14 and earned two more posts a week.

# Content Engagement

Everyone who reacts to or comments on a post in your topic just told you they are in the topic, and the ones who did it on your own or your team's posts already know your name. So the first touch is a warm DM from the profile they engaged with, continuing the conversation the post started, never a cold email and never a thank-you for the like. The post opened the conversation; this play carries it on.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1 and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it.
2. The seat that buys: exact titles and functions, with the seniority floor.
3. The source list: your profile, the team members who have agreed, 5 to 10 creators your buyers follow, and the target accounts if the ABM option is on.
4. Stack answers from core section 1, plus the capture tool and whether the website runs RB2B or Warmly.
5. The three to five topics you post on, in the words buyers use on calls.

## Steps

### 1. Pick the profiles and posts to watch
Tools: LinkedIn, Google Sheets, Attio
Flow:
- Own and team profiles | Your profile plus each team member who has agreed; their profile sends the DMs, so consent is recorded before anything is watched | with: LinkedIn | chips: own profile, team profiles, consent on file
- Creators your buyers follow | 5 to 10 creators whose comment sections are full of your ICP and who post at least weekly | with: LinkedIn | chips: 5 to 10 creators, audience overlap, posts weekly
- ABM option | The execs at your target accounts; the owner engages on their posts first and the play catches who else shows up | with: LinkedIn, Attio | tag: ABM option
= Source list with an owner | One row per watched profile with its type, owner, topics and start date | with: Google Sheets, Attio | chips: profile URL, type, owner, topics

- List your own profile and every team member's profile. Each team member says yes in writing first, because their profile sends the DMs in step 6.
- Choose 5 to 10 creators your buyers follow. Count ICP titles in the comments of three recent posts before adding anyone.
- ABM option: add the execs at your target accounts. The owner engages first; the play catches who else does.
- Store the list with an owner per profile; the owner signs every DM from that source.

### 2. Capture engagement daily
Tools: Trigger.dev, Trigify (option), Apify (option), Teamfluence (option), Google Sheets
Flow:
- Daily pull per watched profile | New posts on every watched profile, then every reaction, comment and repost on each post with the comment text | with: Trigger.dev, Trigify, Apify, Teamfluence | chips: reaction, comment, repost, comment text
= Weight and store | Comments above reposts above reactions, one row per person per post with the post topic and date | with: Trigger.dev, Google Sheets | chips: weight, post URL, topic, date

- A daily job finds new posts on every watched profile and pulls each reaction, comment and repost with the comment text. Trigger.dev is the orchestration we run; Trigify, Apify or Teamfluence are capture options when the user already has one.
- Weight it: comment above repost above reaction. Store weight, post URL, topic and date, one row per person per post.
- Keep the comment text. A question or a disagreement is the best opening line you will get.

### 3. CRM check and qualify
Tools: Attio, HubSpot (option), Claude, Ocean.io, Apollo
Flow:
- Exclusions | Core exclusions, then competitors, creators, agencies, recruiters and your own team | with: Attio, HubSpot | tag: core: exclusions
- Persona and company fit | A model reads the headline, company page and website and scores persona fit and company fit with one reason each | with: Claude, Ocean.io, Apollo | chips: persona fit, company fit, verdict
= Map to the buyer | When the engager sits below the buying seat, find the decision maker, champion and user at the account and add the buyer | with: Apollo, Claude | chips: decision maker, champion, user

- Apply core: exclusions. Also remove competitors, creators, agencies, recruiters and your own team; they engage on every post and none of them buy.
- A model scores persona and company fit from the headline, website and headcount, with a one-line reason each. Below the floor, the row is kept for reporting only.
- When the engager sits below the buying seat, map the account to its decision maker, champion and user and add the buyer. The engager stays on the record as the way in.

### 4. Stack signals
Tools: Attio, Apollo, RB2B (option), Warmly (option), Claude
Flow:
- Engagement count in 30 days | How many watched posts this person engaged with in the last 30 days; three or more is Tier 1 on its own | with: Attio | chips: 1, 2, 3 or more
- Other dated signals | Hiring, funding, website visits where RB2B or Warmly is on the site, prior replies to your emails | with: Apollo, RB2B, Warmly, Attio | chips: hiring, funding, site visit, prior reply
= Tier | Two dated signals in a quarter earn a call; the rest tier by fit | with: Claude, Attio | tag: core: tiering

- Count engagements per person across all watched profiles in 30 days. Three or more is Tier 1 on its own.
- Add the other dated signals: open roles in the function you sell to, a round in the last 90 days, a site visit where RB2B or Warmly is installed, any prior reply.
- Apply core: tiering. Two dated signals in a quarter earn a call; engagement counts as one.

### 5. Enrich and verify
Tools: LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- LinkedIn URL as the key | Every row is keyed on the profile URL; the DM lane sends on that alone, the email lane runs the waterfall | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo | tag: core: enrichment
- Two-pass verification | Email-lane rows only; valid to the main campaign, catch-all to its own | with: MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: verification

- The LinkedIn URL is the key on every row. The DM lane needs nothing else.
- For the email lane, apply core: enrichment and core: verification. A qualified engager with no email still gets the DM lane.

### 6. Run the warm DM sequence
Tools: HeyReach, LinkedIn, Claude
Flow:
- From the profile they engaged with | Connection request, then three DMs over ten days, each sent from the owner of the watched profile | with: HeyReach | chips: day 0 connect, day 3 point, day 6 resource, day 10 question
- ABM option | The owner comments on the exec's post first; the DM lands two days later | with: LinkedIn, HeyReach | tag: ABM option
= Copy continues the topic | A related point, a resource, a question, all on the topic of the post; the engagement itself is never mentioned | with: Claude | chips: {TOPIC}, related point, resource, question

- Load each person into a HeyReach campaign sent from the profile they engaged with: a connection request, then three touches over ten days (a related point, a resource, a question), all on the topic of the post.
- Every message continues the topic as a peer would. Never mention the like, comment or repost. Creators' engagers never hear the creator's name.
- ABM option: the owner comments on the exec's post first, by hand; the connection request goes out two days later.
- Stop on a reply, a booked call or an open deal.

### 7. Launch the email lane and Tier 1 touches
Tools: Slack, LinkedIn, Phone, Claude, EmailBison, Instantly (option), HeyReach
Flow:
- Tier 1 | Slack alert with the comment text and topic, a personal note from the profile owner, then a call | with: Slack, LinkedIn, Phone | chips: name, title, company, topic, comment text, engagement count | tag: high touch
- Tier 2 and 3 | An always-on two-email campaign that opens on the topic, from its own inbox set, with the LinkedIn connection the same day | with: Claude, EmailBison, Instantly, HeyReach | chips: {TOPIC}, {FIRST_NAME}, {COMPANY} | tag: at volume

- Tier 1: a Slack alert with the person, topic, comment text and engagement count. The profile owner sends a personal note, then calls within three days.
- Tier 2 and 3: an always-on two-email campaign per topic from its own inbox set, with the LinkedIn connection the same day as email 1.
- Apply core: copy and send rules. Email 1 names the topic in the first sentence with one direct ask; email 2 is a threaded nudge. Show drafts before upload.
- Write every contacted person to the CRM with play, source profile, topic, tier and date.

### 8. Report
Tools: Attio, Slack, Google Sheets
Flow:
= Weekly read by source profile and topic | Engagers, qualified rate, connection accept rate, DM reply rate, email reply rate, meetings; the topics that produce buyers go back to whoever plans the content | with: Attio, Slack, Google Sheets

- Weekly, by source profile and topic: engagers, qualified rate, connection accept rate, DM reply rate, email reply rate, meetings.
- Send the topics that produce buyers back to whoever plans the content. Buyers in the funnel earn a topic more posts; peers and vendors earn it fewer.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- The first touch comes from the profile they engaged with, never from a cold inbox set. The email lane follows, from inboxes that belong to this play.
- Never reference the like, the comment or the repost. They should feel they met you in the thread, not that they were logged.
- A team member's profile is watched and sends DMs only with their written consent, which they can withdraw at any time.
- Never name the creator. Their engagers hear about the topic, not whose post it was.
- One person is contacted at most once per 60 days across all social plays; check the CRM before loading anyone.
- Scope: this play covers engagers on your own, your team's and your chosen creators' posts, plus the ABM option on execs at target accounts. Engagers on competitor voices' posts belong to Competitor Post Engagers; third-party posts about your brand, competitors or category pain belong to Brand and Keyword Mentions.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-content-engagement for our outbound agency, B2B SaaS 50 to 500 people in the US, seats VP Sales, CRO, VP Marketing and Head of RevOps, three team profiles and eight creators"

1. Consent from three team members; eight creators checked, seven kept. 10 watched profiles with owners, 4 topics.
2. Trigger.dev pulled 61 posts in month one: 4,870 engagements from 3,240 people; 712 comments, 418 reposts, 3,740 reactions.
3. 2,980 after core exclusions, 410 more removed as competitors, creators, agencies, recruiters and team. 1,140 at ICP-fit companies, 620 in the seat, 190 below it mapped to 170 buyers.
4. 86 people engaged three or more times in 30 days. Stacked with 44 hiring signals, 12 rounds and 31 RB2B site visits: 118 Tier 1, 390 Tier 2, 282 Tier 3.
5. 790 rows keyed on LinkedIn URL for the DM lane; 561 emails found, 498 verified valid, 29 catch-all in their own campaign.
6. 790 loaded into HeyReach across three owner profiles; 61% connection accept, 14% DM reply rate by week three.
7. 118 Tier 1 Slack alerts, 71 personal notes, 38 calls completed. 672 Tier 2 and 3 into four topic campaigns; 7.1% email reply rate.
8. 26 meetings in month one; the outbound infrastructure topic produced 14 and earned two more posts a week.

Play 26 · Social signals

Brand and Keyword Mentions

Trigger A public post naming your brand, a competitor or a category pain phrase
Outcome Public reply first, then enriched outreach
Channels linkedin, email, call

Give this to your agent 
Install the Pipeline.tech "Brand and Keyword Mentions" outbound play. Fetch
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/core/SKILL.md and
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/plays/brand-keyword-mentions/SKILL.md,
save them as ~/.claude/skills/pipeline-outbound-core/SKILL.md and
~/.claude/skills/pipeline-brand-keyword-mentions/SKILL.md, then run /pipeline-brand-keyword-mentions.
Paste into Claude Code. It installs the shared core rules plus this play, then runs it as /pipeline-brand-keyword-mentions.
Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

Someone discussing your category, your competitor or you in public is warmer than any cold list, because they raised the topic themselves where their peers can see. The first move is a useful public reply from a real person, since that thread is public proof that you know the problem. The private follow-up two days later is then a continuation, not an interruption.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it.
  2. Brand, product and founder names, and the three to five competitors that come up on sales calls.
  3. Who replies publicly, with LinkedIn profile URLs and the hours they cover.
  4. Stack answers from core section 1, plus one resource per category pain (a post, a template, a short video) shareable without a form.

Steps

1Define the keyword sets and the exclusion list

ToolsClaudeGoogle Sheets
  • Write three sets in a sheet. Brand: company, products, misspellings people actually type, founder names. Competitors: each name alone and paired with "alternative", "vs", "switching from" and "anyone using". Category pain: 5 to 10 phrases from call transcripts, in the buyer's words.
  • Write the exclusion list next to them: listicle and roundup patterns, job posts, your own team, competitor employees, known partners. The classifier in step 3 reads it.
  • Monthly, a model proposes additions and retirements from the posts captured since the last review; the user approves every change.

2Capture posts, authors and engagers daily

ToolsTrigifyLinkedInApify (option)Teamfluence (option)Serper (option)Google Sheets
  • Run the three sets as daily keyword feeds in Trigify; Apify or Teamfluence cover the same job on LinkedIn if the user already has one. Serper searches X, Reddit and blogs with the same sets.
  • For every post store the URL, text, date, author, and each engager with what they did: reaction type or comment text. The comment is often the real signal, so never store only a count. One row per post and one per engager, keyed to the post URL.

3Classify every post

ToolsClaudeSlack
  • A model reads each post against the exclusion list and labels it: brand mention (positive, negative or neutral), competitor complaint or switching intent, category pain, or noise. Noise and excluded authors are dropped and logged.
  • A negative brand mention goes to the owner in Slack with the post and author. It is handled as support by whoever owns that relationship and never loaded into a sequence.
  • Store classification and sentiment on the post row; engagers inherit both.

4Public reply first, from a human

ToolsSlackLinkedInClaude
  • Every kept post lands in a Slack channel with the classification, the author and a one-line suggested angle: a question to answer, a resource that fits, or a detail worth adding.
  • A named person from the inputs replies in the thread from their own profile within hours, while the post is live. The reply answers or adds something specific, never pitches and never argues; when a mention gets a fact wrong, the correction is one calm sentence or nothing.
  • Log the reply text and time on the post row. Nothing private goes out until it is filled.

5Enrich the author and the engagers

ToolsOcean.ioApolloClaudeLeadMagicProspeoMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • Resolve each author and engager to a company, check headcount, industry and location, and get a yes or no ICP verdict from the website. Apply core: exclusions.
  • When the author is not the buyer, keep them as the conversation and map the account to its buying seat per core section 4. The author gets the reply and the DM; the buyer gets the email.
  • Apply core: enrichment, then core: verification.

6Tier and route

ToolsClaudeAttioHubSpot (option)Slack
  • Tier 1: the author of a competitor complaint or category-pain post at an ICP account. The person who replied publicly sends the connection and DM on day 2, and calls if they answer.
  • Tier 2: engagers at ICP accounts and the mapped buyer. HeyReach DM or two-step email on the post topic.
  • Tier 3: acceptable fit, email only.
  • Write every person to the CRM with play name, keyword set, sentiment, post URL, public reply and tier.

7Write and launch

ToolsClaudeHeyReachEmailBisonInstantly (option)Phone
  • Apply core: copy and send rules.
  • Every private message continues the public thread: name their point, add one thing the public reply did not cover, make one ask. Three variants by class. A competitor complaint gets your take on the problem behind it; the switch offer comes only after they reply. A category-pain post gets the matching resource, no form. A positive brand mention gets a thank-you and nothing else unless they ask.
  • Never "I saw you mentioned us" on anything negative; the public reply is why you are in touch.
  • Show the three variants plus one threaded nudge to the user before upload. Upload with {POST_TOPIC}, {COMPETITOR}, {PAIN_PHRASE}, {RESOURCE}; sends start on day 2 from the play's own inbox set. Stop on a reply, a booking or an open deal.

8Report

ToolsAttioSlackClaude
  • Weekly, by keyword set and sentiment: posts captured, kept, replied publicly, Tier 1 count, DM and email reply rate, meetings.
  • Retire phrases whose posts are mostly noise and add the ones buyers actually use; both feed the monthly review in step 1.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • The public reply comes before any private touch. No DM, email or call until the reply is logged on the post.
  • A negative brand mention is a support issue, not a lead. It goes to the owner and never into a sequence.
  • Never argue in public and never pitch in the public reply. It helps or it stays unwritten.
  • One person at most once per 60 days across all social plays.
  • Scope: third-party posts about your brand, your competitors or the category pain, by people who are not you and not the competitor. Engagers on competitor voices' posts belong to Competitor Post Engagers, engagers on your own or your creators' posts to Content Engagement, review-site and stack data to Competitor Complaints.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-brand-keyword-mentions for our outbound agency, B2B SaaS 50 to 500 people in the US, five competitors, two of us can reply publicly"

  1. 6 brand terms, 25 competitor phrases, 8 category-pain phrases, a 14-pattern exclusion list; confirmed Trigify, Ocean.io, Attio, HeyReach, EmailBison and Slack.
  2. Month one: 1,140 posts captured, 612 on LinkedIn and 528 across X, Reddit and blogs; 3,870 engagers stored with reaction or comment.
  3. 41 brand mentions (29 positive, 8 neutral, 4 negative routed to the owner), 96 competitor complaints or switching intent, 203 category pain, 800 noise or excluded.
  4. 340 posts to Slack, 318 replied publicly, median 3 hours; 22 skipped as too old or already answered.
  5. 97 authors and 412 engagers at ICP accounts after exclusions; 38 accounts mapped to a buying seat; 489 addresses found, 441 verified valid, 23 catch-all in a separate campaign.
  6. 71 Tier 1 authors, 450 Tier 2, 118 Tier 3; all written to Attio with keyword set, sentiment and post URL.
  7. Three variants approved; 71 DMs on day 2, 31 replies, 9 calls; 568 emails, 61 replies; 14 meetings booked.
  8. Retired 2 pain phrases that pulled job posts, added 3 that appeared in 40 kept posts.

---
name: pipeline-brand-keyword-mentions
description: Pipeline.tech play. Turn public posts about your brand, your competitors or the category pain you solve into conversations, starting with a useful public reply from a real person. Defines brand, competitor and category-pain keyword sets with an exclusion list, captures matching posts with their authors and engagers daily, classifies each post by type and sentiment with a model, routes negative brand mentions to support, replies publicly first, then enriches the author and engagers, tiers them and runs a connection and DM for Tier 1 and a two-step email on the post topic for the rest. USE WHEN user says "/pipeline-brand-keyword-mentions" OR "brand mentions play" OR "keyword mentions" OR "social listening outreach" OR "competitor mention outreach" OR "who is talking about us" OR "reach people posting about our category".
author: Pipeline.tech (https://pipeline.tech/plays/brand-keyword-mentions)
version: 0.1.0
requires: pipeline-outbound-core
trigger: A public post naming your brand, a competitor or a category pain phrase
outcome: Public reply first, then enriched outreach
category: social
channels: linkedin, email, call

# Brand and Keyword Mentions

Someone discussing your category, your competitor or you in public is warmer than any cold list, because they raised the topic themselves where their peers can see. The first move is a useful public reply from a real person, since that thread is public proof that you know the problem. The private follow-up two days later is then a continuation, not an interruption.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it.
2. Brand, product and founder names, and the three to five competitors that come up on sales calls.
3. Who replies publicly, with LinkedIn profile URLs and the hours they cover.
4. Stack answers from core section 1, plus one resource per category pain (a post, a template, a short video) shareable without a form.

## Steps

### 1. Define the keyword sets and the exclusion list
Tools: Claude, Google Sheets
Flow:
- Brand set | Company, product, common misspellings and founder names | with: Claude, Google Sheets | chips: company, product, misspellings, founders
- Competitor set | Each competitor name paired with alternative, vs, switching from and anyone using | with: Claude, Google Sheets | chips: alternative, vs, switching from, anyone using
- Category pain set | 5 to 10 phrases in the words buyers use on calls, not the words on your website | with: Claude | chips: 5 to 10 phrases, buyer language
= Exclusion list | Listicles, roundups, job posts, your own team, competitor staff and partners; a model expands and reviews every set monthly | with: Claude, Google Sheets | tag: monthly review

- Write three sets in a sheet. Brand: company, products, misspellings people actually type, founder names. Competitors: each name alone and paired with "alternative", "vs", "switching from" and "anyone using". Category pain: 5 to 10 phrases from call transcripts, in the buyer's words.
- Write the exclusion list next to them: listicle and roundup patterns, job posts, your own team, competitor employees, known partners. The classifier in step 3 reads it.
- Monthly, a model proposes additions and retirements from the posts captured since the last review; the user approves every change.

### 2. Capture posts, authors and engagers daily
Tools: Trigify, LinkedIn, Apify (option), Teamfluence (option), Serper (option), Google Sheets
Flow:
- LinkedIn keyword feed | Every post matching a set, with its author, captured each morning | with: Trigify, LinkedIn, Apify, Teamfluence | chips: post URL, text, date, author
- Engagers per post | Everyone who reacted or commented on each kept post, with the reaction or comment itself | with: Trigify, Apify | chips: reaction, comment, profile URL
- Beyond LinkedIn | Searches across X, Reddit and blogs for the same sets, weekly or daily by volume | with: Serper | tag: option

- Run the three sets as daily keyword feeds in Trigify; Apify or Teamfluence cover the same job on LinkedIn if the user already has one. Serper searches X, Reddit and blogs with the same sets.
- For every post store the URL, text, date, author, and each engager with what they did: reaction type or comment text. The comment is often the real signal, so never store only a count. One row per post and one per engager, keyed to the post URL.

### 3. Classify every post
Tools: Claude, Slack
Flow:
- Model classification | Brand mention split by sentiment, competitor complaint or switching intent, category pain, or noise; excluded authors dropped | with: Claude | chips: positive, negative, neutral, complaint, switching, category pain, noise
= Negative brand mention | Routed to the owner as a support matter with the post and the author, and never enters outreach | with: Slack | tag: support not lead

- A model reads each post against the exclusion list and labels it: brand mention (positive, negative or neutral), competitor complaint or switching intent, category pain, or noise. Noise and excluded authors are dropped and logged.
- A negative brand mention goes to the owner in Slack with the post and author. It is handled as support by whoever owns that relationship and never loaded into a sequence.
- Store classification and sentiment on the post row; engagers inherit both.

### 4. Public reply first, from a human
Tools: Slack, LinkedIn, Claude
Flow:
- Slack post per kept mention | Post text, classification, author and a suggested angle for the reply | with: Slack, Claude | chips: classification, angle, author, post URL
= A named person replies in the thread | From their real profile within hours: helpful, specific, no pitch, no argument; the reply is logged with the post | with: LinkedIn | tag: human only

- Every kept post lands in a Slack channel with the classification, the author and a one-line suggested angle: a question to answer, a resource that fits, or a detail worth adding.
- A named person from the inputs replies in the thread from their own profile within hours, while the post is live. The reply answers or adds something specific, never pitches and never argues; when a mention gets a fact wrong, the correction is one calm sentence or nothing.
- Log the reply text and time on the post row. Nothing private goes out until it is filled.

### 5. Enrich the author and the engagers
Tools: Ocean.io, Apollo, Claude, LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- ICP check | Headcount, industry and location on the author's and each engager's company, then a yes or no verdict from the website | with: Ocean.io, Apollo, Claude | tag: core: exclusions
- Map to the buying seat | When the author is not the buyer, find the person who owns the problem at the same account | with: Apollo, Claude | chips: author, buying seat, same account
= Email waterfall and two-pass verification | Work address for every kept person, checked twice | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: enrichment and verification

- Resolve each author and engager to a company, check headcount, industry and location, and get a yes or no ICP verdict from the website. Apply core: exclusions.
- When the author is not the buyer, keep them as the conversation and map the account to its buying seat per core section 4. The author gets the reply and the DM; the buyer gets the email.
- Apply core: enrichment, then core: verification.

### 6. Tier and route
Tools: Claude, Attio, HubSpot (option), Slack
Flow:
- Tier 1 | Author of a competitor complaint or category-pain post at an ICP account: connection and DM on day 2 from the person who replied, a call if they answer | with: Slack, Attio | chips: author, ICP account, day 2, call on reply | tag: high touch
- Tier 2 | Engagers at ICP accounts, plus the mapped buyer: DM or two-step email on the topic | with: Attio | chips: engagers, mapped buyer, topic
- Tier 3 | Acceptable fit, email only | with: Attio | tag: at volume
= Write to the CRM | Keyword set, classification, sentiment, post URL, public reply and tier on every person | with: Attio, HubSpot | chips: keyword set, sentiment, post URL, tier

- Tier 1: the author of a competitor complaint or category-pain post at an ICP account. The person who replied publicly sends the connection and DM on day 2, and calls if they answer.
- Tier 2: engagers at ICP accounts and the mapped buyer. HeyReach DM or two-step email on the post topic.
- Tier 3: acceptable fit, email only.
- Write every person to the CRM with play name, keyword set, sentiment, post URL, public reply and tier.

### 7. Write and launch
Tools: Claude, HeyReach, EmailBison, Instantly (option), Phone
Flow:
- Continue the thread | Every private message picks up the topic of the public post, never your product | with: Claude | chips: {POST_TOPIC}, {COMPETITOR}, {PAIN_PHRASE}, {RESOURCE}
- Three angles by class | Competitor complaint gets your take on the problem; category pain gets a resource; positive brand mention gets a thank-you and nothing else | with: Claude | tag: variants
= Launch from its own inbox set | Tier 1 DM on day 2 and a call on reply; Tier 2 and 3 email from day 2, stop on a reply, a booking or an open deal | with: HeyReach, EmailBison, Instantly, Phone | tag: send

- Apply core: copy and send rules.
- Every private message continues the public thread: name their point, add one thing the public reply did not cover, make one ask. Three variants by class. A competitor complaint gets your take on the problem behind it; the switch offer comes only after they reply. A category-pain post gets the matching resource, no form. A positive brand mention gets a thank-you and nothing else unless they ask.
- Never "I saw you mentioned us" on anything negative; the public reply is why you are in touch.
- Show the three variants plus one threaded nudge to the user before upload. Upload with `{POST_TOPIC}`, `{COMPETITOR}`, `{PAIN_PHRASE}`, `{RESOURCE}`; sends start on day 2 from the play's own inbox set. Stop on a reply, a booking or an open deal.

### 8. Report
Tools: Attio, Slack, Claude
Flow:
= Weekly read by keyword set and sentiment | Posts captured, kept, replied publicly, Tier 1 count, DM and email reply rate, meetings; noisy phrases retired and buyer phrases added | with: Attio, Slack, Claude

- Weekly, by keyword set and sentiment: posts captured, kept, replied publicly, Tier 1 count, DM and email reply rate, meetings.
- Retire phrases whose posts are mostly noise and add the ones buyers actually use; both feed the monthly review in step 1.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- The public reply comes before any private touch. No DM, email or call until the reply is logged on the post.
- A negative brand mention is a support issue, not a lead. It goes to the owner and never into a sequence.
- Never argue in public and never pitch in the public reply. It helps or it stays unwritten.
- One person at most once per 60 days across all social plays.
- Scope: third-party posts about your brand, your competitors or the category pain, by people who are not you and not the competitor. Engagers on competitor voices' posts belong to Competitor Post Engagers, engagers on your own or your creators' posts to Content Engagement, review-site and stack data to Competitor Complaints.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-brand-keyword-mentions for our outbound agency, B2B SaaS 50 to 500 people in the US, five competitors, two of us can reply publicly"

1. 6 brand terms, 25 competitor phrases, 8 category-pain phrases, a 14-pattern exclusion list; confirmed Trigify, Ocean.io, Attio, HeyReach, EmailBison and Slack.
2. Month one: 1,140 posts captured, 612 on LinkedIn and 528 across X, Reddit and blogs; 3,870 engagers stored with reaction or comment.
3. 41 brand mentions (29 positive, 8 neutral, 4 negative routed to the owner), 96 competitor complaints or switching intent, 203 category pain, 800 noise or excluded.
4. 340 posts to Slack, 318 replied publicly, median 3 hours; 22 skipped as too old or already answered.
5. 97 authors and 412 engagers at ICP accounts after exclusions; 38 accounts mapped to a buying seat; 489 addresses found, 441 verified valid, 23 catch-all in a separate campaign.
6. 71 Tier 1 authors, 450 Tier 2, 118 Tier 3; all written to Attio with keyword set, sentiment and post URL.
7. Three variants approved; 71 DMs on day 2, 31 replies, 9 calls; 568 emails, 61 replies; 14 meetings booked.
8. Retired 2 pain phrases that pulled job posts, added 3 that appeared in 40 kept posts.

Competitor Page Followers

Trigger A new follower on a competitor's LinkedIn company page since last week
Outcome Warm pipeline from their audience

Give this to your agent 
Install the Pipeline.tech "Competitor Page Followers" outbound play. Fetch
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/core/SKILL.md and
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/plays/competitor-page-followers/SKILL.md,
save them as ~/.claude/skills/pipeline-outbound-core/SKILL.md and
~/.claude/skills/pipeline-competitor-page-followers/SKILL.md, then run /pipeline-competitor-page-followers.
Paste into Claude Code. It installs the shared core rules plus this play, then runs it as /pipeline-competitor-page-followers.
Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

A new follow on a competitor's company page is a quiet hand-raise: the person wants to hear more from a vendor in your category and has not decided from whom. A weekly diff of the follower list catches them while they are still forming an opinion. On its own the follow is a weak signal, so this play stacks it with harder evidence before anyone picks up the phone.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve.
  2. The 3 to 6 competitors buyers name on sales calls, with company page URLs and any separate product page URLs.
  3. The seat that buys: exact titles and functions, with the seniority floor.
  4. Stack answers from core section 1, the real LinkedIn profile connection requests go out from, and whether RB2B runs on the website.
  5. Two offers in one sentence each: the evaluation offer for accounts with no tool detected, the switch offer with one proof point for accounts on the competitor.

Steps

1Pick the pages and take the baseline

ToolsLinkedInApifyGoogle Sheets
  • Pick the 3 to 6 competitors buyers name on sales calls. Where a competitor runs a separate page per product, add it; product page followers are closer to a decision.
  • Export the full follower list of every page once and store it with the page URL and the export date. Nothing in the baseline is contacted; it only gives next week's pull something to diff against. The LinkedIn URL is the row key from here on.

2Pull weekly and diff

ToolsApifyTeamfluence (option)ClaudeGoogle Sheets
  • Pull every page weekly, same day, same actor, same settings. Apify first; Teamfluence when the user already runs it.
  • Keep only URLs absent from last week's list, with the follow week and the page. The follow week is the signal date for core tiering, so it is never blank.
  • Dedupe by LinkedIn URL. Two pages followed in one week is one row with both pages. Unfollows are not a signal; drop them.

3Remove the noise

ToolsClaudeApolloAttioHubSpot (option)
  • Most new followers of a company page are its staff, alumni, partners, job seekers and students. Remove them first, reading past positions as well as the headline.
  • Then remove recruiters, vendors, and titles outside the buying functions from input 3. A non-buyer title at an ICP account stays only if step 4 can attach the buyer.
  • Apply core: exclusions. Log counts per bucket for step 8.

4Qualify company and persona

ToolsOcean.ioApolloClaude
  • Company first. Firmographics from Ocean.io or Apollo, then a model reads the website and returns a yes or no ICP verdict with one reason. A no stops here, before any paid lookup.
  • Persona second. A buying title at an ICP account is the lead. Below the seat, find the buyer at the same account per core section 4 and attach them; the follower stays as context, never as the recipient.

5Stack the signals

ToolsBuiltWithTheirStackApifyRB2B (option)Claude
  • Technographics set the angle. Competitor detected by BuiltWith or TheirStack means a switch angle; no detectable tool in the category means an evaluation angle.
  • Add every other dated signal: engagement on competitor posts, open roles in the buying function, a funding round, website visits where the user runs RB2B.
  • Apply core: tiering. Two dated signals in a quarter earn a call; the follow is one, a call needs one more.

6Enrich and verify

ToolsLeadMagicProspeoMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • Apply core: enrichment with the LinkedIn URL as the lookup, never name plus company; follower headlines lag job changes. Enrich the buyer attached in step 4 the same way.
  • Apply core: verification.

7Tier and route

ToolsClaudeSlackHeyReachLinkedInPhoneEmailBisonInstantly (option)AttioHubSpot (option)
  • Tier 1: buying title at an ICP account, follow plus one more dated signal. Slack alert to the owner, a connection request with a short note from a real profile, a call in week two if unanswered.
  • Tier 2: buying title at an ICP account, follow alone. Connection request plus a two-step email on the angle from step 5. No call.
  • Tier 3: acceptable fit. An always-on two-email campaign per angle.
  • Apply core: copy and send rules. Open on the evaluation or the switch in the words buyers use on calls. Never mention the follow. Variables: {COMPANY}, {TITLE}, {ANGLE}.
  • Write competitor page, follow week, angle, tier and ICP reason to the CRM record. Stop on a reply, a booking or an open deal.

8Report

ToolsAttioSlack
  • Weekly, per page: new followers, kept after step 3, ICP rate, tier counts, replies, meetings.
  • After a month the table shows which pages feed real pipeline and which only feed staff and students. Swap the second kind for a product page or another competitor.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • A follow is never the only reason for a call. A call needs the follow plus one more dated signal inside the quarter.
  • Never mention the follow. No "saw you started following", no "noticed you follow".
  • Competitor staff and alumni are never contacted, nor the competitor's partners or agencies. Check past positions too.
  • One person at most once per 60 days across all social plays.
  • A person who also reacted to or commented on a competitor post belongs to Competitor Post Engagers. The stronger signal wins; the follow is stored for stacking.
  • Scope: this play is new follows of competitor company and product pages. Reactions and comments on competitor voices' posts belong to Competitor Post Engagers; engagers on your own, your team's or chosen creators' posts belong to Content Engagement; third-party posts about your brand, competitors or the category belong to Brand and Keyword Mentions.
  • A follow older than 90 days is kept for stacking and never drives copy or a tier.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-competitor-page-followers for our contract management platform, mid-market legal and procurement teams in the US and UK, buyers General Counsel and Head of Procurement, competitors the three we see in every deal"

  1. Asked for the three competitors and both offers (evaluation: a side-by-side on their own contracts; switch: migration of the existing repository done for them). Confirmed Apify, Attio, EmailBison, HeyReach, Slack and RB2B.
  2. Page set: 3 company pages plus 1 product page. Baseline: 41,300 followers, stored with the date.
  3. Week two pull: 612 new follows, 598 after dedupe, 9 following two pages.
  4. Noise removal: 187 staff, alumni, partners and agencies; 94 recruiters, students and job seekers; 71 non-buyer titles; 12 out at the CRM gate. 234 through.
  5. 141 at ICP accounts: 58 in the buying seat, 47 below it with the buyer attached; 36 acceptable fit.
  6. Signals: 39 accounts on a competitor, 102 with no tool; 23 people with a second dated signal (14 post engagements, 6 open roles, 3 RB2B visits).
  7. 128 emails found by LinkedIn URL, 121 valid, 7 catch-all in their own campaign.
  8. 21 Tier 1, 84 Tier 2, 36 Tier 3. 105 connection requests, 44 accepted; 21 Slack alerts, 9 calls in week two. 121 addresses across the evaluation and switch campaigns.
  9. First weekly read: 11 replies, 5 interested, 3 meetings, 2 from the product page, which had the highest ICP rate. One company page fed 70 percent staff and students and goes under review.

# Competitor Page Followers

A new follow on a competitor's company page is a quiet hand-raise: the person wants to hear more from a vendor in your category and has not decided from whom. A weekly diff of the follower list catches them while they are still forming an opinion. On its own the follow is a weak signal, so this play stacks it with harder evidence before anyone picks up the phone.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve.
2. The 3 to 6 competitors buyers name on sales calls, with company page URLs and any separate product page URLs.
3. The seat that buys: exact titles and functions, with the seniority floor.
4. Stack answers from core section 1, the real LinkedIn profile connection requests go out from, and whether RB2B runs on the website.
5. Two offers in one sentence each: the evaluation offer for accounts with no tool detected, the switch offer with one proof point for accounts on the competitor.

## Steps

### 1. Pick the pages and take the baseline
Tools: LinkedIn, Apify, Google Sheets
Flow:
- Page set | 3 to 6 competitor company pages, plus separate product pages where the competitor runs them | with: LinkedIn | chips: company page, product page, named on calls
= Baseline export | The full follower list of every page pulled once and stored with the export date, so week one has something to diff against | with: Apify, Google Sheets | chips: full list, export date, per page

- Pick the 3 to 6 competitors buyers name on sales calls. Where a competitor runs a separate page per product, add it; product page followers are closer to a decision.
- Export the full follower list of every page once and store it with the page URL and the export date. Nothing in the baseline is contacted; it only gives next week's pull something to diff against. The LinkedIn URL is the row key from here on.

### 2. Pull weekly and diff
Tools: Apify, Teamfluence (option), Claude, Google Sheets
Flow:
- Weekly follower pull | Every page's follower list pulled on the same weekday, same actor, same settings | with: Apify, Teamfluence | chips: same weekday, per page
- Diff against last week | Rows not present in last week's list, written with the follow week and the page they followed | with: Claude, Google Sheets | chips: new follows, follow week, page
= Dedupe by LinkedIn URL | One row per person; following two pages in the same week is one row with two pages | with: Google Sheets | chips: LinkedIn URL, pages followed

- Pull every page weekly, same day, same actor, same settings. Apify first; Teamfluence when the user already runs it.
- Keep only URLs absent from last week's list, with the follow week and the page. The follow week is the signal date for core tiering, so it is never blank.
- Dedupe by LinkedIn URL. Two pages followed in one week is one row with both pages. Unfollows are not a signal; drop them.

### 3. Remove the noise
Tools: Claude, Apollo, Attio, HubSpot (option)
Flow:
- Competitor staff and alumni | Anyone who works or has worked at the competitor, its partners or agencies, from the current and past positions on the profile | with: Claude, Apollo | chips: current staff, former staff, partners, agencies
- Recruiters, students, vendors | Titles and headlines that never buy, plus students and job seekers | with: Claude | chips: recruiter, student, vendor, job seeker
= CRM gate | Not a customer, not an open deal, not in any sequence in 90 days | with: Attio, HubSpot | tag: core: exclusions

- Most new followers of a company page are its staff, alumni, partners, job seekers and students. Remove them first, reading past positions as well as the headline.
- Then remove recruiters, vendors, and titles outside the buying functions from input 3. A non-buyer title at an ICP account stays only if step 4 can attach the buyer.
- Apply core: exclusions. Log counts per bucket for step 8.

### 4. Qualify company and persona
Tools: Ocean.io, Apollo, Claude
Flow:
- Company check | Headcount, industry and location, then a yes or no ICP verdict with one reason from the company's own website | with: Ocean.io, Apollo, Claude | chips: headcount, industry, ICP yes or no, one reason
= Persona check | A buying title stays as the lead; below the seat at an ICP account, the buyer is found and the follower becomes the context | with: Apollo, Claude | chips: buying title, below seat, buyer attached

- Company first. Firmographics from Ocean.io or Apollo, then a model reads the website and returns a yes or no ICP verdict with one reason. A no stops here, before any paid lookup.
- Persona second. A buying title at an ICP account is the lead. Below the seat, find the buyer at the same account per core section 4 and attach them; the follower stays as context, never as the recipient.

### 5. Stack the signals
Tools: BuiltWith, TheirStack, Apify, RB2B (option), Claude
Flow:
- Technographics | Competitor detected on the account means a switch angle; no detectable tool means an evaluation angle | with: BuiltWith, TheirStack | chips: competitor detected, no tool, switch, evaluation
- Other dated signals | Engagement on competitor posts, open roles in the buying function, a funding round, website visits where RB2B runs | with: Apify, RB2B, Claude | chips: post engagement, hiring, funding, site visit
= Signal count | Follow plus one more dated signal inside a quarter earns a call; the follow alone never does | with: Claude | tag: core: tiering

- Technographics set the angle. Competitor detected by BuiltWith or TheirStack means a switch angle; no detectable tool in the category means an evaluation angle.
- Add every other dated signal: engagement on competitor posts, open roles in the buying function, a funding round, website visits where the user runs RB2B.
- Apply core: tiering. Two dated signals in a quarter earn a call; the follow is one, a call needs one more.

### 6. Enrich and verify
Tools: LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- Person by LinkedIn URL | Current title, company and work email, waterfall keyed on the profile URL | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo | tag: core: enrichment
= Two-pass verification | Valid to the main campaign, catch-all to its own campaign | with: MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: verification

- Apply core: enrichment with the LinkedIn URL as the lookup, never name plus company; follower headlines lag job changes. Enrich the buyer attached in step 4 the same way.
- Apply core: verification.

### 7. Tier and route
Tools: Claude, Slack, HeyReach, LinkedIn, Phone, EmailBison, Instantly (option), Attio, HubSpot (option)
Flow:
- Tier 1 | Buying title at an ICP account, follow plus one more dated signal; Slack alert, connection with a short note, call in week two | with: Slack, HeyReach, Phone | chips: 2 signals, alert, connection, call week 2 | tag: high touch
- Tier 2 | Buying title at an ICP account, follow alone; connection plus a two-step email on the evaluation or switch angle | with: HeyReach, EmailBison, Instantly | chips: 1 signal, connection, 2 emails | tag: send
- Tier 3 | Acceptable fit; always-on two-email campaign on the same angle | with: EmailBison, Instantly | chips: acceptable fit | tag: email only
= Store on the record | Competitor page, follow week, angle and tier written to the CRM | with: Attio, HubSpot | chips: page, follow week, angle, tier

- Tier 1: buying title at an ICP account, follow plus one more dated signal. Slack alert to the owner, a connection request with a short note from a real profile, a call in week two if unanswered.
- Tier 2: buying title at an ICP account, follow alone. Connection request plus a two-step email on the angle from step 5. No call.
- Tier 3: acceptable fit. An always-on two-email campaign per angle.
- Apply core: copy and send rules. Open on the evaluation or the switch in the words buyers use on calls. Never mention the follow. Variables: `{COMPANY}`, `{TITLE}`, `{ANGLE}`.
- Write competitor page, follow week, angle, tier and ICP reason to the CRM record. Stop on a reply, a booking or an open deal.

### 8. Report
Tools: Attio, Slack
Flow:
= Weekly by competitor page | New followers, kept after noise removal, ICP rate, tier counts, replies, meetings; which pages feed pipeline and which feed staff and students | with: Attio, Slack | chips: by page, ICP rate, tier counts, meetings

- Weekly, per page: new followers, kept after step 3, ICP rate, tier counts, replies, meetings.
- After a month the table shows which pages feed real pipeline and which only feed staff and students. Swap the second kind for a product page or another competitor.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- A follow is never the only reason for a call. A call needs the follow plus one more dated signal inside the quarter.
- Never mention the follow. No "saw you started following", no "noticed you follow".
- Competitor staff and alumni are never contacted, nor the competitor's partners or agencies. Check past positions too.
- One person at most once per 60 days across all social plays.
- A person who also reacted to or commented on a competitor post belongs to Competitor Post Engagers. The stronger signal wins; the follow is stored for stacking.
- Scope: this play is new follows of competitor company and product pages. Reactions and comments on competitor voices' posts belong to Competitor Post Engagers; engagers on your own, your team's or chosen creators' posts belong to Content Engagement; third-party posts about your brand, competitors or the category belong to Brand and Keyword Mentions.
- A follow older than 90 days is kept for stacking and never drives copy or a tier.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-competitor-page-followers for our contract management platform, mid-market legal and procurement teams in the US and UK, buyers General Counsel and Head of Procurement, competitors the three we see in every deal"

1. Asked for the three competitors and both offers (evaluation: a side-by-side on their own contracts; switch: migration of the existing repository done for them). Confirmed Apify, Attio, EmailBison, HeyReach, Slack and RB2B.
2. Page set: 3 company pages plus 1 product page. Baseline: 41,300 followers, stored with the date.
3. Week two pull: 612 new follows, 598 after dedupe, 9 following two pages.
4. Noise removal: 187 staff, alumni, partners and agencies; 94 recruiters, students and job seekers; 71 non-buyer titles; 12 out at the CRM gate. 234 through.
5. 141 at ICP accounts: 58 in the buying seat, 47 below it with the buyer attached; 36 acceptable fit.
6. Signals: 39 accounts on a competitor, 102 with no tool; 23 people with a second dated signal (14 post engagements, 6 open roles, 3 RB2B visits).
7. 128 emails found by LinkedIn URL, 121 valid, 7 catch-all in their own campaign.
8. 21 Tier 1, 84 Tier 2, 36 Tier 3. 105 connection requests, 44 accepted; 21 Slack alerts, 9 calls in week two. 121 addresses across the evaluation and switch campaigns.
9. First weekly read: 11 replies, 5 interested, 3 meetings, 2 from the product page, which had the highest ICP rate. One company page fed 70 percent staff and students and goes under review.

Play 28 · First-party signals

Website Visitors

Trigger An identified visit weighted by depth, or an opt-in
Outcome Best-fit visitors reach a rep in minutes
Channels email, linkedin, call
