Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

The best-fit people already on your site should hear from a human while the visit is still fresh, and most teams let them leave with nothing but a cookie. Page views are a poor ranking signal, so this play ranks visitors on who they are: how big their own market is, how strong their offer is, how credible their website looks. An opt-in with a work email is worth more than any anonymous visit, so the loop is built on opt-ins first with identification as the second feed.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of core.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1 and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it.
  2. The site map with intent weights: pages that mean buying (pricing, services, case studies, plays) and pages that mean reading (blog).
  3. Stack answers from core section 1, plus the newsletter tool, the form tool and any visitor identification tool.
  4. The lead magnets on offer and the unique offers a Tier 1 can receive: a lead list, a play sample, a teardown.

Steps

1Instrument the site

Toolsn8nClaude (option)RB2B (option)Warmly (option)
  • Put a UTM on every page and every CTA, so a visit and a booking both carry the page that produced them.
  • Weight pages by intent with the user: pricing, services, case studies and plays high, blog low. Store the weights in the config.
  • Point every opt-in form at the orchestration (n8n or Claude Code), not straight at the newsletter tool. A plain signup may use a personal email; claiming a lead magnet requires a work email.
  • Visitor identification (RB2B or Warmly) is the option, not the spine. Run it only where lawful.

2Capture two feeds into one record

Toolsn8nBeehiivGoogle SheetsRB2B (option)Warmly (option)
  • Feed one is opt-ins, each carrying the form, the page and the UTM. Feed two is identified visits with company, person where available, pages, frequency and recency.
  • Merge both into one record per person per week, by email first and then by domain. When a company shows up in both feeds, the opt-in counts and the visit becomes context.

3Enrich the company and the person

ToolsOcean.ioApolloAI Ark (option)FirecrawlClaude
  • Enrich the company from the email domain: real employee count, industry, location. Self-reported headcount is unreliable and never overrides the domain lookup.
  • Scrape the website with Firecrawl and have Claude score three fields, each 1 to 5 with one reason: TAM size (how many companies they could sell to), offer strength (clear what they sell, to whom, why it wins) and website credibility (proof, named customers, a real team).
  • Store the scores and reasons on the record; they drive step 5 and the opener in step 7.

4CRM check and exclusions

ToolsAttioHubSpot (option)Claude
  • Look up every record in the CRM by email, then by domain. A customer or an open deal gets a visit note on the existing record and a message to the owner, nothing more.
  • Apply core: exclusions. Also drop competitors, agencies, students and job seekers.

5Score and tier

ToolsClaudeAttio
  • Tier 1: ICP fit, a strong three-field score (12 of 15 or higher, no field under 3) and either an opt-in or two high-intent visits inside 7 days. Tier 2: ICP fit plus an opt-in. Tier 3: ICP fit plus a visit only, which goes to the newsletter and nothing else.
  • A company-level identification with no named person is parked until step 6 finds the buyer, and gets outreach only when the pages were high intent.
  • Write tier, feed, pages, the three scores and the source page to the CRM record. Apply core: tiering for anything not covered here.

6Enrich and verify the buyer

ToolsApolloLeadMagicProspeoMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • When the visitor is unknown or sits below the seat, find the buyer by company size. Apply core: enrichment.
  • An opt-in email is still verified before anything sends. Apply core: verification.

7Alert and run outreach by tier

ToolsSlackLinkedInHeyReachPhoneClaudeEmailBisonInstantly (option)BeehiivAttio
  • Tier 1 runs within minutes. Slack alert with the pages viewed, the three scores with reasons, a one-line opener and the direct dial if found. The owner sends a LinkedIn connection the same day and an email with a unique offer tied to what they looked at: a lead list for their ICP, a sample of the play they read, or a teardown of their site. Add them to a Tier 1 segment in the CRM, push that segment to a LinkedIn ad retargeting audience as the option, and send one personalized email a month until they book or are disqualified.
  • Tier 2 gets the welcome flow for their segment plus a two-step email from the owner's own inbox, written to what they claimed. Apply core: copy and send rules. Tier 3 gets the newsletter only.
  • Copy names the page or the magnet the way a colleague would, never the visit. Draft the Tier 1 offer email and the Tier 2 pair with Claude; show them to the user before anything sends.
  • Stop on a reply, a booking, an open deal or a disqualification.

8Report

ToolsAttioSlackGoogle Sheets (option)
  • Weekly: identified visits, opt-ins, tier counts, median minutes to first touch for Tier 1, meetings by source page and by lead magnet.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • Never say you saw them on the site. Name the page, the play or the magnet the way a peer would.
  • An opt-in always outranks a visit. Someone who gave a work email on purpose sits above anyone a tool guessed.
  • Never gate content to force an opt-in. Forced opt-ins produce dead subscribers; the pages stay open and the magnets earn the email.
  • Identification only where lawful, company-level where person-level is not permitted, neither where company-level is not.
  • Only Tier 1 is urgent. Nothing else needs same-day action, and Tier 3 never gets outreach at all.
  • One person at most once per 60 days across first-party plays. A visitor who is also a newsletter reader is one person, not two.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-website-visitors for our outbound agency, B2B companies 11 to 500 people, pricing and plays pages weighted high, three lead magnets live, RB2B installed for US traffic"

  1. Confirmed UTMs on 41 pages and 63 CTAs, intent weights in the config, forms posting to n8n, RB2B on for US traffic.
  2. Week one: 1,840 identified visits across 612 companies and 214 opt-ins (131 magnet claims, 71 work-email signups, 12 booking partials). Merged to 703 records.
  3. 703 companies enriched from the domain; 388 websites scraped and scored, 97 at 12 of 15 or higher.
  4. 19 customers and open deals routed to owners as visit notes; 84 removed as competitors, agencies, students, job seekers or personal-only emails.
  5. 31 Tier 1 (22 opt-ins, 9 with two high-intent visits in 7 days), 118 Tier 2, 319 Tier 3 to the newsletter, 132 company-only parked.
  6. 26 buyers found at company-only accounts with high-intent pages; 157 addresses verified valid, 9 catch-all in their own campaign.
  7. 31 Slack alerts, median 6 minutes to first touch, 31 connections and offer emails, 4 calls the same day. 118 Tier 2 in the welcome flow plus the two-step email.
  8. Week one read: 7 meetings, 5 from the pricing page, 2 from the lead list magnet.

# Website Visitors

The best-fit people already on your site should hear from a human while the visit is still fresh, and most teams let them leave with nothing but a cookie. Page views are a poor ranking signal, so this play ranks visitors on who they are: how big their own market is, how strong their offer is, how credible their website looks. An opt-in with a work email is worth more than any anonymous visit, so the loop is built on opt-ins first with identification as the second feed.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of core.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1 and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it.
2. The site map with intent weights: pages that mean buying (pricing, services, case studies, plays) and pages that mean reading (blog).
3. Stack answers from core section 1, plus the newsletter tool, the form tool and any visitor identification tool.
4. The lead magnets on offer and the unique offers a Tier 1 can receive: a lead list, a play sample, a teardown.

## Steps

### 1. Instrument the site
Tools: n8n, Claude (option), RB2B (option), Warmly (option)
Flow:
- UTM on every page and CTA | Every link and booking button carries its source so a visit and a booking arrive with the page that produced them | chips: source, medium, campaign, page
- Pages weighted by intent | Pricing, services, case studies and plays score high and blog posts score low | with: Claude | chips: pricing, services, case studies, plays, blog
= Forms and identification | Opt-in forms post to the orchestration with the work email; visitor identification runs only where it is lawful in the visitor's region | with: n8n, RB2B, Warmly | tag: option

- Put a UTM on every page and every CTA, so a visit and a booking both carry the page that produced them.
- Weight pages by intent with the user: pricing, services, case studies and plays high, blog low. Store the weights in the config.
- Point every opt-in form at the orchestration (n8n or Claude Code), not straight at the newsletter tool. A plain signup may use a personal email; claiming a lead magnet requires a work email.
- Visitor identification (RB2B or Warmly) is the option, not the spine. Run it only where lawful.

### 2. Capture two feeds into one record
Tools: n8n, Beehiiv, Google Sheets, RB2B (option), Warmly (option)
Flow:
- Opt-ins | Lead magnet claims, newsletter signups with a work email and booking-form partials, each with the page and the UTM | with: n8n, Beehiiv | chips: magnet claim, newsletter signup, booking partial | tag: outranks a visit
- Identified visits | Company, person where available, pages viewed, frequency and recency | with: RB2B, Warmly | chips: company, person, pages, frequency, recency
= One record per person per week | Both feeds merge by email and then by domain; an opt-in outranks a visit from the same company | with: n8n, Google Sheets | chips: person, company, best event, pages, 7 days

- Feed one is opt-ins, each carrying the form, the page and the UTM. Feed two is identified visits with company, person where available, pages, frequency and recency.
- Merge both into one record per person per week, by email first and then by domain. When a company shows up in both feeds, the opt-in counts and the visit becomes context.

### 3. Enrich the company and the person
Tools: Ocean.io, Apollo, AI Ark (option), Firecrawl, Claude
Flow:
- Real company data | Employee count, industry and location from the email domain, never from a self-reported headcount field | with: Ocean.io, Apollo, AI Ark | chips: employee count, industry, location
- Scrape their website | Home, product, pricing and customer pages pulled as text for the model to read | with: Firecrawl | chips: home, product, pricing, customers
= Three-field score | TAM size, offer strength and website credibility, each 1 to 5 with one reason | with: Claude | chips: TAM size, offer strength, website credibility

- Enrich the company from the email domain: real employee count, industry, location. Self-reported headcount is unreliable and never overrides the domain lookup.
- Scrape the website with Firecrawl and have Claude score three fields, each 1 to 5 with one reason: TAM size (how many companies they could sell to), offer strength (clear what they sell, to whom, why it wins) and website credibility (proof, named customers, a real team).
- Store the scores and reasons on the record; they drive step 5 and the opener in step 7.

### 4. CRM check and exclusions
Tools: Attio, HubSpot (option), Claude
Flow:
- Known relationships | Customers and open deals go to their owner as a visit note and never into a sequence | with: Attio, HubSpot | chips: customer, open deal, owner, visit note
= Drop what never qualifies | Competitors, agencies, students and job seekers, plus everything on the core exclusion list | with: Claude | tag: core: exclusions

- Look up every record in the CRM by email, then by domain. A customer or an open deal gets a visit note on the existing record and a message to the owner, nothing more.
- Apply core: exclusions. Also drop competitors, agencies, students and job seekers.

### 5. Score and tier
Tools: Claude, Attio
Flow:
- Tier 1 | ICP fit plus a strong three-field score plus an opt-in or two high-intent visits in 7 days | with: Claude | chips: fit, 12 of 15 or more, opt-in or 2 visits | tag: urgent
- Tier 2 | ICP fit plus an opt-in | chips: fit, opt-in
- Tier 3 | ICP fit plus a visit only; newsletter and nothing else | chips: fit, visit only, newsletter
= Company only | An identification with no named person waits until the buyer is found and gets outreach only when the pages were high intent | with: Attio | chips: no person, buyer found, high intent

- Tier 1: ICP fit, a strong three-field score (12 of 15 or higher, no field under 3) and either an opt-in or two high-intent visits inside 7 days. Tier 2: ICP fit plus an opt-in. Tier 3: ICP fit plus a visit only, which goes to the newsletter and nothing else.
- A company-level identification with no named person is parked until step 6 finds the buyer, and gets outreach only when the pages were high intent.
- Write tier, feed, pages, the three scores and the source page to the CRM record. Apply core: tiering for anything not covered here.

### 6. Enrich and verify the buyer
Tools: Apollo, LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- Find the buyer at the account | When the visitor is unknown or sits below the seat, pick the decision maker by company size | with: Apollo | tag: core: enrichment
- Email waterfall and two-pass verification | Each vendor sees only what the one before it missed; valid to the main campaign, catch-all to its own | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: verification

- When the visitor is unknown or sits below the seat, find the buyer by company size. Apply core: enrichment.
- An opt-in email is still verified before anything sends. Apply core: verification.

### 7. Alert and run outreach by tier
Tools: Slack, LinkedIn, HeyReach, Phone, Claude, EmailBison, Instantly (option), Beehiiv, Attio
Flow:
- Tier 1 within minutes | Slack alert with the pages and the three scores, a LinkedIn connection the same day, an email with a unique offer tied to what they read | with: Slack, LinkedIn, HeyReach, Phone, Claude | chips: pages, 3 scores, lead list, play sample, teardown | tag: within minutes
- Tier 1 segment | A Tier 1 segment in the CRM, a LinkedIn ad retargeting audience as the option, one personalized email a month until they book or are disqualified | with: Attio, LinkedIn, EmailBison, Instantly | chips: T1 segment, retargeting, monthly email
= Tier 2 and 3 | Tier 2 gets the welcome flow for their segment and a two-step email from the owner's own inbox; Tier 3 gets the newsletter only | with: Beehiiv, EmailBison, Instantly | chips: welcome flow, 2-step email, newsletter

- Tier 1 runs within minutes. Slack alert with the pages viewed, the three scores with reasons, a one-line opener and the direct dial if found. The owner sends a LinkedIn connection the same day and an email with a unique offer tied to what they looked at: a lead list for their ICP, a sample of the play they read, or a teardown of their site. Add them to a Tier 1 segment in the CRM, push that segment to a LinkedIn ad retargeting audience as the option, and send one personalized email a month until they book or are disqualified.
- Tier 2 gets the welcome flow for their segment plus a two-step email from the owner's own inbox, written to what they claimed. Apply core: copy and send rules. Tier 3 gets the newsletter only.
- Copy names the page or the magnet the way a colleague would, never the visit. Draft the Tier 1 offer email and the Tier 2 pair with Claude; show them to the user before anything sends.
- Stop on a reply, a booking, an open deal or a disqualification.

### 8. Report
Tools: Attio, Slack, Google Sheets (option)
Flow:
= Weekly read by source | Identified visits, opt-ins, tier counts, speed to first touch for Tier 1, meetings by source page and by lead magnet | with: Attio, Slack, Google Sheets

- Weekly: identified visits, opt-ins, tier counts, median minutes to first touch for Tier 1, meetings by source page and by lead magnet.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- Never say you saw them on the site. Name the page, the play or the magnet the way a peer would.
- An opt-in always outranks a visit. Someone who gave a work email on purpose sits above anyone a tool guessed.
- Never gate content to force an opt-in. Forced opt-ins produce dead subscribers; the pages stay open and the magnets earn the email.
- Identification only where lawful, company-level where person-level is not permitted, neither where company-level is not.
- Only Tier 1 is urgent. Nothing else needs same-day action, and Tier 3 never gets outreach at all.
- One person at most once per 60 days across first-party plays. A visitor who is also a newsletter reader is one person, not two.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-website-visitors for our outbound agency, B2B companies 11 to 500 people, pricing and plays pages weighted high, three lead magnets live, RB2B installed for US traffic"

1. Confirmed UTMs on 41 pages and 63 CTAs, intent weights in the config, forms posting to n8n, RB2B on for US traffic.
2. Week one: 1,840 identified visits across 612 companies and 214 opt-ins (131 magnet claims, 71 work-email signups, 12 booking partials). Merged to 703 records.
3. 703 companies enriched from the domain; 388 websites scraped and scored, 97 at 12 of 15 or higher.
4. 19 customers and open deals routed to owners as visit notes; 84 removed as competitors, agencies, students, job seekers or personal-only emails.
5. 31 Tier 1 (22 opt-ins, 9 with two high-intent visits in 7 days), 118 Tier 2, 319 Tier 3 to the newsletter, 132 company-only parked.
6. 26 buyers found at company-only accounts with high-intent pages; 157 addresses verified valid, 9 catch-all in their own campaign.
7. 31 Slack alerts, median 6 minutes to first touch, 31 connections and offer emails, 4 calls the same day. 118 Tier 2 in the welcome flow plus the two-step email.
8. Week one read: 7 meetings, 5 from the pricing page, 2 from the lead list magnet.

Newsletter Readers

Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

A newsletter list is the warmest list a company owns and usually the least worked, because everyone is afraid of burning it. The readers who click twice, reply or just arrived are already a pipeline source; the question is how to reach them without turning the list into a cold campaign. Segment by real company size, give each segment its own offer, and let the author start the conversation instead of a cold inbox.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it.
  2. The newsletter platform, the author whose name is on the issues, and the author's own mailbox and LinkedIn profile.
  3. Stack answers from core section 1, plus the size bands if they exist (default: under 11, 11 to 50, 51 and up).
  4. One offer per size band in one sentence each, and the lead magnets currently live.

Steps

1Segment by real company size

ToolsBeehiivn8nOcean.ioApolloAI Ark (option)
  • Export every subscriber and resolve each work-email domain to an employee count with Ocean.io or Apollo, AI Ark when the list is large. Self-reported headcount is unreliable, so the domain is the source of truth.
  • Personal addresses are allowed for a plain signup and default to the smallest band. Unresolved work domains default there too, flagged for a retry.
  • Write one size field on every subscriber and build three dynamic segments on it: under 11, 11 to 50, 51 and up. Add a fourth above 500 only once the third band is large enough to split.
  • Run the sync as a scheduled script or an n8n flow every 15 minutes, so a new signup is classified within the hour and lands in the right welcome flow.

2One welcome flow and one offer per segment

ToolsBeehiivClaude
  • Write a distinct welcome flow per band with Claude. The smallest band hears the self-serve or community offer, since a done-for-you service will not fit. The middle band hears the core service and is asked for a call. The largest band hears how the offer plugs into an existing sales team, framed as output per rep.
  • Four emails in the first 24 hours, then a daily email for 30 days, each segment on its own track.
  • Only new signups are enrolled. Existing subscribers are classified for targeting but never dropped into a welcome flow they did not sign up for.

3Pull the engaged readers weekly

ToolsBeehiivGoogle Sheets
  • Every week, pull three groups: readers who clicked two or more times in 30 days with the topics clicked, readers who replied to an issue, and readers who signed up in the last 30 days with the lead magnet or source they came from.
  • Opens never count. Open tracking is noise from mail clients and privacy proxies; a click is a choice.
  • One row per reader in a working sheet with segment, group, topics and dates.

4Match each reader to a person and a company

ToolsLeadMagicProspeoLinkedInOcean.ioApolloClaude
  • Work emails already carry a domain. Personal addresses go through the waterfall to find the LinkedIn profile and current employer. A personal address with no company match stays a reader and leaves this list.
  • Enrich the company: headcount, industry, location, and a yes or no ICP verdict from a cheap model reading the website, with one reason. Record the title.

5CRM check

ToolsAttioHubSpot (option)
  • Match every reader against the CRM. Customers and open deals go to their owner with the clicked topics as a note; a customer reading the pricing page is an expansion conversation, not a cold one.
  • Apply core: exclusions. Anyone in an active sequence or recently not interested stays a reader only.

6Score fit and interest

ToolsClaudeMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • Score ICP fit and interest with Claude. Interest is click depth, with pricing, services and case-study clicks at the top and educational clicks below. A reply sits above everything. A new signup inherits the lead magnet as its topic.
  • Any work address resolved in step 4 goes through core: verification before it is used.

7Tier and route the outreach

ToolsSlackLinkedInGmailHeyReachEmailBisonInstantly (option)Attio
  • Tier 1: ICP fit plus a reply or buying-topic clicks. Slack alert with the topics and a one-line opener. The author sends a personal note on LinkedIn or from their own mailbox: thanks for reading, the topic they clicked keeps coming up with teams like theirs, how are they handling it. Never from the newsletter sender address.
  • Tier 2: ICP fit with educational clicks or a lead-magnet signup. HeyReach connection and a two-step email from inboxes separate from the newsletter, on the topics they read. Apply core: copy and send rules.
  • Tier 3: acceptable fit. No outreach; the daily issues continue.
  • Every reader stays subscribed whatever the tier. Write every contacted person to the CRM with play name, segment, tier, topics and date.

8Report weekly by segment and topic

ToolsBeehiivAttioSlack
  • Weekly: segment sizes, readers pulled, click-to-tier rates, Tier 1 notes sent, replies, meetings, pipeline, split by segment and topic. The topic column tells the author which subjects produce buyers.
  • Send the read to whoever writes the issues so next week's content follows the topics that convert.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • Opens never count. Clicks, replies and signups do.
  • Size comes from the domain, never from a self-reported field. A form headcount is a hint at best.
  • No reader is ever unsubscribed, moved or removed from the daily issues because they received outreach. The list is the asset; the outreach is a layer on top.
  • The first touch comes from the author or a human, never from the newsletter sender address.
  • Existing subscribers never get a welcome flow. Classification applies to everyone; enrollment applies to new signups only.
  • One person is contacted at most once per 60 days across all first-party plays. A reader who also hit the website is one signal, not two emails.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-newsletter-readers for our outbound agency, B2B SaaS and agencies 11 to 500 people in the US, a 4,200-subscriber daily newsletter written by the founder"

  1. Asked for the author's mailbox and profile, the three offers and the live lead magnets; confirmed the stack.
  2. 4,200 subscribers: 2,960 work emails resolved to 1,870 domains, 1,240 personal addresses defaulted to the smallest band. Segments: 1,980 under 11, 1,410 at 11 to 50, 810 at 51 and up.
  3. Three welcome flows approved, 34 emails each, enrollment on for new signups only. 61 new signups in week one, all classified within the hour.
  4. Weekly pull: 312 readers clicked twice or more, 19 replied, 118 signed up in 30 days; 449 rows after dedupe.
  5. 214 personal addresses matched to a profile and a work domain, 98 left as readers. 196 of 351 companies ICP fit.
  6. 23 customers and 9 open deals routed to their owners with notes; 14 removed as in sequence or not interested.
  7. 150 scored: 31 Tier 1 (12 replies, 19 pricing or case-study clickers), 72 Tier 2, 47 Tier 3. 106 addresses verified, 4 catch-all in their own campaign.
  8. 31 founder notes sent, 9 replies, 5 calls booked in week one. 72 Tier 2 connections and a two-step email loaded from separate inboxes. Top converting topics: pricing, inbox infrastructure, list building.

# Newsletter Readers

A newsletter list is the warmest list a company owns and usually the least worked, because everyone is afraid of burning it. The readers who click twice, reply or just arrived are already a pipeline source; the question is how to reach them without turning the list into a cold campaign. Segment by real company size, give each segment its own offer, and let the author start the conversation instead of a cold inbox.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve, who owns it.
2. The newsletter platform, the author whose name is on the issues, and the author's own mailbox and LinkedIn profile.
3. Stack answers from core section 1, plus the size bands if they exist (default: under 11, 11 to 50, 51 and up).
4. One offer per size band in one sentence each, and the lead magnets currently live.

## Steps

### 1. Segment by real company size
Tools: Beehiiv, n8n, Ocean.io, Apollo, AI Ark (option)
Flow:
- Resolve every work domain | Each work-email domain becomes an employee count; personal addresses default to the smallest band | with: Ocean.io, Apollo, AI Ark | chips: domain, employee count, personal email
- Write the size field | One size value on every subscriber record, with the enrichment date | with: n8n, Beehiiv | chips: under 11, 11 to 50, 51 and up, unresolved
= Dynamic segments on a schedule | Segments built on the size field; the sync runs on a timer so a new signup is classified within the hour | with: n8n, Beehiiv | tag: hourly

- Export every subscriber and resolve each work-email domain to an employee count with Ocean.io or Apollo, AI Ark when the list is large. Self-reported headcount is unreliable, so the domain is the source of truth.
- Personal addresses are allowed for a plain signup and default to the smallest band. Unresolved work domains default there too, flagged for a retry.
- Write one size field on every subscriber and build three dynamic segments on it: under 11, 11 to 50, 51 and up. Add a fourth above 500 only once the third band is large enough to split.
- Run the sync as a scheduled script or an n8n flow every 15 minutes, so a new signup is classified within the hour and lands in the right welcome flow.

### 2. One welcome flow and one offer per segment
Tools: Beehiiv, Claude
Flow:
- Smallest band | Self-serve or community offer; free resources and the entry product | with: Claude | chips: self-serve, community, free resources
- Middle band | The core service and a call; insight posts and in-depth breakdowns | with: Claude | chips: core service, book a call
- Largest band | The sales-team integration story: more pipeline per rep, not outsourced email | with: Claude | chips: sales-team integration, per-rep output
= Four then daily | Four emails in the first 24 hours, then a daily sequence for 30 days per segment; new signups only | with: Beehiiv | tag: new signups only

- Write a distinct welcome flow per band with Claude. The smallest band hears the self-serve or community offer, since a done-for-you service will not fit. The middle band hears the core service and is asked for a call. The largest band hears how the offer plugs into an existing sales team, framed as output per rep.
- Four emails in the first 24 hours, then a daily email for 30 days, each segment on its own track.
- Only new signups are enrolled. Existing subscribers are classified for targeting but never dropped into a welcome flow they did not sign up for.

### 3. Pull the engaged readers weekly
Tools: Beehiiv, Google Sheets
Flow:
- Repeat clickers | Two or more clicks in 30 days, with the topics clicked | with: Beehiiv | chips: clicks, topics, last click date
- Repliers | Anyone who replied to an issue, with the reply text | with: Beehiiv | chips: reply, issue
- New signups | Signed up in the last 30 days, with the lead magnet or source they came from | with: Beehiiv | chips: signup date, lead magnet, source

- Every week, pull three groups: readers who clicked two or more times in 30 days with the topics clicked, readers who replied to an issue, and readers who signed up in the last 30 days with the lead magnet or source they came from.
- Opens never count. Open tracking is noise from mail clients and privacy proxies; a click is a choice.
- One row per reader in a working sheet with segment, group, topics and dates.

### 4. Match each reader to a person and a company
Tools: LeadMagic, Prospeo, LinkedIn, Ocean.io, Apollo, Claude
Flow:
- Personal email to person | Personal addresses resolved to a LinkedIn profile and a work domain through the waterfall | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo, LinkedIn | tag: core: enrichment
= Company and ICP check | Headcount, industry and location, then a yes or no from the company website with one reason | with: Ocean.io, Apollo, Claude | chips: company, title, ICP yes or no, reason

- Work emails already carry a domain. Personal addresses go through the waterfall to find the LinkedIn profile and current employer. A personal address with no company match stays a reader and leaves this list.
- Enrich the company: headcount, industry, location, and a yes or no ICP verdict from a cheap model reading the website, with one reason. Record the title.

### 5. CRM check
Tools: Attio, HubSpot (option)
Flow:
= Customers and open deals to their owner | Matched against the CRM; existing relationships leave the list with the clicks as a note on the record | with: Attio, HubSpot | tag: core: exclusions

- Match every reader against the CRM. Customers and open deals go to their owner with the clicked topics as a note; a customer reading the pricing page is an expansion conversation, not a cold one.
- Apply core: exclusions. Anyone in an active sequence or recently not interested stays a reader only.

### 6. Score fit and interest
Tools: Claude, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- Fit plus click depth | ICP fit from step 4 combined with how deep the clicks go; pricing, services and case-study clicks rank highest | with: Claude | chips: ICP fit, click depth, pricing, services, case study
- Replies and magnets | A reply outranks every click; a lead-magnet signup carries the magnet as its topic | with: Claude | chips: reply, lead magnet
= Verify resolved addresses | Any work address found in step 4 passes two verifiers before it is used | with: MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: verification

- Score ICP fit and interest with Claude. Interest is click depth, with pricing, services and case-study clicks at the top and educational clicks below. A reply sits above everything. A new signup inherits the lead magnet as its topic.
- Any work address resolved in step 4 goes through core: verification before it is used.

### 7. Tier and route the outreach
Tools: Slack, LinkedIn, Gmail, HeyReach, EmailBison, Instantly (option), Attio
Flow:
- Tier 1 | Slack alert, then a personal note from the author on LinkedIn or from the author's own mailbox, on the topic they clicked | with: Slack, LinkedIn, Gmail | chips: name, company, segment, topic, click count | tag: author first
- Tier 2 | HeyReach connection plus a two-step email from separate inboxes on the topics they read | with: HeyReach, EmailBison, Instantly | tag: at volume
- Tier 3 | Stays in the daily issues; written to the CRM with the score | with: Attio | chips: newsletter reader, tier, topics

- Tier 1: ICP fit plus a reply or buying-topic clicks. Slack alert with the topics and a one-line opener. The author sends a personal note on LinkedIn or from their own mailbox: thanks for reading, the topic they clicked keeps coming up with teams like theirs, how are they handling it. Never from the newsletter sender address.
- Tier 2: ICP fit with educational clicks or a lead-magnet signup. HeyReach connection and a two-step email from inboxes separate from the newsletter, on the topics they read. Apply core: copy and send rules.
- Tier 3: acceptable fit. No outreach; the daily issues continue.
- Every reader stays subscribed whatever the tier. Write every contacted person to the CRM with play name, segment, tier, topics and date.

### 8. Report weekly by segment and topic
Tools: Beehiiv, Attio, Slack
Flow:
= Weekly read by segment and topic | Segment sizes, click-to-tier rates, replies, meetings, and which topics produce buyers, sent to whoever writes the issues | with: Beehiiv, Attio, Slack

- Weekly: segment sizes, readers pulled, click-to-tier rates, Tier 1 notes sent, replies, meetings, pipeline, split by segment and topic. The topic column tells the author which subjects produce buyers.
- Send the read to whoever writes the issues so next week's content follows the topics that convert.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- Opens never count. Clicks, replies and signups do.
- Size comes from the domain, never from a self-reported field. A form headcount is a hint at best.
- No reader is ever unsubscribed, moved or removed from the daily issues because they received outreach. The list is the asset; the outreach is a layer on top.
- The first touch comes from the author or a human, never from the newsletter sender address.
- Existing subscribers never get a welcome flow. Classification applies to everyone; enrollment applies to new signups only.
- One person is contacted at most once per 60 days across all first-party plays. A reader who also hit the website is one signal, not two emails.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-newsletter-readers for our outbound agency, B2B SaaS and agencies 11 to 500 people in the US, a 4,200-subscriber daily newsletter written by the founder"

1. Asked for the author's mailbox and profile, the three offers and the live lead magnets; confirmed the stack.
2. 4,200 subscribers: 2,960 work emails resolved to 1,870 domains, 1,240 personal addresses defaulted to the smallest band. Segments: 1,980 under 11, 1,410 at 11 to 50, 810 at 51 and up.
3. Three welcome flows approved, 34 emails each, enrollment on for new signups only. 61 new signups in week one, all classified within the hour.
4. Weekly pull: 312 readers clicked twice or more, 19 replied, 118 signed up in 30 days; 449 rows after dedupe.
5. 214 personal addresses matched to a profile and a work domain, 98 left as readers. 196 of 351 companies ICP fit.
6. 23 customers and 9 open deals routed to their owners with notes; 14 removed as in sequence or not interested.
7. 150 scored: 31 Tier 1 (12 replies, 19 pricing or case-study clickers), 72 Tier 2, 47 Tier 3. 106 addresses verified, 4 catch-all in their own campaign.
8. 31 founder notes sent, 9 replies, 5 calls booked in week one. 72 Tier 2 connections and a two-step email loaded from separate inboxes. Top converting topics: pricing, inbox infrastructure, list building.

Founder Connections

Trigger A one-time export of the founder's LinkedIn connections, refreshed quarterly
Outcome Warm pipeline ranked by fit, not memory
Channels linkedin, email, call

Give this to your agent 
Install the Pipeline.tech "Founder Connections" outbound play. Fetch
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/core/SKILL.md and
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/plays/founder-connections/SKILL.md,
save them as ~/.claude/skills/pipeline-outbound-core/SKILL.md and
~/.claude/skills/pipeline-founder-connections/SKILL.md, then run /pipeline-founder-connections.
Paste into Claude Code. It installs the shared core rules plus this play, then runs it as /pipeline-founder-connections.
Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

The warmest data a company owns sits in the founder's LinkedIn network, and it almost never gets worked, because the founder works it from memory: the twenty people who come to mind, not the three thousand who accepted a request over ten years. Half of those connections have changed jobs since, and some now hold the budget you sell into. This play exports the whole network, ranks every person by fit, and puts the founder in front of the right ones with an opener that matches how well they actually know each other.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve.
  2. Who exports: the founder plus every exec with 1,000 or more connections, and whether the founder's own followers are included.
  3. Stack answers from core section 1, plus the founder's mailbox and the AE who takes Tier 1 calls.
  4. The offer in one sentence, one thing the founder can show in 15 minutes, and which customers may be named.

Steps

1Export the connections

ToolsLinkedInApify (option)Google Sheets
  • Have the founder go to LinkedIn Settings, Data privacy, Get a copy of your data, and tick Connections only. Request Messages as a second file if recorded interactions should count in the scoring.
  • Repeat for every exec with 1,000 or more connections and tag each file with the exporter; the holder sends in step 7.
  • Option: export the founder's own LinkedIn and X followers with Apify and run them through the same pipeline from step 2. Followers tier one step lower and the opener never claims a relationship.

2Load and clean

ToolsGoogle SheetsClaude
  • Load every file into one sheet, one row per person, with the connected-on date and the holder's name; both drive step 7.
  • Dedupe across exporters on profile URL; a person connected to two execs is one row with both holders, and the stronger relationship sends.
  • Flag rows with no company; LinkedIn leaves it blank for people between roles, and they go to the live-profile check.

3Enrich the companies from the live profile

ToolsLinkedInOcean.ioApolloAI Ark (option)Claude
  • Resolve the current company from the live profile, not the CSV; the company column froze the day the request was accepted.
  • Enrich industry, headcount, location and funding with Ocean.io or Apollo, AI Ark past a few thousand rows.
  • Apply core: lists for the ICP verdict: yes or no with one reason from the company's own website, stored on the row.

4Enrich the contacts and route the movers

ToolsApolloProspeoAttioHubSpot (option)Claude
  • Pull current title and seniority, confirm they are still in role, and set the buying-role flag by the size bands in core: enrichment.
  • Route the movers. Someone now at a non-ICP company is dropped. A former customer who moved belongs to Champion Moves, where the relationship rather than the connection sets the tone.
  • Apply core: exclusions. An open deal goes to its owner with a Slack note that the founder is connected; the founder never cuts across a live deal.

5Enrich and verify

ToolsLeadMagicProspeoMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • Apply core: enrichment for the work email at the current company.
  • Apply core: verification.
  • Phone where the user's rules allow it, Tier 1 only. A connection with no email stays flagged and gets the LinkedIn message only.

6Score and tier

ToolsClaudeAttioGoogle Sheets
  • Tier 1: ICP plus decision maker plus connected within 24 months, or any recorded interaction at any age. Tier 2: ICP plus a buying role. Tier 3: partial fit. Followers tier one step down.
  • Apply core: tiering for what each tier means in touch.
  • The founder reads the Tier 1 list and removes or reassigns rows: they know who is a friend rather than a buyer. Write every row to the CRM with play name, tier, holder, connected-on date and reason.

7Outreach by connected-on date and holder

ToolsClaudeLinkedInPhoneGmailHeyReachEmailBisonInstantly (option)Beehiiv
  • Apply core: copy and send rules, with one change: everything comes from the holder's own profile and mailbox, never a play inbox set. The daily inbox cap still applies.
  • Connected under 12 months: assume they remember and open on what the founder is building now. Older: one line of reintroduction (how and roughly when you connected), then the point.
  • Tier 1: a DM from the founder's profile, then an AE calls within a week and names the connection. Tier 2: a two-step email from the founder's mailbox plus one HeyReach message. Tier 3: a light nurture or a newsletter invitation.
  • The ask is to show something, never a meeting: a 15-minute look at what the founder is building, or a teardown of their setup. Draft one variant per tier and age band and show the user before upload.

8Report and refresh

ToolsAttioSlackLinkedIn
  • Report replies and meetings by tier, by connected-on age band (under 12 months, 12 to 24, over 24) and by holder. The age read shows how far back the network stays warm; the holder read shows whose network to export next.
  • Quarterly: re-export, keep only rows dated after the last run, and push them through steps 2 to 7.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • Always from the founder's own profile and mailbox, never a cold inbox set. Another exec's connection is sent by that exec.
  • Never pretend to remember someone. The connected-on date sets the opener; only history on record is mentioned.
  • Open deals are excluded, and their owners are told about the connection.
  • The quarterly refresh adds new connections only. The full list is never re-run.
  • The founder approves the Tier 1 list before anything is sent.
  • One person at most once per 60 days across first-party plays; a connection who is also a subscriber or a champion hears from the play with the stronger relationship.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-founder-connections for our outbound agency, B2B SaaS 50 to 500 people in North America, founder and CRO exporting, Attio and EmailBison"

  1. Asked for the offer line, what the founder can show and nameable customers; confirmed exports from the founder (4,870 connections) and the CRO (1,920), plus Attio, Gmail, EmailBison, HeyReach and Slack. Followers declined for the first run.
  2. 6,790 rows loaded, 412 duplicates across exporters merged, 6,378 people; 540 with no company flagged.
  3. Live profiles resolved 6,112 current companies; 2,340 people had changed company since connecting, 1,102 to companies outside the ICP. 1,580 at ICP-fit companies.
  4. 697 with a buying role or partial fit; 23 former customers to Champion Moves, 38 excluded (9 deal owners notified), 24 no longer in role. 612 remain.
  5. 551 work emails found, 61 flagged LinkedIn only; 518 valid, 24 catch-all to their own campaign, 9 removed. 77 direct dials for Tier 1 candidates.
  6. 88 Tier 1, 301 Tier 2, 223 Tier 3. Founder removed 11 and reassigned 6 to the CRO; 77 Tier 1 approved.
  7. 77 DMs over two weeks, 41 AE calls connected; 301 Tier 2 loaded into "Founder Connections, Q4" from the two mailboxes; 223 Tier 3 invited to the newsletter.
  8. Week six: 31% reply rate on Tier 1 and 14 meetings; Tier 2 at 9% and 11 meetings; the under-12-months band replied at twice the rate of over-24. Refresh set for February.

# Founder Connections

The warmest data a company owns sits in the founder's LinkedIn network, and it almost never gets worked, because the founder works it from memory: the twenty people who come to mind, not the three thousand who accepted a request over ten years. Half of those connections have changed jobs since, and some now hold the budget you sell into. This play exports the whole network, ranks every person by fit, and puts the founder in front of the right ones with an opener that matches how well they actually know each other.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The ICP in one paragraph: industries, headcount band, geography, the problem you solve.
2. Who exports: the founder plus every exec with 1,000 or more connections, and whether the founder's own followers are included.
3. Stack answers from core section 1, plus the founder's mailbox and the AE who takes Tier 1 calls.
4. The offer in one sentence, one thing the founder can show in 15 minutes, and which customers may be named.

## Steps

### 1. Export the connections
Tools: LinkedIn, Apify (option), Google Sheets
Flow:
- Connections archive per exporter | LinkedIn Settings, Data privacy, Get a copy of your data, Connections only: name, company, position, connected on, profile URL | with: LinkedIn | chips: name, company, position, connected on, profile URL
- Every exec with 1K or more | The founder first, then every exec whose network clears 1K connections, each file tagged with the exporter's name | with: Google Sheets | chips: founder, exec, 1K floor
- Own followers | The founder's LinkedIn and X followers exported the same way and run through the same pipeline from step 2 | with: Apify | tag: option

- Have the founder go to LinkedIn Settings, Data privacy, Get a copy of your data, and tick Connections only. Request Messages as a second file if recorded interactions should count in the scoring.
- Repeat for every exec with 1,000 or more connections and tag each file with the exporter; the holder sends in step 7.
- Option: export the founder's own LinkedIn and X followers with Apify and run them through the same pipeline from step 2. Followers tier one step lower and the opener never claims a relationship.

### 2. Load and clean
Tools: Google Sheets, Claude
Flow:
- One row per person | Connected-on date and connection holder kept on every row, profile URL as the key | with: Google Sheets | chips: connected on, holder, profile URL
- Dedupe across exporters | The same person in two files becomes one row with every holder listed and the earliest connected-on date | with: Claude | chips: holders, earliest date
= Flag empty companies | Rows with no company are flagged for the live-profile check in step 3 rather than dropped | chips: no company, flag

- Load every file into one sheet, one row per person, with the connected-on date and the holder's name; both drive step 7.
- Dedupe across exporters on profile URL; a person connected to two execs is one row with both holders, and the stronger relationship sends.
- Flag rows with no company; LinkedIn leaves it blank for people between roles, and they go to the live-profile check.

### 3. Enrich the companies from the live profile
Tools: LinkedIn, Ocean.io, Apollo, AI Ark (option), Claude
Flow:
- Current company from the profile | The CSV company is years stale on half the rows; the live profile sets the company that gets enriched | with: LinkedIn, Apollo | chips: current company, domain
- Firmographics | Industry, headcount, location and funding on every current company | with: Ocean.io, Apollo, AI Ark | chips: industry, headcount, location, funding
= ICP yes or no | A verdict with one reason from the company's own website, written to the row | with: Claude | chips: ICP yes, ICP no, reason

- Resolve the current company from the live profile, not the CSV; the company column froze the day the request was accepted.
- Enrich industry, headcount, location and funding with Ocean.io or Apollo, AI Ark past a few thousand rows.
- Apply core: lists for the ICP verdict: yes or no with one reason from the company's own website, stored on the row.

### 4. Enrich the contacts and route the movers
Tools: Apollo, Prospeo, Attio, HubSpot (option), Claude
Flow:
- Title, seniority, still in role | Current title and seniority from the live profile, a still-in-role check, a decision-maker or buying-role flag by company size | with: Apollo, Prospeo, Claude | chips: title, seniority, in role, buying role
- Movers | Moved to a non-ICP company: dropped; was a customer and moved: handed to Champion Moves | with: Attio, HubSpot | chips: non-ICP drop, Champion Moves
= Exclusions and open deals | Customers, open deals and active sequences out; an open deal goes to its owner with the connection noted | with: Attio, HubSpot, Slack | tag: core: exclusions

- Pull current title and seniority, confirm they are still in role, and set the buying-role flag by the size bands in core: enrichment.
- Route the movers. Someone now at a non-ICP company is dropped. A former customer who moved belongs to Champion Moves, where the relationship rather than the connection sets the tone.
- Apply core: exclusions. An open deal goes to its owner with a Slack note that the founder is connected; the founder never cuts across a live deal.

### 5. Enrich and verify
Tools: LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- Email waterfall | Work address at the current company; each vendor only sees what the one before it missed | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo | tag: core: enrichment
- Two-pass verification | Valid to the main campaign, catch-all to its own | with: MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: verification
- Phone where allowed | Direct dials only for Tier 1 candidates and only where the user's rules and the recipient's region permit a call | with: Prospeo | chips: Tier 1 only, region rules

- Apply core: enrichment for the work email at the current company.
- Apply core: verification.
- Phone where the user's rules allow it, Tier 1 only. A connection with no email stays flagged and gets the LinkedIn message only.

### 6. Score and tier
Tools: Claude, Attio, Google Sheets
Flow:
- Tier 1 | ICP plus decision maker plus connected within 24 months, or any recorded interaction: a message, an event, a reply | with: Claude | chips: ICP, decision maker, 24 months, interaction | tag: high touch
- Tier 2 | ICP plus a buying role | with: Claude | chips: ICP, buying role
- Tier 3 | Partial fit: an ICP company without a buying role, or a buying role at a near-ICP company | with: Claude | chips: partial fit
= Founder approves Tier 1 | The Tier 1 list goes to the founder as a sheet before anything sends; every row to the CRM with tier, holder and connected-on date | with: Google Sheets, Attio | chips: approved, removed, reassigned

- Tier 1: ICP plus decision maker plus connected within 24 months, or any recorded interaction at any age. Tier 2: ICP plus a buying role. Tier 3: partial fit. Followers tier one step down.
- Apply core: tiering for what each tier means in touch.
- The founder reads the Tier 1 list and removes or reassigns rows: they know who is a friend rather than a buyer. Write every row to the CRM with play name, tier, holder, connected-on date and reason.

### 7. Outreach by connected-on date and holder
Tools: Claude, LinkedIn, Phone, Gmail, HeyReach, EmailBison, Instantly (option), Beehiiv
Flow:
- Opener by connected-on age | Under 12 months assumes they remember and opens direct; older gets a one-line reintroduction first | with: Claude | chips: under 12 months, over 12 months, reintroduce
- Tier 1 | A DM from the founder's own profile, then a call from an AE who names the connection | with: LinkedIn, Phone | tag: high touch
- Tier 2 | Two-step email from the founder's own mailbox plus one HeyReach message from the founder's profile | with: Gmail, EmailBison, Instantly, HeyReach | chips: {HOLDER}, {CONNECTED_YEAR}, {COMPANY}
- Tier 3 | A light nurture or an invitation to the newsletter | with: Beehiiv, EmailBison | tag: nurture

- Apply core: copy and send rules, with one change: everything comes from the holder's own profile and mailbox, never a play inbox set. The daily inbox cap still applies.
- Connected under 12 months: assume they remember and open on what the founder is building now. Older: one line of reintroduction (how and roughly when you connected), then the point.
- Tier 1: a DM from the founder's profile, then an AE calls within a week and names the connection. Tier 2: a two-step email from the founder's mailbox plus one HeyReach message. Tier 3: a light nurture or a newsletter invitation.
- The ask is to show something, never a meeting: a 15-minute look at what the founder is building, or a teardown of their setup. Draft one variant per tier and age band and show the user before upload.

### 8. Report and refresh
Tools: Attio, Slack, LinkedIn
Flow:
- Read by tier, age and holder | Replies and meetings by tier, by connected-on age band and by connection holder | with: Attio, Slack | chips: tier, age band, holder
= Quarterly refresh | Re-export each quarter and add only connections dated after the last run; never a full re-run | with: LinkedIn | chips: new rows only, quarterly

- Report replies and meetings by tier, by connected-on age band (under 12 months, 12 to 24, over 24) and by holder. The age read shows how far back the network stays warm; the holder read shows whose network to export next.
- Quarterly: re-export, keep only rows dated after the last run, and push them through steps 2 to 7.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- Always from the founder's own profile and mailbox, never a cold inbox set. Another exec's connection is sent by that exec.
- Never pretend to remember someone. The connected-on date sets the opener; only history on record is mentioned.
- Open deals are excluded, and their owners are told about the connection.
- The quarterly refresh adds new connections only. The full list is never re-run.
- The founder approves the Tier 1 list before anything is sent.
- One person at most once per 60 days across first-party plays; a connection who is also a subscriber or a champion hears from the play with the stronger relationship.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-founder-connections for our outbound agency, B2B SaaS 50 to 500 people in North America, founder and CRO exporting, Attio and EmailBison"

1. Asked for the offer line, what the founder can show and nameable customers; confirmed exports from the founder (4,870 connections) and the CRO (1,920), plus Attio, Gmail, EmailBison, HeyReach and Slack. Followers declined for the first run.
2. 6,790 rows loaded, 412 duplicates across exporters merged, 6,378 people; 540 with no company flagged.
3. Live profiles resolved 6,112 current companies; 2,340 people had changed company since connecting, 1,102 to companies outside the ICP. 1,580 at ICP-fit companies.
4. 697 with a buying role or partial fit; 23 former customers to Champion Moves, 38 excluded (9 deal owners notified), 24 no longer in role. 612 remain.
5. 551 work emails found, 61 flagged LinkedIn only; 518 valid, 24 catch-all to their own campaign, 9 removed. 77 direct dials for Tier 1 candidates.
6. 88 Tier 1, 301 Tier 2, 223 Tier 3. Founder removed 11 and reassigned 6 to the CRO; 77 Tier 1 approved.
7. 77 DMs over two weeks, 41 AE calls connected; 301 Tier 2 loaded into "Founder Connections, Q4" from the two mailboxes; 223 Tier 3 invited to the newsletter.
8. Week six: 31% reply rate on Tier 1 and 14 meetings; Tier 2 at 9% and 11 meetings; the under-12-months band replied at twice the rate of over-24. Refresh set for February.

Play 31 · First-party signals

Lead-Magnet Campaigns

Trigger Your own lead magnets, one cold campaign each
Outcome Cold email run like ad sets, opt-ins routed by segment
Channels email

Give this to your agent 
Install the Pipeline.tech "Lead-Magnet Campaigns" outbound play. Fetch
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/core/SKILL.md and
https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pipeline-tech/plays/main/plays/lead-magnet-campaigns/SKILL.md,
save them as ~/.claude/skills/pipeline-outbound-core/SKILL.md and
~/.claude/skills/pipeline-lead-magnet-campaigns/SKILL.md, then run /pipeline-lead-magnet-campaigns.
Paste into Claude Code. It installs the shared core rules plus this play, then runs it as /pipeline-lead-magnet-campaigns.
Workflow, generated from the skillOpen full size

Most companies post their lead magnets on social and wait. Cold email puts the same magnet in front of the whole TAM this week, and one campaign per magnet per size band on equal budgets shows in two weeks which magnet converts. Every opt-in arrives with a work email, so it is enriched, segmented and scored before the welcome email lands.

Load pipeline-outbound-core first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill. List and send mechanics are shared with pipeline-full-tam-cold-email through core and are not repeated here.

Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to ./pipeline-plays.config.json:

  1. The candidate lead magnets, with what each delivers and how it is produced today.
  2. The newsletter size bands (from Newsletter Readers), so lists and segments line up.
  3. The TAM: output of pipeline-full-tam-map, or a CSV with domain, employee count and industry.
  4. Newsletter tool, form tool, orchestration (n8n or Claude Code), plus stack answers from core section 1.

Steps

1Pick two or three magnets

ToolsClaude
  • Every magnet is an offer with its own fulfilment automation, so launch with two or three, never six. Good candidates: a free lead list for their ICP, a library of plays as runnable skills, a market map with stack, roadmap and costs.
  • Three questions per candidate. Is it the product in miniature? Claimable with a work email? Deliverable inside 24 hours with nobody in the loop? One no and it is not a magnet.
  • A free call gated behind a qualification floor is a site CTA, not a magnet, and gets no campaign here.
  • Decide the heavy-user upsell now: a domain that claims lists every month is offered the full universe build for less than they can build it themselves.

2Build the form and the plumbing

Toolsn8nClaude (option)Ocean.ioLeadMagicBeehiivAttioSupabase
  • The form takes 60 seconds to 3 minutes and posts to the orchestration layer. Work email is required to claim; a personal address joins the newsletter but claims nothing.
  • Enrich the domain for real employee count and industry; self-reported headcount is unreliable.
  • Push the subscriber to the newsletter with the size segment set, then to the CRM. Apply core: CRM and alerts.
  • One UTM per campaign, variant in utm_content, so every opt-in is attributed to magnet, band and variant.
  • Where the magnet is a list, deliver through a portal with credits per company domain per month, so heavy users surface by themselves.

3Build the lists

ToolsOcean.ioApollo (option)AI Ark (option)BlitzAPISupabaseAttioBeehiivEmailBisonLeadMagicProspeoMillionVerifierBounceBan
  • Apply core: lists, for the full TAM, split into the newsletter's size bands so dashboard and welcome flow share one band.
  • One list per magnet and band. The lists are the audiences; a company sits in one at a time.
  • Apply core: exclusions, plus existing subscribers and anyone who has claimed a magnet before.
  • Apply core: enrichment and core: verification.

4Write one campaign per magnet

ToolsClaude
  • Apply core: copy and send rules.
  • The magnet is the entire ask: "want me to send the list for your ICP?" No call or demo anywhere in the sequence.
  • Two variants per magnet. Reply-to-claim: email 1 asks, the reply gets the form link through Reply Routing. Link-to-claim: the threaded nudge carries the link, so email 1 stays link-free per core.
  • Two or three angles per magnet are the creatives, differing in opener and proof, not in synonyms.
  • Approve plain text, then apply the spintax and QA gate from pipeline-full-tam-cold-email step 5.

5Launch like ad sets

ToolsEmailBisonInstantly (option)Google Sheets
  • One inbox set per magnet so deliverability reads per magnet. Apply core: send rules.
  • Every magnet-band-variant pair gets the same daily send budget for two weeks, no favourites before the data is in.
  • The metric is opt-ins per 1K sent. Reply rate misleads because the link variant converts without a reply.
  • At two weeks, kill every pair under the median, move its budget to the pairs above it, rotate a new creative into the freed slot. Repeat fortnightly.

6Fulfil and route the opt-ins

Toolsn8nSupabaseLinkedInBeehiivClaudeAttioSlack
  • Deliver every claim inside 24 hours, automated. A list ships unverified and says so in plain words, with LinkedIn URLs so the claimant can verify cheaply.
  • The welcome flow for their segment starts on opt-in. Newsletter Readers owns the flows; this play sets the segment.
  • Score the signup on TAM size, offer and website. Apply core: tiering. Tier 1s enter the loop Website Visitors owns, with a Slack alert.
  • A domain that claims repeatedly gets the step 1 upsell, sent by a person, not an automation.

7Handle replies and non-claimers

ToolsEmailBisonClaudeSlackAttio
  • Replies go through Reply Routing. Here "interested" means they want the magnet, so the drafted answer carries the form link only.
  • A claimer who never opens the magnet gets one nudge after 3 days, then silence. The newsletter does the rest.
  • Non-claimers stay on the list and are eligible for a different magnet after 30 days, never the one they ignored. Log the magnet seen in the CRM.
  • Not-interested and unsubscribes apply across every magnet, not just the one answered.

8Report weekly like a media dashboard

ToolsGoogle SheetsEmailBisonBeehiivAttioSlack
  • One row per magnet, band and variant: sent, opt-in rate, work-email rate, delivered inside 24 hours, Tier 1 rate, calls booked, revenue, plus inbox and data cost, so cost per opt-in and per call are known per magnet.
  • Read it top down. Low opt-in rate indicts the magnet or creative. Low work-email rate indicts the band. Low Tier 1 rate with healthy opt-ins means the wrong company size; move budget to another band.
  • Post the table to Slack every Monday with the step 5 kill and scale decisions.
  • Apply core: credits and handoff.

Guardrails

  • A magnet must be the product in miniature, never a PDF nobody asked for.
  • Never gate content to force an opt-in. Forced opt-ins produce dead subscribers.
  • Every claim is fulfilled within 24 hours. If that cannot be automated, the magnet is not ready.
  • The same domain never sees two magnets in the same month.
  • A list handed over unverified says so in plain words, every time.
  • The campaigns follow core send rules. Ad-set framing changes the budgeting, not the deliverability rules.
  • No meeting ask anywhere in the sequence or the reply. Calls come from the loop and the newsletter.

Example run

User: "/pipeline-lead-magnet-campaigns with the free lead list and the plays library, bands under 11, 11 to 50 and 50 plus"

  1. Two magnets kept, market map parked. Form, enrichment and newsletter push live, portal credits at 3 list claims per domain per month.
  2. 48,200 companies split into three bands, 41,600 after suppressing customers, open deals and 2,900 existing subscribers. Six lists, 36,400 verified contacts.
  3. Two variants and three angles per magnet, 12 campaigns, QA clean, launched on 24 inboxes with equal daily budgets.
  4. Week 2: 31,000 sent, 0.7% bounce, 196 opt-ins, 6.3 per 1K. Free list in the 11 to 50 band hit 11.2 per 1K on the link variant; plays library under 11 hit 2.1. Three pairs under the median killed, budget moved, one new creative rotated in.
  5. Week 4: 64,000 sent, 445 opt-ins, 88% work email, all delivered inside 24 hours, 41 Tier 1s into the loop, 9 calls booked, 2 domains at the credit cap offered the upsell.
The skill fileplays/lead-magnet-campaigns/SKILL.mdExactly what gets installed. Copy it, save it as ~/.claude/skills/pipeline-lead-magnet-campaigns/SKILL.md next to the core skill, and run /pipeline-lead-magnet-campaigns.

Every opt-in is enriched on its work-email domain, pushed to a segmented newsletter and scored into a Tier 1 loop. # Lead-Magnet Campaigns

Most companies post their lead magnets on social and wait. Cold email puts the same magnet in front of the whole TAM this week, and one campaign per magnet per size band on equal budgets shows in two weeks which magnet converts. Every opt-in arrives with a work email, so it is enriched, segmented and scored before the welcome email lands.

Load `pipeline-outbound-core` first. Where a step says "apply core", follow that section of the core skill. List and send mechanics are shared with `pipeline-full-tam-cold-email` through core and are not repeated here.

## Inputs

Ask for these before step 1, and save them to `./pipeline-plays.config.json`:

1. The candidate lead magnets, with what each delivers and how it is produced today.
2. The newsletter size bands (from Newsletter Readers), so lists and segments line up.
3. The TAM: output of `pipeline-full-tam-map`, or a CSV with domain, employee count and industry.
4. Newsletter tool, form tool, orchestration (n8n or Claude Code), plus stack answers from core section 1.

## Steps

### 1. Pick two or three magnets
Tools: Claude
Flow:
= Two or three magnets, not six | Each one is the product in miniature, claimable with a work email, deliverable inside 24 hours; a gated free call is a separate CTA | with: Claude | chips: free lead list, plays as skills, market map

- Every magnet is an offer with its own fulfilment automation, so launch with two or three, never six. Good candidates: a free lead list for their ICP, a library of plays as runnable skills, a market map with stack, roadmap and costs.
- Three questions per candidate. Is it the product in miniature? Claimable with a work email? Deliverable inside 24 hours with nobody in the loop? One no and it is not a magnet.
- A free call gated behind a qualification floor is a site CTA, not a magnet, and gets no campaign here.
- Decide the heavy-user upsell now: a domain that claims lists every month is offered the full universe build for less than they can build it themselves.

### 2. Build the form and the plumbing
Tools: n8n, Claude (option), Ocean.io, LeadMagic, Beehiiv, Attio, Supabase
Flow:
- Form posts to orchestration | 60 seconds to 3 minutes to fill, work email required to claim | with: n8n, Claude | chips: name, work email, ICP in one line
- Enrich the domain | Real employee count from the domain, never self-reported | with: Ocean.io, LeadMagic | chips: employees, industry, website
- Push with segment and UTM | Subscriber lands in the newsletter with size band, magnet, campaign and variant | with: Beehiiv, Attio | chips: utm_campaign, utm_content
= Portal with credits | Where the magnet is a list, delivery runs through a portal with credits per company domain per month | with: Supabase | tag: heavy users surface

- The form takes 60 seconds to 3 minutes and posts to the orchestration layer. Work email is required to claim; a personal address joins the newsletter but claims nothing.
- Enrich the domain for real employee count and industry; self-reported headcount is unreliable.
- Push the subscriber to the newsletter with the size segment set, then to the CRM. Apply core: CRM and alerts.
- One UTM per campaign, variant in `utm_content`, so every opt-in is attributed to magnet, band and variant.
- Where the magnet is a list, deliver through a portal with credits per company domain per month, so heavy users surface by themselves.

### 3. Build the lists
Tools: Ocean.io, Apollo (option), AI Ark (option), BlitzAPI, Supabase, Attio, Beehiiv, EmailBison, LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan
Flow:
- Full TAM in size bands | Same bands as the newsletter; one list per magnet and band | with: Ocean.io, Apollo, AI Ark, BlitzAPI, Supabase | tag: core: lists
- Suppress | Customers, open deals, existing subscribers, anyone emailed in 90 days | with: Attio, Beehiiv, EmailBison | tag: core: exclusions
- Enrich and verify | Waterfall, then two passes | with: LeadMagic, Prospeo, MillionVerifier, BounceBan | tag: core: verification

- Apply core: lists, for the full TAM, split into the newsletter's size bands so dashboard and welcome flow share one band.
- One list per magnet and band. The lists are the audiences; a company sits in one at a time.
- Apply core: exclusions, plus existing subscribers and anyone who has claimed a magnet before.
- Apply core: enrichment and core: verification.

### 4. Write one campaign per magnet
Tools: Claude
Flow:
= Two steps, the magnet is the whole CTA | Reply-to-claim and link-to-claim as two variants, two or three angles per magnet as the creatives, no meeting ask anywhere | with: Claude | chips: email 1 x3, threaded nudge, reply variant, link variant

- Apply core: copy and send rules.
- The magnet is the entire ask: "want me to send the list for your ICP?" No call or demo anywhere in the sequence.
- Two variants per magnet. Reply-to-claim: email 1 asks, the reply gets the form link through Reply Routing. Link-to-claim: the threaded nudge carries the link, so email 1 stays link-free per core.
- Two or three angles per magnet are the creatives, differing in opener and proof, not in synonyms.
- Approve plain text, then apply the spintax and QA gate from `pipeline-full-tam-cold-email` step 5.

### 5. Launch like ad sets
Tools: EmailBison, Instantly (option), Google Sheets
Flow:
- Inbox set per magnet | Even daily volume, the same send budget for every magnet-band-variant pair for two weeks | with: EmailBison, Instantly | tag: send
- Read opt-ins per 1K sent | Not reply rate; kill pairs under the median, move the budget to the winners, rotate a new creative into the freed slot | with: Google Sheets | chips: opt-ins per 1K, median cut, new creative

- One inbox set per magnet so deliverability reads per magnet. Apply core: send rules.
- Every magnet-band-variant pair gets the same daily send budget for two weeks, no favourites before the data is in.
- The metric is opt-ins per 1K sent. Reply rate misleads because the link variant converts without a reply.
- At two weeks, kill every pair under the median, move its budget to the pairs above it, rotate a new creative into the freed slot. Repeat fortnightly.

### 6. Fulfil and route the opt-ins
Tools: n8n, Supabase, LinkedIn, Beehiiv, Claude, Attio, Slack
Flow:
- Deliver inside 24 hours | A list is handed over unverified and says so, with LinkedIn URLs so the still-there check is cheap | with: n8n, Supabase, LinkedIn | chips: unverified, LinkedIn URL, phone where available
- Welcome flow by segment | Newsletter Readers owns the flow | with: Beehiiv | tag: hand-off
- Score and loop | TAM size, offer and website; Tier 1 enters the Website Visitors loop; repeat claimers get the upsell | with: Claude, Attio, Slack | tag: hand-off

- Deliver every claim inside 24 hours, automated. A list ships unverified and says so in plain words, with LinkedIn URLs so the claimant can verify cheaply.
- The welcome flow for their segment starts on opt-in. Newsletter Readers owns the flows; this play sets the segment.
- Score the signup on TAM size, offer and website. Apply core: tiering. Tier 1s enter the loop Website Visitors owns, with a Slack alert.
- A domain that claims repeatedly gets the step 1 upsell, sent by a person, not an automation.

### 7. Handle replies and non-claimers
Tools: EmailBison, Claude, Slack, Attio
Flow:
- Replies | Every reply goes through Reply Routing; interested means send the form link, not book a call | with: EmailBison, Claude, Slack
- One nudge | Claimers who never open the magnet get one reminder after 3 days | with: EmailBison | chips: one nudge, then stop
- Rotate non-claimers | Eligible for a different magnet after 30 days, never the same one | with: Attio | chips: 30 days, new magnet

- Replies go through Reply Routing. Here "interested" means they want the magnet, so the drafted answer carries the form link only.
- A claimer who never opens the magnet gets one nudge after 3 days, then silence. The newsletter does the rest.
- Non-claimers stay on the list and are eligible for a different magnet after 30 days, never the one they ignored. Log the magnet seen in the CRM.
- Not-interested and unsubscribes apply across every magnet, not just the one answered.

### 8. Report weekly like a media dashboard
Tools: Google Sheets, EmailBison, Beehiiv, Attio, Slack
Flow:
= Per magnet, band and variant | Sent, opt-in rate, work-email rate, delivered, Tier 1 rate, calls booked, revenue | with: Google Sheets, EmailBison, Beehiiv, Attio | chips: cost per opt-in, cost per call

- One row per magnet, band and variant: sent, opt-in rate, work-email rate, delivered inside 24 hours, Tier 1 rate, calls booked, revenue, plus inbox and data cost, so cost per opt-in and per call are known per magnet.
- Read it top down. Low opt-in rate indicts the magnet or creative. Low work-email rate indicts the band. Low Tier 1 rate with healthy opt-ins means the wrong company size; move budget to another band.
- Post the table to Slack every Monday with the step 5 kill and scale decisions.
- Apply core: credits and handoff.

## Guardrails

- A magnet must be the product in miniature, never a PDF nobody asked for.
- Never gate content to force an opt-in. Forced opt-ins produce dead subscribers.
- Every claim is fulfilled within 24 hours. If that cannot be automated, the magnet is not ready.
- The same domain never sees two magnets in the same month.
- A list handed over unverified says so in plain words, every time.
- The campaigns follow core send rules. Ad-set framing changes the budgeting, not the deliverability rules.
- No meeting ask anywhere in the sequence or the reply. Calls come from the loop and the newsletter.

## Example run

User: "/pipeline-lead-magnet-campaigns with the free lead list and the plays library, bands under 11, 11 to 50 and 50 plus"

1. Two magnets kept, market map parked. Form, enrichment and newsletter push live, portal credits at 3 list claims per domain per month.
2. 48,200 companies split into three bands, 41,600 after suppressing customers, open deals and 2,900 existing subscribers. Six lists, 36,400 verified contacts.
3. Two variants and three angles per magnet, 12 campaigns, QA clean, launched on 24 inboxes with equal daily budgets.
